In `install.sh` the legacy and removal lists are NUL-delimited (`install.sh:1155-1163`, `install.sh:1173`) and `safe_rel` is re-checked immediately before each `rm` in `sync_finish` (`install.sh:1200-1210`; `install.ps1:1183-1189`). `install.sh` applies tombstones to the Claude `skills/` prefix and to the Codex skills root (`skillp` `""`), not the Codex config root (`"-"`, `install.sh:1146`, `install.sh:1753-1754`); `install.ps1` only to `skills/<t>` (`install.ps1:1126`).

Uninstall removes each tracked, gate-passing file that is unmodified (manifest sha **or** a known earlier-release copy), pack files included whatever the `packs` record says, keeps modified ones, prunes emptied dirs (never through a symlink, `install.sh:1223-1236`, `install.ps1:379-394`), then deletes the manifest; with no manifest it removes nothing (`uninstall_root`, `install.sh:1244-1297`, called `install.sh:1674`, `install.sh:1681-1682` with the root's skills prefix for `load_known`; `install.ps1:801-828`; there is no uninstall counterpart of `pack_adopt`). `uninstall_root` re-checks each `$w/plan.tsv` row the same way as `sync_place` (`IFS=$'\t' read -r rel osha csha extra`; the row is used only when `extra` is empty and `shas_ok "$osha" "$csha"` passes, else `warn` `skip malformed plan row <rel, printf %q> — not removed` and nothing is removed for it, `install.sh:1271-1275`). When the doctrine mode is not `off`, the `CLAUDE.md` / `AGENTS.md` block is handled first, after hook unregistration (`install.sh:1673`, `install.sh:1680`; `install.ps1:799`): removed in mode `write` (unless the pre-write re-check fails, which switches the mode to `handsoff`, `install.sh:988`, `install.ps1:758`; see `ConsumerDoctrineBlock`), left alone with a warning in mode `handsoff`. In mode `write`, `agentic-os/doctrine.md` is then removed by `uninstall_root` as an ordinary tracked file (kept if modified). In mode `handsoff` (Claude root only: `"$id" == claude` in `install.sh`; ps1 has one root), an existing `agentic-os/doctrine.md` is **kept**, before the unmodified check, with plain `warn` `keep agentic-os/doctrine.md — the CLAUDE.md block AgenticOS could not remove may still import it; delete it after removing that block` (not counted in `N_KEPT`; `install.sh:1280-1282`, `install.ps1:811-815`). The manifest is still deleted, so the kept file is no longer tracked.

---

### ArmIsolationRecord

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `persistence` |
| **Ingestion route** | Append-only JSONL at `<out>/isolation.jsonl`, written by two harnesses. (1) `run-arms.sh` (`run-arms.sh:55`), where `<out>` is `--out DIR` or the default `eval/metrics/runs/<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ UTC>-arms` (`run-arms.sh:23`, `run-arms.sh:35`, `run-arms.sh:53-54`). Called by `preflight_arm` (`run-arms.sh:90-99`, call at `run-arms.sh:94`) after `iso_preflight`, for `on` then `off`, after both arms are set up and before either arm sends a prompt (`run-arms.sh:109-114`); on failure the run exits 1 straight after, so a failing `on` arm means no `off` line is written (`run-arms.sh:95-98`). (2) `run-release-arms.sh` (`run-release-arms.sh:369`, `run-release-arms.sh:443`), where `<out>` is `--out DIR` or the default `$HOME/agentic-os-eval-runs/<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ UTC>-release-arms`, made absolute, refused when it has `.` / `..` segments or lies inside the repo (`run-release-arms.sh:181-190`; tested `run-release-arms-test.sh:364-365`): **one record per sample** (arm x scenario x round), written in the set-up loop right after `iso_preflight on <root> <arm src> <arm packs>` (`run-release-arms.sh:442-443`), in interleaved order (round, then scenario, then arms rotated by the round, `run-release-arms.sh:415-419`). Every sample is set up and preflighted on the host before any prompt is sent, under both `--sandbox docker` and `--sandbox none`. The first failing sample's record is written, its failures are printed, and the run exits 1 (`run-release-arms.sh:444-448`). With `--sandbox docker`, each preflighted host root is later tarred to a scratch file, extracted into a fresh docker volume and chowned to uid 1000 (`docker_start_sample`, `run-release-arms.sh:466-482`). The record describes the host root before that copy, and before the `--stub-claude` test hook adds `<root>/.stub` (`run-release-arms.sh:471`). **Config re-check (changed):** `cfg_baseline` writes one `<sha256> <path>` line for every non-directory under the host root's `home/.claude` (minus `._*`), in `LC_ALL=C` sorted path order, before the model runs (`run-release-arms.sh:506-513`). `cfg_verify` re-hashes exactly those paths, in the container as uid 1000 (docker) or on the host (none), and compares the whole lines, **so file names are now compared as well as content**. A listed file that is gone gives a `MISSING <path>` line and so a mismatch; files Claude Code creates itself are not in the list and are allowed (`run-release-arms.sh:514-531`). Under docker it runs once at sample start, where a mismatch fails the start (`run-release-arms.sh:501-503`). In both modes it runs again **before every prompt after the first**, and a mismatch stops the sample with `SAMPLE_ERROR="installed config changed between prompts"` (`run-release-arms.sh:644`). It does not run after the last prompt. A start failure is recorded as an incomplete row with no prompts run; two consecutive start failures stop the run after that row and `summary.json`, with exit 1 (`run-release-arms.sh:628-636`, `run-release-arms.sh:699`, `run-release-arms.sh:711`). The check's result is not written to this record. The `.jsonl` is written straight by `iso_record`, not through `redact.mjs`. In both harnesses the line is written by `iso_record` (`arm-isolation.sh:298-321`, 7 args: `<jsonl> <arm> <session_id> <run_id> <repo_sha> <auth_var> <harness_dirty>`): values are passed to `node -e` through environment variables, never a shell-built JSON string (`arm-isolation.sh:299-300`, `arm-isolation.sh:305-307`), and one `JSON.stringify(...) + "\n"` line is appended with `>>` (`arm-isolation.sh:308-320`). The record is written whether preflight passes or fails. Read back by `eval/metrics/run-arms-isolation-test.sh` (`run-arms-isolation-test.sh:92-93` expects `on:pass:true,off:pass:true`, i.e. a 64-char listing hash; `run-arms-isolation-test.sh:98-100` expects `harness_dirty` to be a boolean on both lines equal to `iso_harness_dirty "$REPO"`; `run-arms-isolation-test.sh:129` expects a `"preflight":"fail"` line after a planted ancestor `CLAUDE.md`) and by `eval/metrics/run-release-arms-test.sh` (`run-release-arms-test.sh:264-265` expects one `pass` record per sample). Cited by reports as proof of isolation (`eval/README.md:99-104`; release arms: `eval/metrics/README.md` "Release arms"). |
| **Source** | `eval/metrics/lib/arm-isolation.sh` (lines 21-44, 63-86, 97-172, 181-321); `eval/metrics/run-arms.sh` (lines 46-55, 69-116); `eval/metrics/run-release-arms.sh` (lines 165-196, 205-223, 232-260, 369, 392-394, 413-452, 464-546, 617-667, 690, 699, 711); prose `eval/README.md` ("Arm isolation", lines 42-109), `eval/metrics/README.md` ("Release arms") |

**Shape** (one line per arm for `run-arms.sh`, one per sample for `run-release-arms.sh`; field order as written by `arm-isolation.sh:311-319`):

```json
{"run_id":"<uuid>","repo_sha":"a330c7b","harness_dirty":false,"arm":"on","session_id":"<uuid>","recorded_utc":"2026-10-19T12:00:00.000Z","config_dir_listing_sha256":"<64 hex>","preflight":"pass","failures":[],"auth_var":"ANTHROPIC_API_KEY","env_passthrough":["PATH","LANG","LC_ALL","TERM","USER","LOGNAME","SHELL","ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"]}
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `run_id` | string (UUID) | yes | One per harness run, shared by every line of that run; `crypto.randomUUID()` (`run-arms.sh:49-50`; `run-release-arms.sh:192-193`). In `run-release-arms.sh` it is also the `run_id` of every `ReleaseArmSampleRow` of the run (`run-release-arms.sh:690`). Every docker volume and network of the run, and every container except the `--smoke` probe client (`--rm`, unlabelled, `run-release-arms.sh:602`), carries the label `aos-run=<run_id>`, which the exit cleanup uses to remove them (cleanup `run-release-arms.sh:205-223`; networks `run-release-arms.sh:393-394`, `run-release-arms.sh:596`; volume `run-release-arms.sh:470`; containers `run-release-arms.sh:478`, `run-release-arms.sh:483`, `run-release-arms.sh:498`, `run-release-arms.sh:553`, `run-release-arms.sh:566`, `run-release-arms.sh:597`; tested `run-release-arms-docker-test.sh:125-126`). Its first 8 characters name the run's docker objects (`run-release-arms.sh:194`) |
| `repo_sha` | string | yes | **Differs by writer.** `run-arms.sh`: `git rev-parse --short HEAD` of the repo (abbreviated, not full SHA), or literal `"unknown"` when git fails (`run-arms.sh:51`); both arms (ON install, ON manifest, every task dir) are built from a `git archive HEAD` export via `iso_export_src` (now `iso_export_ref <repo> HEAD <dir>`), never the working tree, so this is exactly what ran (`run-arms.sh:69-76`; `arm-isolation.sh:97-108`); a dirty tree only prints a stderr note (`run-arms.sh:71-73`). `run-release-arms.sh`: the **full 40-hex** commit sha of the arm's ref, `git rev-parse --verify --quiet "<ref>^{commit}"`; an unresolvable ref aborts the run before any record (`run-release-arms.sh:242`, passed at `run-release-arms.sh:443`). That arm's installer, library and preflight expectations all come from `iso_export_ref <repo> <sha>` (`run-release-arms.sh:253`; `arm-isolation.sh:104-108`). Join to `ReleaseArmSampleRow` on `session_id` |
| `harness_dirty` | boolean | yes | `true` when `git status --porcelain -- eval/metrics` is non-empty (uncommitted or untracked changes under `eval/metrics/`), else `false`; also `false` if git fails, since stderr is discarded and output is empty (`iso_harness_dirty`, `arm-isolation.sh:292-296`). Computed once per run (`run-arms.sh:52`; `run-release-arms.sh:196`) and passed as `iso_record`'s 7th arg (`run-arms.sh:94`; `run-release-arms.sh:443`; `arm-isolation.sh:298`, `arm-isolation.sh:302`, `arm-isolation.sh:305`); written as `e.HARNESS_DIRTY === "true"`, so always a JSON boolean (`arm-isolation.sh:312`). Exists because the harness scripts run from the working tree while the arms come from a git export, so `repo_sha` alone does not identify the harness that ran (`arm-isolation.sh:292-293`; `eval/README.md:100-101`). Unit-tested on a scratch repo (`run-arms-isolation-test.sh:216-221`) |
| `arm` | string | yes | `run-arms.sh`: enum `on` \| `off` (`run-arms.sh:113-114`); `iso_preflight` adds the failure `unknown arm: <arm>` for anything else (`arm-isolation.sh:284`). `run-release-arms.sh`: the operator-chosen arm **name** from `NAME=REF[:FLAGS]`, matching `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*$` and unique within the run (`run-release-arms.sh:239-240`, written at `run-release-arms.sh:443`); the preflight itself always runs in `on` mode there (`run-release-arms.sh:442`), so `unknown arm` cannot occur, and there is no OFF arm |
| `session_id` | string (UUID) | yes | `crypto.randomUUID()` per arm (`run-arms.sh:109`) or per sample (`run-release-arms.sh:440`). The same value is passed to `claude -p` (`run-arms.sh:104` `--session-id`; release arms: prompt 1 `--session-id`, later prompts `--resume <same id>` until the first non-zero exit, `run-release-arms.sh:646`, `run-release-arms.sh:662-665`) and used to locate the transcript (`run-arms.sh:124-125`; release arms: `score-sample.mjs --sid`, `run-release-arms.sh:570`, `run-release-arms.sh:675`, lookup `score-sample.mjs:39-51`) |
| `recorded_utc` | string (ISO 8601 UTC) | yes | `new Date().toISOString()` at write time (`arm-isolation.sh:314`) |
| `config_dir_listing_sha256` | string | yes | `ISO_LISTING_SHA` (`arm-isolation.sh:287-288`, `arm-isolation.sh:307`, `arm-isolation.sh:315`): sha256 over the sorted path listing of the arm's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` plus a `<sha256>  <path>` line per regular file (`iso_listing_sha256`, `arm-isolation.sh:63-74`). Normally 64 lowercase hex; the literal `"unavailable"` only when the config dir does not exist (`arm-isolation.sh:287-288`), which is itself a failure (`arm-isolation.sh:227`) |
| `preflight` | enum string | yes | `"pass"` when `failures` is empty, else `"fail"` (`arm-isolation.sh:316`) |
| `failures` | array of string | yes | `ISO_FAILS` from `iso_preflight` (`arm-isolation.sh:218-290`), one human-readable message per failed check, split on newlines with empty entries dropped (`arm-isolation.sh:304`, `arm-isolation.sh:310`). `[]` on pass. Messages may contain absolute temp paths and up to 5 offending entry paths (`iso_join`, `arm-isolation.sh:183`). Possible messages: `config dir missing: ...` (`arm-isolation.sh:227`), `unexpected entries in temp HOME: ...` (`arm-isolation.sh:231`), `project config in task dir: ...` (`arm-isolation.sh:235`), `project config in ancestor: ...` (`arm-isolation.sh:241`), `agentic-os markers in managed policy dir: ...` (`arm-isolation.sh:253`; markers come from the arm's own export, `arm-isolation.sh:174-179`, `arm-isolation.sh:248`), `OFF config dir is not empty: ...` (`arm-isolation.sh:261`). ON preflight is exact: config file set == `iso_on_manifest <src> <enabled packs>` + `iso_on_allowlist <src>` (`arm-isolation.sh:267`, `arm-isolation.sh:271`), both **computed per export from that export's own `install.sh`**; the former fixed `ISO_ON_ALLOWLIST` / `ISO_SHIPPED_COMMANDS` constants are removed. `iso_on_allowlist` (`arm-isolation.sh:141-148`) is always `settings.json`, plus `.agentic-os-manifest.json` only when the export's `install.sh` contains that literal (installers with the #272 lifecycle manifest; v3.0.1 has none, per the comment at `arm-isolation.sh:142-143`). `iso_on_manifest` (`arm-isolation.sh:150-172`) lists every `skills/…`, `agents/…`, `hooks/…` file of the export minus the items of every `assets/packs/<pack>.txt` whose `<pack>` is **not** in the space-separated enabled list (`InstallPackList`; a `skills/<n>/…` path is matched on `skills/<n>`, comment and blank lines skipped; an export without `assets/packs` ships everything; `arm-isolation.sh:157-170`), plus `iso_shipped_commands` (`arm-isolation.sh:128-139`, used at `arm-isolation.sh:166`, `arm-isolation.sh:171`): `commands/<file>` for each word of the export installer's first `install_files "commands" …` line. That matches `iso_install_library <src> <root> <log> [flag…]`, which runs the export's `install.sh --force <flags>` (`arm-isolation.sh:119-126`): `run-arms.sh` passes no flags and no packs (core-only default install); `run-release-arms.sh` accepts only `--with-<pack>` flags, checks each pack exists in the ref (`assets/packs/<pack>.txt`), passes the flags to the installer and the pack names to the preflight (`run-release-arms.sh:243-257`, `run-release-arms.sh:428-431`, `run-release-arms.sh:442`). When no `install_files "commands"` line is found: `cannot compute the install manifest of <src> (installer command allowlist not found)` (`arm-isolation.sh:267-270`); the manifest is then treated as empty, so the file-set messages below also fire. ON file-set messages: `ON config has files outside the install manifest: ...` (`arm-isolation.sh:274`; e.g. a file of a pack that was not enabled), `ON config is missing manifest files: ...` (`arm-isolation.sh:276`), `ON manifest: N library file(s) differ from HEAD in <cfg>` (`arm-isolation.sh:281`; compared against the arm's own export, `arm-isolation.sh:279`, despite the word `HEAD`). Then `iso_check_on_settings` (`arm-isolation.sh:282`, defined `arm-isolation.sh:190-216`), which compares `settings.json` against the export's consumer template `assets/consumer/claude-settings.json` with `$HOME/.claude/hooks` rewritten to `<cfg>/hooks`, reduced to `{hooks}` (`arm-isolation.sh:200-203`), and can emit `ON settings.json is missing or not a JSON object` (`arm-isolation.sh:197`; returns early, no further settings messages), `ON settings.json top-level keys are <keys>, expected ["hooks"]` (`arm-isolation.sh:205`), `ON settings.json has hook commands outside <cfg>/hooks: ...` (`arm-isolation.sh:206-211`; a command passes only if it matches `bash <cfg>/hooks/*.sh`), `ON settings.json hook commands differ from what install.sh registers` (`arm-isolation.sh:212-213`; sorted command lists compared, duplicates kept, `iso_hook_commands` `arm-isolation.sh:185-188`), `ON settings.json differs from what install.sh writes` (`arm-isolation.sh:214-215`; whole file `jq -S` compared). Several settings messages can appear together. Finally `unknown arm: <arm>` (`arm-isolation.sh:284`). Exercised by the test (`run-arms-isolation-test.sh:132-199`; settings plants `run-arms-isolation-test.sh:181-198`) |
| `auth_var` | string | yes | The NAME of the auth variable in use, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, first non-empty in that order (`arm-isolation.sh:23`, `arm-isolation.sh:34-44`). The credential value is never an input to `iso_record` and is never written (`arm-isolation.sh:21-22`, `arm-isolation.sh:298-300`); both harnesses exit 1 before any record if neither is set (`run-arms.sh:46`; `run-release-arms.sh --sandbox none`: `run-release-arms.sh:170`). Under `run-release-arms.sh --sandbox docker` (the default) it is always `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`: that variable is required and OAuth tokens are refused, because the proxy injects the key (`run-release-arms.sh:174-175`) |
| `env_passthrough` | array of string | yes | `ISO_ENV_PASSTHROUGH` split on spaces (`PATH LANG LC_ALL TERM USER LOGNAME SHELL`, `arm-isolation.sh:27`) with `auth_var` appended (`arm-isolation.sh:318`). Variable names only, no values. Matches the allowlist `iso_exec` keeps for the child (`arm-isolation.sh:328`). `PATH` is kept by name but its value is replaced by `iso_child_path` (system default `/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin` plus the dirs of `claude`/`node`/`jq`, `arm-isolation.sh:28`, `arm-isolation.sh:78-86`), not passed through (`arm-isolation.sh:24-26`, `arm-isolation.sh:329`, `arm-isolation.sh:333`). **Describes `iso_exec` only**, i.e. `run-arms.sh` and `run-release-arms.sh --sandbox none` (`run-release-arms.sh:652`). Under `--sandbox docker` the prompt env is a separate explicit `env -i` list set by `docker_prompt` (`PATH`, `LANG`, `HOME`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `TMPDIR`, the four `XDG_*`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://aos-api-proxy:8080`, a placeholder `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_MODEL=<--model>`, **`ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_SONNET_MODEL=<--model>`, `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL=<--small-model>`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL=<--model>` (new)**, `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC=1`, `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER=1`; `run-release-arms.sh:535-546`), which this field does not record |

---

### BlockGeneralPurposeHookIO

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `message` |
| **Ingestion route** | The Claude Code **PreToolUse** hook, registered with matcher `Agent\|Task` and `"onFailure": "block"` in `.claude/settings.json:22-24` (consumer copy: `assets/consumer/claude-settings.json:12-14`). INPUT is the PreToolUse event JSON read from **stdin** (`input=$(< /dev/stdin)`, `block-general-purpose.sh:40`); OUTPUT is printed to **stdout** for the hook runtime. Always exits 0. **Fails closed**: missing `jq` or any jq error (invalid JSON, non-object payload, missing/non-string `tool_name`, non-string `subagent_type`) emits a deny (`block-general-purpose.sh:42-44`, `block-general-purpose.sh:46-48`, `block-general-purpose.sh:65-67`). No bypass switch (`block-general-purpose.sh:25-27`). |
| **Source** | `.claude/hooks/block-general-purpose.sh` (lines 29–68) |

**INPUT** (PreToolUse event, stdin) — only these fields are consumed:

```json
{ "tool_name": "Agent", "tool_input": { "subagent_type": "engineer" } }
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tool_name` | string (non-empty) | yes | Not a string or `""` → jq `error` → fail-closed deny. Anything other than `"Agent"` / `"Task"` → `skip` (allow) — `block-general-purpose.sh:50-51` |
| `tool_input.subagent_type` | string | no | Absent → `""`. Lowercased with `[^a-z0-9]` stripped; `""` or `generalpurpose` → deny, otherwise allow. A non-string value makes `ascii_downcase` error → fail-closed deny — `block-general-purpose.sh:53-54` |

**OUTPUT** (stdout) — exactly one of:

Allow (named specialist, or not an Agent/Task call): no output, exit 0 (`block-general-purpose.sh:59`).

Deny (`block-general-purpose.sh:36`, `block-general-purpose.sh:61-62`):

```json
{ "hookSpecificOutput": { "hookEventName": "PreToolUse", "permissionDecision": "deny", "permissionDecisionReason": "<text>" } }
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `hookSpecificOutput` | object | yes (on deny) | Container for the three fields below |
| `hookSpecificOutput.hookEventName` | string | yes | Literal `"PreToolUse"` — `block-general-purpose.sh:36`, `block-general-purpose.sh:62` |
| `hookSpecificOutput.permissionDecision` | string | yes | Literal `"deny"` — `block-general-purpose.sh:36`, `block-general-purpose.sh:62` |
| `hookSpecificOutput.permissionDecisionReason` | string | yes | Prefixed `block-general-purpose: `. Gives the cause (general-purpose/untyped, `jq` missing, or unparseable payload) followed by the specialist list `$SPECIALISTS` — `block-general-purpose.sh:31`, `block-general-purpose.sh:43`, `block-general-purpose.sh:61`, `block-general-purpose.sh:66` |

---

### ConsumerDoctrineBlock

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `persistence` |
| **Ingestion route** | A marked block the installer owns inside a consumer-owned instruction file (`DOC_TARGET`, `install.sh:1636-1642`): Claude `<DEST>/CLAUDE.md` (`install.sh:1637`, `CLAUDE_MD` `install.sh:671`); Codex user scope `$CODEX_CONFIG_DIR/AGENTS.md`, i.e. `${CODEX_HOME:-$HOME/.codex}/AGENTS.md` (`install.sh:1641`, `install.sh:178`); Codex `--project` `<project root>/AGENTS.md` (`install.sh:1639`). `install.ps1` is Claude-only: `$Root\CLAUDE.md` (`$DocTarget`, `install.ps1:567`). **Planned** before any write (dry run and uninstall included) by `doctrine_plan` (`install.sh:741-825`, called `install.sh:1656` right after the record is read at `install.sh:1643-1644` and `DOC_PROJECT` is set at `install.sh:1645-1655`) / `Set-DoctrinePlan` (`install.ps1:662-705`, called `install.ps1:782` after `Read-DoctrineRecord` `install.ps1:781`), which sets `DOC_MODE` / `$script:DocMode` to `write`, `handsoff`, `optout`, `off` or (`install.sh` `--codex --project` only) `current` (mode table below), and, in `install.sh --codex --project` only, `DOC_UPGRADE` (`install.sh:679`). The doctrine is considered only when `--with-doctrine` / `-WithDoctrine` is given, the run is an uninstall, or the doctrine root's manifest holds a `doctrine` record (`install.sh:745`, `install.ps1:664`; record format in `AgenticOsInstallManifest`); otherwise the mode stays `off`. **Written** by `doctrine_apply` (`install.sh:872-935`): Claude after the hook merge and before `sync_finish` writes the manifest (`install.sh:1729`); Codex after the hook merge (`install.sh:1763`), before both `sync_finish` calls (`install.sh:1769-1770`). ps1 `Install-DoctrineBlock` (`install.ps1:707-749`, called `install.ps1:1180`). A write that is not followed by the doctrine root's manifest is rolled back (see "Crash rollback" below). **Removed** by `doctrine_remove` (`install.sh:974-1011`, called `install.sh:1673`, `install.sh:1680` only when the mode is not `off`) / `Remove-DoctrineBlock` (`install.ps1:751-779`, called `install.ps1:799`), after hook unregistration and before the manifest files are removed. Its state is persisted as the manifest `doctrine` record (`created`, `block_sha256`). Which blocks count as AgenticOS's is read from the release block and, for Codex, `ConsumerDoctrineBlockShas`. |
| **Source** | `install.sh` (lines 14-22, 76, 100, 114, 145-148, 224-261, 451, 459-460, 472-473, 556-565, 646-1011, 1631-1659, 1666-1682, 1707-1736, 1763-1770); `install.ps1` (lines 14-18, 48, 64-66, 551-784, 798-799, 811-815, 1049-1052, 1065, 1169-1212, 1225); body source `assets/consumer/doctrine.md` (see `ConsumerDoctrineFile`); known Codex block shas `assets/consumer/doctrine-block-shas.txt` (see `ConsumerDoctrineBlockShas`) |

**Shape** — Claude (`CLAUDE.md`, body `@agentic-os/doctrine.md`, `install.sh:721`, `install.ps1:571`), inserted into an LF file that already had content (in a CRLF file every line below, the separator included, ends in `\r\n`):

```
<!-- agentic-os:doctrine:begin -->
@agentic-os/doctrine.md
<!-- agentic-os:doctrine:end -->

<the file's previous bytes, unchanged>
```

Codex (`AGENTS.md`) has the same frame with the body replaced by the verbatim bytes of `assets/consumer/doctrine.md` (`cat "$DOCTRINE_SRC"`, `install.sh:721`; `DOCTRINE_SRC` `install.sh:1634`), because AGENTS.md has no import syntax (`install.sh:648-650`).

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| begin marker | line | yes | `<!-- agentic-os:doctrine:begin -->` as a whole line, optionally followed by one CR (`DOC_BEGIN`, `install.sh:674`; `$DocBegin`, `install.ps1:565`; must equal the builder's `BEGIN_MARKER`, `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:33`, checked by `validate.sh`, see `ConsumerDoctrineFile`). Matched in `doc_scan` (`install.sh:694-705`: awk strips one trailing `\r` per line, `sub(/\r$/, "", l)`, then compares exactly, counting every match and keeping the first line number) and in `Get-DocLines` / `Test-Marker` (`install.ps1:595-606`: lines split on `\n`, `-ceq $Marker` or `-ceq "$Marker\r"`) |
| body | lines | yes | Claude: the one line `@agentic-os/doctrine.md`, an import of the manifest-tracked `<DEST>/agentic-os/doctrine.md`, resolved relative to `CLAUDE.md` (`install.sh:648-650`, `install.sh:721`; `install.ps1:571`). Codex: the doctrine inlined (`install.sh:721`) |
| end marker | line | yes | `<!-- agentic-os:doctrine:end -->`, same matching (`DOC_END`, `install.sh:675`; `$DocEnd`, `install.ps1:566`; must equal the builder's `END_MARKER`, `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:34`) |
| separator | one empty line | no | Written after the end marker only when the file was non-empty before insertion: `\r\n` in a CRLF file, else `\n` (`install.sh:906-907`; ps1 `$eol`, `install.ps1:730`, `install.ps1:733`). Uninstall takes it out again unless the record says `created: true` (`install.sh:651-652`, `install.sh:993-998`; ps1 `install.ps1:764-767`). In `install.sh --codex --project` uninstall never edits `AGENTS.md` (every block is hands-off there, see the mode table), so the separator stays |
| rest of file | bytes | no | Never changed by the installer (`install.sh:650-651`) |

**Line endings.** The release block is built with LF endings (`doctrine_block`, `install.sh:718-723`, saved as `$WORK/doctrine.lf` `install.sh:750`; ps1 `$DocBlockLf`, `install.ps1:571`). It is written in the target's own line ending: `doc_block_eol` (`install.sh:863-866`) rewrites every line to end in `\r\n` when `DOC_CRLF` is 1, i.e. when the target's **first line** ends in CR (`doc_scan`, `install.sh:700`); ps1 uses `\r\n` when `Get-DocLines` sees more than one line and line 0 ends in `\r` (`install.ps1:605`), via `$DocBlockLf.Replace("\n", $eol)` (`install.ps1:730-731`). A file the installer creates is always LF (`DOC_CRLF=0`, `install.sh:892`; ps1 writes `$DocBlockLf`, `install.ps1:722`).

**Block sha.** The manifest `doctrine.block_sha256` is the sha of the LF block (`DOC_BLOCK_SHA`, `install.sh:751`; ps1 `Get-TextSha $DocBlockLf`, `install.ps1:668`). The current block's sha is LF-normalised too: `doc_cur_sha` (`install.sh:725-729`) cuts lines begin..end (`sed -n "B,Ep"`), strips one trailing CR per line and hashes the result; ps1 `Get-CurBlockSha` (`install.ps1:613`) joins lines begin..end with `\n`, removes only a CR that ends a line (``-replace "`r(?=`n|$)"``), as `install.sh` does, appends `\n` and hashes. So a CRLF conversion is not an edit. ps1 reads and writes the file as ISO-8859-1 (`$Latin1`, `install.ps1:570`; `Read-Latin1` `install.ps1:615`; `Write-BesideBytes` `install.ps1:631-649`; `Get-TextSha` `install.ps1:579-582`), so every byte round-trips and the hash matches `install.sh`'s.

**Shipped-block check.** A block is AgenticOS's only if its LF-normalised sha is a block AgenticOS has shipped; a manifest record is **not** proof, because in project scope the repository ships the manifest (`install.sh:766-767`). `install.sh` `doc_known_block <sha>` (`install.sh:731-739`) succeeds when the sha equals the current `DOC_BLOCK_SHA` (`install.sh:736`), or, on a non-Claude (Codex) run only, when `DOC_KNOWN_FILE` exists and holds the sha as a whole line (`grep -qxF`, `install.sh:737-738`). `DOC_KNOWN_FILE` is `$REPO_ROOT/assets/consumer/doctrine-block-shas.txt` (`install.sh:1635`; format in `ConsumerDoctrineBlockShas`). Claude accepts only the current block, since the Claude body never changes. ps1 is Claude-only and compares `Get-CurBlockSha` with `$script:DocBlockSha` only; it does not read the known-shas file (`install.ps1:679-683`). Used by the plan (`install.sh:768`), by the install-time update / refresh choice (`install.sh:927`; ps1 `install.ps1:741`) and by the uninstall re-check right before writing (`install.sh:986`; ps1 `install.ps1:756`).

**Target state** (computed by `doctrine_plan`, `install.sh:752-773`; `Set-DoctrinePlan`, `install.ps1:669-685`):

- `unsafe` — `doc_unsafe_reason` (`install.sh:707-716`) / `Get-DocUnsafeReason` (`install.ps1:617-624`) returns `is a symlink`, `is not a regular file` (exists but is not one), or `is hard-linked elsewhere` (`find -links +1`; ps1 `LinkType -eq 'HardLink'`);
- `absent` — no file;
- `malformed` — begin and end counts differ, either is above 1, or a single begin is not before the end: `has malformed AgenticOS doctrine markers (<nb> begin, <ne> end)` (`install.sh:759-760`, `install.ps1:674-675`);
- `none` — no markers;
- `block` — one well-formed block that passes the shipped-block check (`install.sh:763-768`, `install.ps1:677-681`);
- `foreign` — one well-formed block that fails the shipped-block check, **with or without** a record: `holds an AgenticOS doctrine block that matches no block AgenticOS has shipped (edited by hand, or not written by AgenticOS)` (`install.sh:768-771`, `install.ps1:681-683`).

**Mode** (`DOC_MODE` / `$script:DocMode`):

| State | Explicit `--with-doctrine` (`install.sh:775-804`; `install.ps1:686-693`) | Flagless install with a record (`install.sh:817-823`; `install.ps1:700-704`) | Uninstall (`install.sh:805-816`; `install.ps1:694-699`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `unsafe` | hard error `Error: <f> <why> — AgenticOS will not rewrite it. Replace it with a regular file, or install without --with-doctrine. Nothing was changed.`, exit 1 (`install.sh:777-779`; ps1 `-WithDoctrine`, `install.ps1:687`) | `handsoff` | `handsoff` with a record, else `off` (`install.sh:809`, `install.ps1:696`) |
| `malformed` | hard error `Error: <f> <why>. Fix or remove the <begin> ... <end> lines by hand. Nothing was changed.`, exit 1 (`install.sh:780-782`, `install.ps1:688`) | `handsoff` | `handsoff` (`install.sh:810`, `install.ps1:696`) |
| `foreign` | with a record: `write` (the block is refreshed after a whole-file `.bak`, see below). No record (always the case in `install.sh --codex --project`): hard error `Error: <f> has an AgenticOS-format doctrine block that is not a block AgenticOS shipped, and no install record<DOC_NOREC_WHY>. Remove the <begin> ... <end> lines by hand, then re-run. Nothing was changed.`, exit 1 (`install.sh:786-789`, message `install.sh:788`; ps1 `install.ps1:691`, same text without `<DOC_NOREC_WHY>`) | `handsoff` | `handsoff` (`install.sh:810`, `install.ps1:696`) |
| `absent` / `none` | `write` | `optout` | `off` |
| `block` | with a record: `write`. No record, `install.sh --codex --project` (`DOC_PROJECT`, `install.sh:791-797`): the flag counts as consent. When the block's LF-normalised sha equals the current `DOC_BLOCK_SHA` (a CRLF copy of the current block included) the mode is `current`; otherwise (an older shipped block, i.e. a `ConsumerDoctrineBlockShas` line) it is `write` with `DOC_UPGRADE=true` (upgrade after a whole-file `.bak`, see below). No record, user scope: hard error `Error: <f> holds a shipped AgenticOS doctrine block, but no install record says AgenticOS wrote it (it may be quoted, e.g. in a code fence). Remove the <begin> ... <end> lines by hand, then re-run. Nothing was changed.`, exit 1 (`install.sh:798-801`; ps1 `install.ps1:692`), since even a shipped block may be text the user quoted, so it is not adopted | `write` | with a record: `write` (`install.sh:814`, `install.ps1:698`). No record: `handsoff` with why `holds an AgenticOS doctrine block, but no install record<DOC_NOREC_WHY> says AgenticOS put it there (it may be quoted text)` (`install.sh:813-815`; ps1 `install.ps1:699`, without `<DOC_NOREC_WHY>`), since a shipped block with no record may be the user quoting it |

`<DOC_NOREC_WHY>` is empty except in `install.sh --codex --project`, where it is ` AgenticOS can trust (in --codex --project the manifest is repository content)` (`install.sh:1654`).

**Project scope (`install.sh --codex --project`).** The record read from `<project>/.codex/.agentic-os-manifest.json` is treated as absent (`DOC_OLD_SHA=""`, `DOC_OLD_CREATED=false`, `install.sh:1647-1655`), because the repository can commit that file. So: a flagless run returns from the plan at once (mode `off`, `install.sh:745`) and never touches `AGENTS.md`; an uninstall puts every existing block (shipped, foreign, malformed, or unsafe target) in `handsoff` or `off`, so it never edits or deletes `AGENTS.md`; `--with-doctrine` acts as a first install: absent / no markers → `write`; exactly the current shipped block → `current`; an older shipped block → `write` with `DOC_UPGRADE=true`; foreign or malformed markers, or an unsafe target → hard error. No `doctrine` record is written there, after any mode, because `write_manifest` requires `DOC_PROJECT != true` (`install.sh:459-460`); a record already in that manifest (e.g. from an older release) is dropped by the rewrite. This replaces the earlier `created: false` record.

A flagless install with no record never reaches the table (`off`, `install.sh:745`); in `install.sh --codex --project` that is every flagless install. Because the plan runs before `refuse_linked_dirs`, the hook preflight and `sync_place` (`install.sh:1656` vs `install.sh:1667`, `install.sh:1714-1718`; `install.ps1:782` vs `install.ps1:794`, `install.ps1:1064-1066`), a hard error changes nothing. On install the plan also runs before `pack_plan` (`install.sh:1692`, `install.sh:1738`; ps1 `Set-PackPlan`, `install.ps1:1029`). A missing doctrine source is refused here too (see `AgenticOsInstallManifest` "Up-front refusals").

- **`write`** — the install / update or uninstall rules below. Claude stages `agentic-os/doctrine.md` and verifies it before the block is written (see `AgenticOsInstallManifest`).
- **`handsoff`** — `⚠ <f> <why> — AgenticOS leaves it untouched. If it holds the AgenticOS doctrine block, delete the lines from '<begin>' to '<end>' by hand.` (`doc_handsoff_warning`, `install.sh:868-870`; `Write-DocHandsOff`, `install.ps1:658-660`). The target is not touched and the run carries on. On install, the record is carried forward (`install.sh:876-881`; `install.ps1:708-712`; never reached in `--codex --project`, see above) and Claude still stages `agentic-os/doctrine.md` (`DOCTRINE=true` for `write|handsoff`, `install.sh:1657-1659`; ps1 `$Doctrine`, `install.ps1:783-784`), so an existing import keeps resolving. On uninstall (`install.sh:979`, `install.ps1:752`) the manifest is removed as usual, but Claude **keeps** `agentic-os/doctrine.md` with a warning, since the block left in `CLAUDE.md` may still import it (`install.sh:1280-1282`, `install.ps1:811-815`; see `AgenticOsInstallManifest`). A `write`-mode uninstall whose pre-write re-check fails switches to `handsoff` at that point and emits this warning (see "Uninstall" below); because `doctrine_remove` / `Remove-DoctrineBlock` run before the manifest files are removed, that doctrine-file keep applies to it too.
- **`optout`** (flagless install only) — `no AgenticOS doctrine block in <f> — treating that as an opt-out: the doctrine is no longer installed or kept current. Re-run with --with-doctrine to add it back.` (`install.sh:885-887`; ps1 `-WithDoctrine`, `install.ps1:714-717`). Nothing is written to the target. `DOC_CREATED` stays empty, so the new manifest drops the record. Claude no longer stages the doctrine file, so the tracked copy is pruned as `no longer shipped` (kept if modified).
- **`current`** (`install.sh --codex --project` with `--with-doctrine` only, block sha equal to the current `DOC_BLOCK_SHA`) — `<f> already holds the current AgenticOS doctrine block; nothing to change (no install record is kept in --codex --project)` (`install.sh:882-884`). Nothing is written to the target and `DOC_CREATED` stays empty, so the new manifest has no record. `DOCTRINE` stays `false` (`install.sh:1658-1659`), and the `AGENTS.override.md` warning (mode `write` only) is not given.
- **`off`** — nothing.

**Install / update** (mode `write`; `doctrine_apply`, `install.sh:891-934`; `Install-DoctrineBlock`, `install.ps1:719-748`):

| Case | Action | Manifest `created` |
|---|---|---|
| target absent | create the file holding only the LF block; `create <f> with the AgenticOS doctrine block` (`install.sh:891-896`, `install.ps1:719-724`) | `true` (no record at all in `install.sh --codex --project`, `install.sh:460`) |
| exists, no block | block (in the file's line ending) at the top, then the separator and the old content when the file was non-empty; `add the AgenticOS doctrine block to the top of <f>` (`install.sh:903-911`, `install.ps1:732-734`) | `false` (a re-add into the user's own file is not a creation; `install.sh:899-902`, `install.ps1:727-729`) |
| block present, byte-identical to the release block in the file's line ending | no write, no message (`install.sh:913-916`, `install.ps1:736-737`) | `true` only if the old record said `true`, else `false` (`install.sh:901-902`, `install.ps1:729`; not recorded in `--codex --project`, `install.sh:460`) |
| block present, differs, `DOC_UPGRADE=true` (`install.sh --codex --project` with `--with-doctrine`, an older shipped block, see the mode table) | whole file backed up first (`doc_backup`, `install.sh:921`), then lines begin..end replaced in place, the lines before and after kept (`install.sh:919`); note (not a warning) `update the AgenticOS doctrine block in <f> to this release's (it held an older shipped block); the whole file was first saved as <bak>`, or in a dry run `update the AgenticOS doctrine block in <f> to this release's (it holds an older shipped block); the whole file would first be saved as <bak>` (`install.sh:920-926`) | none (project scope writes no record, `install.sh:460`) |
| block present, differs, LF-normalised sha passes the shipped-block check (Claude / ps1: the release block, i.e. a line-ending change; Codex: the release block or any sha in `ConsumerDoctrineBlockShas`) | lines begin..end replaced in place, the lines before and after kept (`install.sh:919`, `install.ps1:739-740`); `update the AgenticOS doctrine block in <f>` (`install.sh:927-928`, `install.ps1:741-742`) | as above |
| block present, fails the shipped-block check (state `foreign`; only reachable with `--with-doctrine` / `-WithDoctrine` **and** a record, and never in `install.sh --codex --project`) | whole file backed up first, then the block replaced in place; `⚠ refresh the doctrine block in <f> — it differed from what AgenticOS wrote; the whole file is saved as <bak>` (`install.sh:929-932`, `install.ps1:743-746`) | as above |

The recorded `block_sha256` no longer counts as a match for the update row. Backup name: `<f>.bak.<STAMP>`, or `<f>.bak.<STAMP>.<n>` (n = 1, 2, …) while that name exists or is a link; `STAMP` is `YYYYmmddHHMMSS.<pid>` (`doc_backup`, `install.sh:855-861`, `STAMP` `install.sh:383`; `Backup-DocTarget`, `install.ps1:651-656`, `$Stamp` `install.ps1:197`). `cp -p` / `Copy-Item`, skipped in dry run (`install.sh:859`, `install.ps1:654`). The project-scope upgrade row and the install refresh row are the only callers. Every write goes through `doc_write` and is skipped in dry run; right before it, `doctrine_apply` arms the crash rollback, and a failed `doc_write` exits 1 (`doc_arm_rollback …; doc_write … \|\| exit 1`, `install.sh:894`, `install.sh:934`; ps1 `install.ps1:722`, `install.ps1:748`). After a Codex apply in mode `write`, `install.sh` warns when `AGENTS.override.md` exists beside the target: `⚠ <dir>/AGENTS.override.md exists; Codex reads it instead of AGENTS.md there, so the doctrine block will not load` (`install.sh:1764-1768`). In mode `write`, Claude install ends with `The AgenticOS doctrine is imported from <f>; it is kept current while the block is there, until --uninstall.` (`install.sh:1734-1736`; ps1 `-Uninstall`, `install.ps1:1225`).

**Crash rollback** (install, mode `write`, real run). It covers a stop between the doctrine edit and the manifest that records it (disk full, kill, Ctrl-C). Without the record, a re-run would refuse the block and uninstall would leave it, so the edit is undone rather than recorded first (`install.sh:937-942`; ps1 `install.ps1:1175-1177`).

- **Arm** — `doc_arm_rollback <target> <new content>` (`install.sh:943-950`) runs right before `doc_write` (`install.sh:894`, `install.sh:934`). When the target exists it copies its current bytes (`cp -p`) to `$WORK/doctrine.pre` and sets `DOC_ROLLBACK_PRE` to the sha256 of the target before the edit; otherwise it only sets `DOC_ROLLBACK_PRE=absent` (no marker file is written; `install.sh:944-948`). It then sets `DOC_ROLLBACK` to the sha256 of the content about to be written (`install.sh:949`). Both start as `""` (`install.sh:680-681`).
- **Disarm** — `write_manifest` sets `DOC_ROLLBACK=""` right after it moves the manifest of `DOC_MF_ROOT` into place (`install.sh:473`). For Claude that is `sync_finish` (`install.sh:1730`) after `doctrine_apply` (`install.sh:1729`). For Codex it is the config-root `sync_finish` (`install.sh:1770`), which runs after the skills-root `sync_finish` (`install.sh:1769`), so a failure in the skills root also triggers the rollback. A stop while `write_manifest` is filling its temp file (before the `mv`) therefore still rolls the doctrine edit back, and `cleanup` removes that temp file, because it is held in `INFLIGHT` (`install.sh:451`, cleared `install.sh:472`). The disarm does not depend on whether a `doctrine` line was written (in project scope none is).
- **Fire** — `cleanup` (`install.sh:232-246`), installed as the EXIT trap (`trap cleanup EXIT`, `install.sh:256`) and also run by the signal handler below. Its first statement is `trap '' INT TERM HUP PIPE` (`install.sh:235`, comment `install.sh:233-234`), so a second Ctrl-C, the TERM that `timeout` forwards, or a dead output pipe cannot cut the rollback or the temp cleanup short. It then removes `INFLIGHT` (the temp file beside a target that `place()` / `doc_write` may be filling, or the `.agentic-os-manifest.XXXXXX` temp that `write_manifest` is filling, `install.sh:451`) and clears it (`install.sh:236`). `INFLIGHT` is a global (`install.sh:227`), never a `local`, so the EXIT trap still sees it when errexit ends the run inside `place()` (per the test comment `install-doctrine-test.sh:698-700`, a `local INFLIGHT` in `place()` would hide it from the EXIT trap on bash 5.2+; 5.1 and 3.2 still show it). When `doc_rollback` is defined (an early exit happens before it exists) it runs `doc_rollback || say_err "Warning: could not restore ${DOC_TARGET:-the doctrine target}; delete the lines between the AgenticOS doctrine markers by hand."` (`install.sh:239-241`); the `||` keeps errexit from ending `cleanup` inside the rollback (comment `install.sh:237-238`). There is no subshell and no `set +e`. Then it removes `TMP`, `WORK` and every `PREFLIGHT_DIRS` entry (`install.sh:242-245`). Every message from this path goes through `say_err` (`install.sh:231`): `printf '%s\n' "$*" >&2 2>/dev/null || true`, i.e. to stderr, with a failed write (e.g. `install.sh | tee` with `tee` gone) silently ignored (comment `install.sh:228-230`). `doc_rollback` (`install.sh:955-972`, comment `install.sh:952-954`) returns 0 at once unless `DOC_ROLLBACK`, `DOC_TARGET` and `WORK` are all non-empty (`install.sh:957`). It returns 0 silently when the edit never landed: pre-state `absent` and the target still absent (no file, no link), or the target a regular non-link file whose sha equals `DOC_ROLLBACK_PRE` (`install.sh:958-960`). If the target is a symlink, is not a regular file, is hard-linked (`find -links +1`) or its sha differs from `DOC_ROLLBACK`, it `say_err`s `Warning: the install stopped before recording the doctrine block in <f>, which has changed since (or is now a link); left as it is.`, changes nothing and returns 0 (`install.sh:962-965`). The `-links +1` test only selects that warning: `doc_write` would refuse a hard-linked target anyway (comment `install.sh:961`). Otherwise it restores: `rm -f` the target when the pre-state is `absent`, else `doc_write "$WORK/doctrine.pre"` over it; either returns 1 on failure (`install.sh:966-970`), which gives the `could not restore` warning above. On success it `say_err`s `The install stopped before recording the doctrine block, so <f> was restored to how it was before this run.` (`install.sh:971`). The rollback does not change the installer's exit status.
- **Signals** — `on_sig` (`install.sh:251-255`) is the handler for INT, TERM and HUP (`trap 'on_sig INT' INT`, `… TERM`, `… HUP`, `install.sh:257-259`). There is no PIPE handler: bash already runs the EXIT trap (`cleanup`) before dying of SIGPIPE, and `cleanup` ignores PIPE (comment `install.sh:260-261`). `on_sig` has no `trap ''` line of its own: it runs `cleanup` first (whose first statement ignores INT / TERM / HUP / PIPE, comment `install.sh:252`), then `trap - EXIT "$1"` (EXIT and that signal back to default, so `cleanup` does not run twice) and `kill -s "$1" $$`, re-raising the signal. The installer therefore dies by that signal after cleanup, so a calling script or loop sees death by signal (and a Ctrl-C stops it too), the same as the installer before #271 (comment `install.sh:247-248`). Because the signals are trapped, bash acts on one only once the running foreground command (e.g. `curl`, `cp`) returns; that delay is accepted (comment `install.sh:249-250`). The earlier plain-exit handlers (`exit 130` / `143` / `129`) are gone. SIGKILL cannot be trapped, so a killed installer neither rolls back nor removes its temp files and dirs, and a doctrine edit made before the kill stays on disk without its manifest record.
- Modes `handsoff`, `current` and `optout`, dry runs, and uninstall (`doctrine_remove`) never arm it.
- **ps1** — `$script:DocWritten` (sha of what `Install-DoctrineBlock` wrote) and `$script:DocPre` (the target's text before that write, `$null` when it did not exist), both initially `$null` (`install.ps1:575-576`). Both are set right before `Write-BesideBytes`: on create, `DocPre = $null` and `DocWritten = Get-TextSha $DocBlockLf` (`install.ps1:722`); on add / update / refresh, `DocPre = $text` and `DocWritten = Get-TextSha $new` (`install.ps1:748`). `$manifestLanded = $false` (`install.ps1:1178`), then a `try` runs `Install-DoctrineBlock`, the removals loop, `Remove-EmptyDirs` and the non-dry-run `Write-Manifest`, and sets `$manifestLanded = $true` (`install.ps1:1179-1192`). Its `finally` (`install.ps1:1193-1212`) acts only when `$manifestLanded` is still `$false` and `$script:DocWritten` is set, so it is meant to cover any stop from the doctrine write through the manifest write ("a throw, Ctrl-C", per the comment at `install.ps1:1175-1177`), but not a killed process. Inside an inner `try`: silent when the edit never landed, i.e. `DocPre` is `$null` and the target is still absent and not a reparse point, or the target is a leaf whose Latin-1 text equals `DocPre` (`install.ps1:1196-1199`). Restore when the target is a leaf, not a reparse point, `LinkType` is not `HardLink`, and `Get-TextSha (Read-Latin1 $DocTarget)` equals `DocWritten`: `Remove-Item` when `DocPre` is `$null`, else `Write-BesideBytes $DocTarget $script:DocPre`, then `Write-Host` `The install stopped before recording the doctrine block, so <f> was restored to how it was before this run.` (`install.ps1:1200-1204`). Otherwise `Write-Host` `Warning: the install stopped before recording the doctrine block in <f>, which has changed since (or is now a link); left as it is.` (`install.ps1:1205-1206`). Any error in that block is caught and gives `Warning: could not restore <f>; delete the lines between the AgenticOS doctrine markers by hand.` (`install.ps1:1208-1209`). The original exception then propagates (a `finally`, no `catch` at the outer level).

**Uninstall** (mode `write`; `doctrine_remove`, `install.sh:976-1011`; `Remove-DoctrineBlock`, `install.ps1:751-779`):

- No backup is taken: on uninstall, mode `write` only follows state `block` **with** an install record, i.e. a block that passed the shipped-block check (`install.sh:813-815`, `install.ps1:698`). Edited (foreign) blocks, and shipped blocks with no record, are left alone in mode `handsoff`. In `install.sh --codex --project` there is never a record, so this path is never taken.
- **Re-check before writing.** Hooks are unregistered between the plan and the removal, so the target is scanned and hashed again first (`doc_scan` + `doc_cur_sha` + `doc_known_block`, `install.sh:983-991`; `Get-DocLines` + `Get-CurBlockSha` compared with `$script:DocBlockSha`, `install.ps1:754-761`). If the markers are no longer exactly one begin and one end with the begin before the end, or the block is no longer a shipped block, `DOC_WHY` / `$script:DocWhy` becomes `changed during the uninstall and no longer holds a block AgenticOS shipped`, the hands-off warning is printed (`⚠ <f> changed during the uninstall and no longer holds a block AgenticOS shipped — AgenticOS leaves it untouched. …`), and nothing is written or deleted (`install.sh:986-990`, `install.ps1:756-760`). It also sets `DOC_MODE=handsoff` / `$script:DocMode = 'handsoff'` (`install.sh:988`, `install.ps1:758`), so the Claude `uninstall_root` (`install.sh:1280-1282`) / ps1 uninstall loop (`install.ps1:811-815`), which runs next, keeps `agentic-os/doctrine.md` that the block left in place may still import. This re-check is about content; `doc_recheck` / `Assert-DocUnchanged` (below) still guard the file type at the write itself.
- Lines begin..end are removed (remainder `install.sh:992`, `install.ps1:763`). When the block is at line 1 **and** the record's `created` is not `true`, one leading separator is dropped: `\r\n` if the remainder starts with it, else one `\n`, else nothing (`install.sh:993-998`, `install.ps1:764-767`). Block at line 1 with `created: true`: the remainder is kept as is, since a file the installer created never got a separator (`install.sh:999-1000`, `install.ps1:768`). Block below line 1: the lines before it plus the remainder (`install.sh:1001-1002`, `install.ps1:770`).
- If the result is empty **and** the record says `created: true`, the file is deleted: `remove <f> (created by AgenticOS, now empty)` (`install.sh:1004-1006`, `install.ps1:772-774`). Otherwise the file is rewritten: `remove the AgenticOS doctrine block from <f>` (`install.sh:1007-1009`, `install.ps1:775-777`). A failed `doc_recheck` before the delete, or a failed `doc_write`, exits 1 (`\|\| exit 1`, `install.sh:1006`, `install.sh:1009`). In `install.sh --codex --project` uninstall never reaches this path (no record, so every block is hands-off), so it never edits or deletes `AGENTS.md`.

**Writes:** `install.sh` `doc_write` (`install.sh:838-853`): `mkdir -p` the parent, `doc_recheck`, `mktemp "<dir>/.agentic-os.XXXXXX"` beside the target, `cp -p` the existing file into it so its mode is kept (a new file gets `chmod 644`), overwrite the temp's content, `doc_recheck` again, then `mv -f` over the target, so a link is replaced, never written through. The temp path is held in the global `INFLIGHT` (`install.sh:227`) straight from `mktemp` and cleared after the `mv`, so `cleanup` removes it if a signal or error stops the run while it is being filled. Each step is checked: `mkdir`, the first `doc_recheck` and `mktemp` failing return 1 at once (`mkdir -p "$dir" \|\| return 1`, `doc_recheck "$f" \|\| return 1`, `INFLIGHT="$(mktemp "$dir/.agentic-os.XXXXXX")" \|\| return 1`, `install.sh:843-845`; a failed `mktemp` leaves `INFLIGHT` empty, since the substitution's output is empty); a failure in the copy / write / second re-check / `mv` chain removes the temp file beside the target (`rm -f "$INFLIGHT"; INFLIGHT=""`) and returns 1 (`install.sh:846-852`). Install and uninstall callers add `\|\| exit 1`; the rollback restore uses it too and turns a failure into `return 1` (`install.sh:969`). The created-and-empty delete also runs `doc_recheck` right before `rm -f` (`install.sh:1006`). `doc_recheck` (`install.sh:827-836`) re-runs `doc_unsafe_reason` (symlink, not a regular file, hard link; not content) and, on a hit, prints `Error: <f> <why> (it changed during the install) — not written. Re-run to finish.` and returns 1 (it no longer exits itself). Every write and delete is skipped in dry run (`install.sh:894`, `install.sh:934`, `install.sh:1006`, `install.sh:1009`). ps1 `Write-BesideBytes` (`install.ps1:631-649`): create the parent, `Assert-DocUnchanged`, `CreateNew` temp `.agentic-os.<random>` beside the target, Latin-1 bytes. When the target is a file, it copies the target's ACL to the temp (`Set-Acl` from `Get-Acl`, only when `$PSVersionTable.PSEdition -eq 'Desktop'` or `$IsWindows`, `install.ps1:641-643`) and its attributes minus `ReadOnly` (`install.ps1:644-645`). Then `Assert-DocUnchanged` again (`install.ps1:647`) and `Move-Item -Force`. `Assert-DocUnchanged` (`install.ps1:626-629`) throws the same message from `Get-DocUnsafeReason`; it also runs right before `Remove-Item` in the created-and-empty delete (`install.ps1:774`).

UNVERIFIED: the `install.ps1` doctrine paths (plan, CRLF handling, ACL / attribute copy, `Assert-DocUnchanged`, the uninstall re-check, hands-off doctrine-file keep, and the `try` / `finally` crash rollback) are cited from source only; they were not executed for this catalog (no `pwsh` available locally). The `install.sh` crash rollback and signal path are exercised by `scripts/install-doctrine-test.sh` test 28 (`install-doctrine-test.sh:679-812`, modes listed at `install-doctrine-test.sh:762`): shimmed `mktemp` / `mv` / `cp` / `chmod` fail the manifest write or send SIGTERM in the window between the doctrine edit and its record (also to `timeout`, which forwards a second TERM), during the rollback's restore, during the doctrine write and during `place()`, kill the output reader (a dead pipe, also combined with a failed restore), make `cp` fail inside `place()` so errexit ends the run (mode `cpfail-place`: no temp file may remain, which a `local INFLIGHT` would break on bash 5.2+ (5.1 and 3.2 still show the local to the trap), `install-doctrine-test.sh:698-700`, `install-doctrine-test.sh:749`), and send SIGTERM while `write_manifest` fills its real temp file (mode `termmanifest`, triggered by the `chmod` shim: the doctrine edit is rolled back and no `.agentic-os-manifest.*` is left, `install-doctrine-test.sh:701-702`, `install-doctrine-test.sh:712-713`, `install-doctrine-test.sh:754-760`). Every mode checks that no WORK dir is left and that `find "$h/.claude" -name '.agentic-os*' ! -name '.agentic-os-manifest.json'` finds nothing, so a leftover `.agentic-os.*` or `.agentic-os-manifest.*` temp file anywhere under `.claude` fails the test (`install-doctrine-test.sh:780-785`). Mode `rbterm` (SIGTERM during the restore) also requires the install to exit 1, i.e. the TERM that arrives while `cleanup` runs is ignored and does not replace the install's own failure status (`install-doctrine-test.sh:773-775`). Test 29 (`install-doctrine-test.sh:814-852`) sends SIGINT / SIGTERM to the process group mid-install and requires death by that signal, and the same result as the base installer when one is available; it is skipped without `python3`. UNVERIFIED: this catalog run read those tests but did not run them or see their results.

### ConsumerDoctrineBlockShas

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `persistence` (generated shipped asset) |
| **Ingestion route** | Committed file `assets/consumer/doctrine-block-shas.txt` (`KNOWN`, `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:31`). **Written** (appended) by `scripts/build-consumer-doctrine.sh` in its default (write) mode; **checked** by its `--check` mode, which `scripts/validate.sh` invariant `consumer-doctrine` runs when the builder is present (see `ConsumerDoctrineFile`). Ships in the release tarball under `assets/consumer` (`ARCHIVE_PATHS`, `release.sh:72`). **Read** by `install.sh` as `DOC_KNOWN_FILE` (`install.sh:1635`) in `doc_known_block` (`install.sh:731-739`), on Codex runs only (plan, install update choice and the uninstall re-check), to tell an AgenticOS block written by this or an earlier release from a foreign one (see `ConsumerDoctrineBlock` "Shipped-block check"). In `install.sh --codex --project` with `--with-doctrine`, a block matching one of its lines (but not the current block) is upgraded after a whole-file `.bak` (`DOC_UPGRADE`, see `ConsumerDoctrineBlock`). `install.ps1` does not read it. A missing file is tolerated by `install.sh` (only the current block is then accepted, `install.sh:737`) but fails the builder's `--check`. |
| **Source** | `scripts/build-consumer-doctrine.sh` (lines 27-34, 115-135); `assets/consumer/doctrine-block-shas.txt` (lines 1-2); `install.sh` (lines 731-739, 1635) |

**Shape** (`doctrine-block-shas.txt:1-2`):

```
# Generated by scripts/build-consumer-doctrine.sh — append-only, do not edit.
a37f02b475aa69420b66f8b4ad17949945a27a3c7615502210dde5fcb3a3ab1e
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| header | comment line | yes | Literal `# Generated by scripts/build-consumer-doctrine.sh — append-only, do not edit.`, written only when the builder creates the file (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:130-132`). Never matches a sha lookup |
| sha line | string (64 lowercase hex) | yes (≥ 1) | One per Codex doctrine block ever generated: the sha256 of the begin marker line, the generated doctrine, and the end marker line, LF, each line `\n`-terminated (`block_sha`, `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:116`, markers `BEGIN_MARKER` / `END_MARKER` `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:33-34`). This equals `install.sh`'s LF-normalised Codex block sha (`doctrine_block`, `install.sh:718-723`), so long as the markers are equal (checked by `validate.sh`, see `ConsumerDoctrineFile`) |

**Rules:**

- **Append-only.** Write mode appends the current `block_sha` only when no line already equals it (`grep -qxF … || printf '%s\n' … >>`, `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:133`); nothing ever removes a line (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:27-30`), so an older release's block is still recognised and refreshed as `update` rather than treated as foreign. Write mode refuses a symlinked file (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:128`).
- **`--check`** fails with `build-consumer-doctrine: <KNOWN> lacks the current Codex block sha <sha> — run: bash scripts/build-consumer-doctrine.sh`, exit 1, when the file is missing or no line equals the current sha (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:122-124`), after the whole-file doctrine diff (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:119-121`).
- **Reader** (`install.sh:737-738`): `grep -qxF -- <sha>`, whole-line fixed-string match; skipped when `PLATFORM` is `claude` or the file does not exist.

### ConsumerDoctrineFile

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `persistence` (generated shipped asset) |
| **Ingestion route** | Committed file `assets/consumer/doctrine.md`, generated by `scripts/build-consumer-doctrine.sh` (`TARGET`, `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:26`) from `.claude/rules/` (`RULES_DIR`, `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:25`). Ships in the release tarball under `assets/consumer` (`ARCHIVE_PATHS`, `release.sh:72`); the builder does not (`validate.sh:487-488`). **Read** by `install.sh` as `DOCTRINE_SRC` = `$REPO_ROOT/assets/consumer/${DOCTRINE_REL#*/}`, i.e. `assets/consumer/doctrine.md` (`install.sh:1634`, `DOCTRINE_REL=agentic-os/doctrine.md` `install.sh:670`): Claude stages it as `agentic-os/doctrine.md` while the doctrine mode is `write` or `handsoff` (`install.sh:1658-1659`, `install.sh:1707-1711`), installed and manifest-tracked like any shipped file (see `AgenticOsInstallManifest`); Codex inlines it into the `AGENTS.md` block (`doctrine_block`, `install.sh:721`). `install.ps1` `$DoctrineSrc` (`install.ps1:568`), staged at `install.ps1:1049-1052`. A missing file aborts any non-uninstall run that considers the doctrine (`install.sh:746-749`, `install.ps1:665-667`). Each generated version's Codex block sha is appended to `ConsumerDoctrineBlockShas`. **Checked** by `scripts/validate.sh` invariant `consumer-doctrine` (`check_consumer_doctrine`, `validate.sh:479-522`, called `validate.sh:1278`). |
| **Source** | `scripts/build-consumer-doctrine.sh` (lines 1-135); `assets/consumer/doctrine.md` (lines 1-103); `scripts/validate.sh` (lines 479-522, 1278); `install.sh` (lines 670, 674-675, 721, 746-749, 1634, 1658-1659, 1707-1711); `install.ps1` (lines 568, 665-667, 784, 1049-1052) |

**Shape** (`generate`, `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:90-105`; `doctrine.md:1-9`):

```
<!-- GENERATED by scripts/build-consumer-doctrine.sh from .claude/rules/ in
     the AgenticOS repo — do not edit. Installed by `install.sh --with-doctrine`
     and removed by `install.sh --uninstall`. -->
# AgenticOS doctrine — grounding, verification, review tiers

These rules apply to every session and every agent you dispatch. They exist to
cut two failure modes: claims nobody checked, and gates that block on opinion.

<!-- source: .claude/rules/factual-correctness.md -->
<curated rule text>
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| header | 7 lines | yes | Literal 3-line GENERATED comment, `# AgenticOS doctrine — grounding, verification, review tiers`, blank line, 2-line intro (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:91-99`) |
| source marker | line | yes (≥ 1) | Per rule: a blank line, then `<!-- source: .claude/rules/<name>.md -->` (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:102`). Rules in order `factual-correctness grounding review-tiers verification` (`RULES`, `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:38`); repo-only rules are left out (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:36-37`). Current markers at `doctrine.md:9`, `doctrine.md:20`, `doctrine.md:30`, `doctrine.md:94`; the file is 103 lines |
| rule body | lines | yes | The rule file copied verbatim except for `REWRITES` lines (`curate`, `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:60-88`). The source must exist (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:62`) and end with a newline (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:63`) |

**`REWRITES` table** (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:40-53`): one row per line, `rule <TAB> exact source line <TAB> replacement line`; replacement `@DELETE` drops the line (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:40`, `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:76`). Read into a variable with `IFS= read -r -d '' REWRITES <<'EOF' || true` (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:43`), not a command substitution around a quoted heredoc, because macOS `/bin/bash` 3.2 mis-parses the heredoc's apostrophes inside `$(...)` (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:41-42`); `read -d ''` returns non-zero at end of input, hence `|| true` under `set -e`. Each rule's rows are selected with `awk -F'\t' '$1 == r'` into a temp file (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:64`) and loaded by the curating awk via `ENVIRON["REWRITES_FILE"]` (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:65-70`). Currently 9 rows (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:44-52`): 1 for `grounding`, 8 for `review-tiers`. Among them, the review-tiers verdict-block sentence (`` End with the machine verdict block — `pass | fix-before-merge | hold`, where ``) is replaced by `` Only Tier 0/1 findings may justify a `hold` verdict. `` (both two-space indented; `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:48`, output `doctrine.md:90`) and the three continuation lines that cite `review-verdict.md` and `scripts/parse-verdict.sh` are `@DELETE`d (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:49-51`). Each row must match **exactly one** whole line of its rule (`$0 == from[i]`, `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:74-78`); otherwise the build prints `build-consumer-doctrine: rewrite for <src> matched <n> lines (want 1): <line>` and fails with `the rules changed under a rewrite — update REWRITES in <script>`, exit 1 (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:81-87`, `err` `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:55`). The output must not contain `agentic-os:doctrine:` (the installer's block markers), else the build fails (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:111-113`). Runs under `LC_ALL=C` (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:22`).

**Block markers.** The builder declares `BEGIN_MARKER='<!-- agentic-os:doctrine:begin -->'` and `END_MARKER='<!-- agentic-os:doctrine:end -->'` (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:32-34`), which must equal `install.sh` `DOC_BEGIN` / `DOC_END` (`install.sh:674-675`), and hashes `BEGIN_MARKER` line + generated doctrine + `END_MARKER` line into `block_sha` (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:115-116`; see `ConsumerDoctrineBlockShas`).

**Modes:** default regenerates the file with `cp`, refusing a symlinked target or a symlinked `ConsumerDoctrineBlockShas` file, creates the shas file with its header when absent and appends the current `block_sha` when missing, then prints `build-consumer-doctrine: wrote <TARGET> (block sha <sha>)` (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:126-135`). `--check` runs `diff -u` against the committed file and exits 1 when stale, then exits 1 when the shas file is missing or lacks the current `block_sha` (`build-consumer-doctrine.sh:118-125`).

**`validate.sh` invariant `consumer-doctrine`** (`check_consumer_doctrine`, `validate.sh:479-522`; failures tagged `consumer-doctrine`):

- (a) file missing: fails only when `assets/consumer/` exists and `install.sh` contains `--with-doctrine` (`validate.sh:491-496`);
- (b) every `<!-- source: .claude/rules/<name>.md -->` line must name a `^[a-z0-9-]+\.md$` file that exists under `.claude/rules/` (`validate.sh:497-502`), and there must be at least one (`validate.sh:503`);
- (c) the file must not contain `agentic-os:doctrine:` (`validate.sh:504-506`);
- marker equality, for `m` in `BEGIN:begin` and `END:end` (`validate.sh:507-517`): when `install.sh` exists and contains `--with-doctrine`, it must contain the exact whole line `DOC_BEGIN="<!-- agentic-os:doctrine:begin -->"` / `DOC_END="<!-- agentic-os:doctrine:end -->"` (`grep -qxF`, `validate.sh:510-511`; the lines at `install.sh:674-675`), else `DOC_BEGIN marker is not <!-- agentic-os:doctrine:begin -->` / `DOC_END marker is not <!-- agentic-os:doctrine:end -->` tagged on `install.sh` (`validate.sh:512`); when the builder exists, it must contain the exact whole line `BEGIN_MARKER='<!-- agentic-os:doctrine:begin -->'` / `END_MARKER='<!-- agentic-os:doctrine:end -->'` (`grep -qxF`, `validate.sh:514`; the lines at `build-consumer-doctrine.sh:33-34`), else `BEGIN_MARKER marker is not <!-- agentic-os:doctrine:begin -->` / `END_MARKER marker is not <!-- agentic-os:doctrine:end -->` tagged on the builder (`validate.sh:515`). A whole-line match pins both the variable name and the value, so leading whitespace, a trailing comment or any other variable carrying the string does not pass;
- (d) when `scripts/build-consumer-doctrine.sh` is present, `--check` must pass (`validate.sh:518-520`), which now also covers `ConsumerDoctrineBlockShas`.
