### ConsumerHookRegistration

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `persistence` |
| **Ingestion route** | Consumer-owned config the installer reads and rewrites. **Claude:** `<DEST>/settings.json`, DEST canonicalized with `pwd -P` (`install-hook-settings.sh:268-275`, `install-hook-settings.sh:324-327`); merged on install by `merge_claude_hook_settings` (`install-hook-settings.sh:260-314`, called `install.sh:1727`) and stripped on uninstall by `unregister_claude_hook_settings` (`install-hook-settings.sh:316-354`, called `install.sh:1670-1671` before files are removed at `install.sh:1674`); `install.ps1` `Update-ClaudeHookSettings "merge"` / `"uninstall"` (`install.ps1:458-549`, called `install.ps1:1174`, `install.ps1:797` — uninstall only when `$Root` exists). **Claude hook-path gate:** Claude hook commands are registered unquoted and with the canonical dir (`bash <DEST>/hooks/x.sh`, template `claude-settings.json:7`), so whitespace would split the command and a shell metacharacter would inject one. The single gate is the lib's `_validate_hook_dir` (`install-hook-settings.sh:19-31`): any path not matching `^[/.a-zA-Z0-9._-]+$` gets `Error: the Claude install dir resolves to a path with whitespace or shell metacharacters: <path>` plus the hint `… To move an existing install: run with --uninstall and the same CLAUDE_DIR, then reinstall elsewhere. Nothing was changed.`, exit 1 (`install-hook-settings.sh:26-30`). `install.sh` calls it right after the library API gate, for Claude install, update and dry run only, on the given DEST and on the canonical DEST (nearest existing ancestor's `pwd -P` plus the not-yet-existing tail) (`install.sh:358-372`). `merge_claude_hook_settings` calls it on the given DEST and, after `mkdir -p`, on the canonical DEST (`install-hook-settings.sh:272`, `install-hook-settings.sh:275`). `unregister_claude_hook_settings` does **not** call it: uninstall runs no hook command, only string-compares commands and rewrites JSON, so it works for an install made into such a path earlier (`install-hook-settings.sh:324-327`). `_reject_whitespace_dest` no longer exists. The given CLAUDE_DIR must still pass `install.sh`'s basic charset `^[/.a-zA-Z0-9._\ -]+$` in every mode (a space passes; `install.sh:161-165`; see `AgenticOsInstallManifest` "Up-front refusals"). ps1 refuses a resolved `$Root` outside `^[/\\:.a-zA-Z0-9._-]+$` except with `-Uninstall` (`install.ps1:191-193`). Codex paths may contain spaces (`_validate_install_dest` allows a space, `install-hook-settings.sh:11-17`), because the Codex template quotes its commands (`codex-hooks.json:9`, `codex-hooks.json:21`; `install-hook-settings.sh:84-86`). **Codex:** `$CODEX_CONFIG_DIR/hooks.json` (`install-hook-settings.sh:124`, `install-hook-settings.sh:175`), via `merge_codex_hook_settings` (`install-hook-settings.sh:120-168`, called `install.sh:1761`) / `unregister_codex_hook_settings` (`install-hook-settings.sh:172-198`, called `install.sh:1679`, before `install.sh:1681-1682`). In project scope `install.sh` first refuses a symlinked or escaping `<project>/.codex` (`install.sh:194-214`). **Codex registration flag:** `install.sh` runs `unset CODEX_HOOKS_REGISTERED` at startup (`install.sh:71`), so the caller's environment cannot preset it. `merge_codex_hook_settings` sets the shell variable `CODEX_HOOKS_REGISTERED=true` whenever it registers: the no-`jq` fresh-file copy (`install-hook-settings.sh:136`) and the `jq` merge or fresh write (`install-hook-settings.sh:165-166`). With no `jq` and an existing `hooks.json` it changes nothing, leaves the flag unset and prints `⚠ jq required to merge AgenticOS hooks into existing <file> — hooks NOT registered` to stderr (`install-hook-settings.sh:138-140`). `install.sh` reads the flag for its closing message (`install.sh:1773-1777`): on a dry run or when the flag is `true`, `Done. Restart Codex to load the skills and agents. Review and trust the new hooks with /hooks.`; otherwise `Done. Restart Codex to load the skills and agents. Hooks were NOT registered: jq is needed to merge them into the existing <CODEX_CONFIG_DIR>/hooks.json. Install jq and re-run.` **Library interface:** the lib declares `AGENTIC_OS_HOOK_LIB_API=2` (`install-hook-settings.sh:5-9`); `install.sh` runs `unset AGENTIC_OS_HOOK_LIB_API` before sourcing it (`install.sh:335-338`), then refuses (exit 1, nothing written) a lib whose API is below `REQUIRED_LIB_API=2` or that lacks any of the four merge / unregister functions (`install.sh:345-356`; see `AgenticOsInstallManifest` "Up-front refusals"). The lib has **no `check` mode**: `merge_claude_hook_settings <repo_root> <dest> [retired]` and `merge_codex_hook_settings <repo_root> <dest>` always write (`install-hook-settings.sh:261-262`, `install-hook-settings.sh:120-121`). **Symlink refused:** `_refuse_link` (`install-hook-settings.sh:58-65`) makes a symlinked `settings.json` / `hooks.json` an error `Error: <file> is a symlink — AgenticOS will not rewrite it. … Nothing was changed.` with return 1 and no change, in merge and unregister alike (`install-hook-settings.sh:126`, `install-hook-settings.sh:177`, `install-hook-settings.sh:266`, `install-hook-settings.sh:329`); `preflight_hook_settings` checks the same first and exits 1 with that text (`install.sh:592-595`); ps1 throws the same message for a reparse-point `settings.json` before any mode branch (`install.ps1:478-480`). **Fail-fast preflight:** before any file is copied into the destination — dry run included — `install.sh` runs `preflight_hook_settings claude "$DEST" settings.json` / `preflight_hook_settings codex "$CODEX_CONFIG_DIR" hooks.json` (`install.sh:587-617`, called `install.sh:1716`, `install.sh:1752`, after `doctrine_plan` (`install.sh:1656`), `pack_plan` and `refuse_linked_dirs`, and before `sync_place`). It passes when the file is absent (`install.sh:596`). When the file exists, a scratch dir is made with `mktemp -d "${base%/}/agentic-os-preflight.XXXXXX"`, where `base` is `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}` when that matches `^[/.a-zA-Z0-9._-]+$`, else `/tmp` (`install.sh:599-601`); the Claude preflight merge runs `_validate_hook_dir` on that scratch path, so it must be plain. It is recorded in `PREFLIGHT_DIRS` (`install.sh:602`) and removed by `cleanup` (`install.sh:244-245`), which runs from the EXIT trap and from the `on_sig` INT / TERM / HUP handler (`cleanup` ignores INT / TERM / HUP / PIPE first thing; there is no PIPE handler, since bash runs the EXIT trap before dying of SIGPIPE), even when the doctrine rollback fails (`install.sh:232-246`, traps `install.sh:256-259`); not on SIGKILL. The file is copied (`cp`) into it (`install.sh:603`) and the **real** merge runs against that copy **in a subshell**, so a library `exit` fails the check instead of silently ending the installer (Claude with `RETIRED_HOOKS` only, `install.sh:608`; Codex `install.sh:610`). Stdout is discarded; stderr goes to `<scratch>/err`. On a non-zero return the captured stderr is printed indented by two spaces (`install.sh:613`), then `Error: <dir>/<name> cannot be merged (it must be valid JSON with an object "hooks") — fix it first. Nothing was changed.`, and it exits 1 (`install.sh:612-616`). So a dry run never depends on the library honouring a flag. It also passes when `jq` is missing (the no-`jq` merge returns 0 on an existing file, `install-hook-settings.sh:278-289`, `install-hook-settings.sh:129-142`). `install.ps1` keeps its native `Update-ClaudeHookSettings "check"` (`install.ps1:1066`, after `Set-DoctrinePlan` `install.ps1:782`, `Set-PackPlan` `install.ps1:1029` and `Assert-NoLinkedDirs` `install.ps1:1064-1065`; returns without writing, `install.ps1:482`, `install.ps1:528`; malformed input throws). Not run on uninstall. **Registration gate:** the real merge runs only after `verify_hooks` / the ps1 `$badHooks` block confirms every shipped hook is on disk at the release sha (`install.sh:1719`, `install.sh:1757`; `install.ps1:1157-1167`), and, for Claude in doctrine mode `write`, after `agentic-os/doctrine.md` is confirmed on disk (`install.sh:1720-1723`, `install.ps1:1169-1173`); otherwise nothing is registered (see `AgenticOsInstallManifest`). **Rewrite (sh):** `_tmp_beside` (`install-hook-settings.sh:46-56`) creates `mktemp "<dir>/.<name>.XXXXXX"` beside the target (O_EXCL, unpredictable name) and, when the target exists, `cp -p`s it there first so its mode survives (e.g. a 0600 `settings.json`); the result is rewritten and `mv -f`'d over the target, replacing the directory entry rather than writing through it — Claude merge `install-hook-settings.sh:295-306`, Codex merge `install-hook-settings.sh:147-158`; unregister uses `_unregister_tmp` (`install-hook-settings.sh:67-71`) then `mv` (`install-hook-settings.sh:197`, `install-hook-settings.sh:353`). A missing file is created the same way from the rewritten template (`install-hook-settings.sh:159-164`, `install-hook-settings.sh:307-312`; no-`jq` copy `install-hook-settings.sh:130-135`, `install-hook-settings.sh:279-283`), so a **freshly created** `settings.json` / `hooks.json` is mode **0600** (mktemp's mode), not umask-default. **Rewrite (ps1):** `Write-Beside` (`install.ps1:226-237`: `CreateNew` temp `.agentic-os.<random>` beside the target, UTF-8 without BOM, then `Move-Item -Force`) for both a fresh file (rewritten template verbatim, `install.ps1:481-490`) and a rewrite (`install.ps1:547`); the old file's attributes are not copied. **Merge without `jq`:** template copied only when no file exists (Claude: only when DEST is `~/.claude`), else a warning and no change, return 0 (`install-hook-settings.sh:129-141`, `install-hook-settings.sh:278-288`). **Unregister without `jq` fails closed:** when the config file exists, prints `Error: jq is required to unregister AgenticOS hooks from <file> … Nothing was removed.` and returns 1 (`install-hook-settings.sh:184-187`, `install-hook-settings.sh:336-339`); under `set -e` (`install.sh:52`) that aborts `--uninstall` before `uninstall_root` deletes any file. With no config file (or, Claude, no DEST dir) it returns 0 (`install-hook-settings.sh:178-181`, `install-hook-settings.sh:323`, `install-hook-settings.sh:330-333`). `install.ps1` does not use `jq`. **Dry run** writes nothing in the install dir: `install.sh` skips the real merge (`install.sh:1724-1725`, `install.sh:1758-1759`); the preflight writes only under its `agentic-os-preflight.*` scratch dir in `$TMPDIR` or `/tmp`; unregister writes to a plain `mktemp` file outside the install dir and deletes it (`install-hook-settings.sh:69-71`, `install-hook-settings.sh:197`, `install-hook-settings.sh:353`); ps1 returns before writing (`install.ps1:486`, `install.ps1:540-543`). |
| **Source** | `scripts/lib/install-hook-settings.sh` (lines 1-80, 82-198, 200-354); templates `assets/consumer/claude-settings.json`, `assets/consumer/codex-hooks.json`; `install.ps1` (lines 99-104, 189-193, 226-237, 398-549, 794-797, 1064-1066, 1087, 1157-1174); `install.sh` (lines 52, 63-71, 160-165, 194-214, 224-261, 335-372, 587-644, 1086-1089, 1238-1242, 1628-1682, 1712-1777) |

**Shape** (only the fields the installer reads or writes; all other keys are preserved untouched). Example: the template's `PreToolUse` groups (`claude-settings.json:3-17`) after the merge rewrote `$HOME/.claude/hooks` to `<DEST>/hooks` for DEST `/Users/me/.claude`:

```json
{ "hooks": { "PreToolUse": [
  { "matcher": "Bash", "hooks": [
    { "type": "command", "command": "bash /Users/me/.claude/hooks/block-bad-bash.sh" },
    { "type": "command", "command": "bash /Users/me/.claude/hooks/survey-before-act.sh" }
  ] },
  { "matcher": "Agent|Task", "hooks": [
    { "type": "command", "command": "bash /Users/me/.claude/hooks/block-general-purpose.sh", "onFailure": "block" }
  ] }
] } }
```

No Claude template handler carries `statusMessage`; that key appears only in the Codex template (`codex-hooks.json:10`, `codex-hooks.json:22`), where its `AgenticOS:` prefix marks ownership.

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `hooks` | object | no | Absent → treated as `{}` on merge, left absent on uninstall. Non-object → error, file untouched (`install-hook-settings.sh:246-247`, `install.ps1:499-501`). On uninstall, removed entirely if stripping empties a non-empty object (`install-hook-settings.sh:256`, `install-hook-settings.sh:116`; `install.ps1:529-530`) |
| `hooks.<Event>` | array of Group | no | Events shipped: Claude `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `SessionStart`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `PreCompact`, `Stop`, `SubagentStop` (`claude-settings.json:3-42`); Codex `SessionStart`, `PreCompact` (`codex-hooks.json:3-26`). Claude merge: a non-array value for a shipped event → error (`install-hook-settings.sh:251-252`, `install.ps1:520-522`). An event emptied by stripping is deleted; one that was already `[]` is kept (`install-hook-settings.sh:239-243`; `install.ps1:505-513`) |
| Group `.matcher` | string | no | Copied from the template; never used for ownership |
| Group `.hooks` | array of Handler | yes | A group emptied by stripping is dropped (`install-hook-settings.sh:237`, `install.ps1:436`) |
| Handler `.type` | string | yes | Template literal `"command"` |
| Handler `.command` | string | yes | Template `$HOME/.claude/hooks` / `$HOME/.codex/hooks` rewritten to `<dest>/hooks` (`install-hook-settings.sh:204-213`, `install-hook-settings.sh:87-96`; `install.ps1:408`, `install.ps1:470`). Claude commands are unquoted (`bash $HOME/.claude/hooks/<name>.sh`, `claude-settings.json:7-41`), hence the `_validate_hook_dir` hook-path gate above; Codex commands quote the path (`bash \"$HOME/.codex/hooks/<name>.sh\"`, `codex-hooks.json:9`, `codex-hooks.json:21`). **Claude ownership key:** a handler is AgenticOS-owned only when its whole command, trimmed and with runs of `/` collapsed, equals a template command or `bash $HOME/.claude/hooks/<retired>` — each in the literal, canonical-DEST, and as-given-DEST forms (`install-hook-settings.sh:231-233`, `install-hook-settings.sh:244-245`; `install.ps1:408-427`). Compound commands are never owned |
| Handler `.onFailure` | string | no | Template sets `"block"` on the `Agent\|Task` handler only (`claude-settings.json:14`) |
| Handler `.timeout` | number | no | Template sets `30` on the `post-edit-scan.sh` handler (`claude-settings.json:22`); copied through, never used for ownership |
| Handler `.statusMessage` | string | no | Codex template only; no Claude template handler sets it. **Codex ownership key:** a group is owned when any handler's `statusMessage` starts with `AgenticOS:` (`install-hook-settings.sh:103-104`; `codex-hooks.json:10`, `codex-hooks.json:22`) |

**Merge / uninstall semantics:**

- Claude merge: retired handlers stripped from **every** event; in each shipped event, template handlers stripped and the template groups appended; re-running is byte-identical (`install-hook-settings.sh:223-226`, `install-hook-settings.sh:248-253`; `install.ps1:502-526`).
- Claude uninstall: template + retired handlers stripped from every event (`install-hook-settings.sh:227`, `install-hook-settings.sh:255`; `install.ps1:502`).
- Codex merge / uninstall: owned groups stripped from every event; merge then appends the template groups (`install-hook-settings.sh:98-117`). Codex does not use the retired-hook list (`install.sh:1679`, `install.sh:1752`, `install.sh:1761`).
- Retired names (Claude `settings.json` only), the union of:
  - `RETIRED_HOOKS` (space-separated) / `$RetiredHooks` (array), both currently empty (`install.sh:67`, `install.ps1:99`);
  - **install:** basenames derived from old-manifest `hooks/<name>.sh` entries this release no longer ships — collected in `$WORK/retired.derived` (`install.sh:1086-1089`, initialised `install.sh:1629`, passed to the merge at `install.sh:1727`) / `$script:DerivedRetired` (`install.ps1:102-104`, `install.ps1:1087`). The preflight merge uses `RETIRED_HOOKS` only (`install.sh:608`); in ps1 the derived list is still empty when `"check"` runs (`install.ps1:1066`);
  - **uninstall:** every `hooks/<name>.sh` the manifest tracks (`manifest_hook_names`, `install.sh:1238-1242`, passed at `install.sh:1670-1671`; `install.ps1:796`).
  Each name must match `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+\.sh$` or the installer exits (`install-hook-settings.sh:33-44`); ps1 builds the `bash $HOME/.claude/hooks/<name>` set from both lists (`install.ps1:474-475`).
- Uninstall prints `unregister hook: <command>` for each removed command (`install-hook-settings.sh:73-80`, `install.ps1:535-538`).

### FindingsLedgerEvent

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `persistence` |
| **Ingestion route** | Append-only JSONL at `<main repo>/.claude/ledger/findings.jsonl`, resolved via `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` so linked worktrees converge on the main checkout's ledger; falls back to the nearest CWD ancestor containing `.claude/`; `--ledger PATH` overrides (`ledger.py:178-212`, `ledger.py:241-247`). Written by `cmd_add` (`ledger.py:332-380`) and `_transition` for `promote` / `retire` (`ledger.py:426-448`) through `append_event`, one `json.dumps(event, sort_keys=True, ensure_ascii=False)` line per event (`ledger.py:268-279`), under an exclusive sidecar `<ledger>.lock` with a `LOCK_TIMEOUT_S = 20` deadline, exit 2 on timeout (`ledger.py:100`, `ledger.py:215-238`). Read back and validated by `read_events` (`ledger.py:250-265`): every line must carry all `REQUIRED_KEYS` and a `status` in `STATUSES`, else exit 2. `cmd_add` rejects any control character (Unicode category `Cc`, via `has_control_chars`) in `--file` / `--category` / `--symbol` with exit 2 (`ledger.py:328-336`), so a newline-joined scheme-1 payload cannot collide with a scheme-2 one (`ledger.py:167-172`, `ledger-format.md:78-84`). Events are never edited or deleted (`ledger-format.md:9-11`). |
| **Source** | `.claude/skills/findings-ledger/scripts/ledger.py` (lines 90-100, 131-175, 250-279, 328-380, 426-460); prose schema `.claude/skills/findings-ledger/references/ledger-format.md` (lines 14-29, 65-84; reviewer category vocabulary 93-109) |

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fingerprint` | string (16 hex) | yes | sha256 hex truncated to `FINGERPRINT_LEN = 16` (`ledger.py:94`). Scheme 1: `file.strip() + "\n" + normalize(claim)` (`ledger.py:131-135`, must stay byte-stable). Scheme 2: `"\n".join(("v2", normalize_path(file), category, normalize_symbol(symbol)))` (`ledger.py:167-175`); a namespaced category's `/` is part of the hashed string |
| `fingerprint_scheme` | integer `1` \| `2` | no | `2` (`SCHEME_STRUCTURED`) when `add --category` given, else `1` (`SCHEME_TEXT`) — `ledger.py:95-96`, `ledger.py:345-357`. Absent on pre-#279 events, read as `1` (`ledger.py:309`, `ledger.py:435`). Transition events copy it from the fingerprint's first event (`ledger.py:435`) |
| `category` | string (kebab-case slug, optionally `namespace/slug`) \| null | no | Stripped, lowercased and must match `CATEGORY_RE` = `^(?:<slug>/)?<slug>$` with `<slug>` = `[a-z0-9]+(?:-[a-z0-9]+)*` — at most one namespace segment, e.g. `fail-open`, `slop/unused-dep` — else exit 2 (`ledger.py:97-99`, `ledger.py:146-149`, `ledger.py:346-350`); the normalized value is what is stored (`ledger.py:368`). Reviewer vocabulary (prose only, not enforced by `ledger.py`): `code-reviewer` / `security-reviewer` use the slugs `security/scanner-unavailable`, `slop/scanner-unavailable`, `slop/nonexistent-api`, `slop/unused-dep`, `slop/stdlib-dup-dep`, `dead-code`, `slop/duplicated-helper`, `slop/speculative-generality`, `slop/comment-noise`, each with a fixed `--symbol` convention (`ledger-format.md:93-109`). No control characters (`ledger.py:333-336`). Null for scheme 1; absent on legacy events (read via `.get`, `ledger.py:310`, `ledger.py:436`) |
| `symbol` | string \| null | no | Stored as passed (`args.symbol`, `ledger.py:369`); normalized only inside the hash — backticks, a trailing `()` and surrounding whitespace peeled in any order until stable, then internal whitespace collapsed (`ledger.py:152-164`). No control characters (`ledger.py:333-336`). `--symbol` without `--category` exits 2 (`ledger.py:353-354`). Null when omitted; absent on legacy events |
| `file` | string | yes | Repo-relative path the finding is about (`ledger.py:508`); stored raw (`ledger.py:370`). No control characters (`ledger.py:333-336`). Normalized only in the scheme-2 hash: trimmed, `\` → `/`, empty and `.` segments dropped (so `a//b`, `./a/b`, `a/./b`, `a/b/` all hash as `a/b`), leading `/` kept, `..` left alone (`ledger.py:138-143`). Scheme 1 uses `file.strip()` only (`ledger.py:134`) |
| `claim` | string | yes | One-sentence claim summary (`ledger.py:509`). Transition events copy the first event's claim (`ledger.py:439`) |
| `tier` | integer `1` \| `2` | yes | `--tier` choices `(1, 2)` (`ledger.py:510`); tier 1 without non-blank `--evidence` is demoted to 2 (`ledger.py:340-344`). Transitions copy the first event's tier (`ledger.py:440`) |
| `source` | string | yes | Emitting agent on `add` (`ledger.py:512`); default `"triage"` on `promote` / `retire` (`ledger.py:536`, `ledger.py:543`) |
| `run_id` | string \| null | yes (key) | Required on `add` (`ledger.py:513`); defaults to null on transitions (`ledger.py:537`, `ledger.py:544`). Recurrence counts distinct `run_id`s among `NEW`/`RECURRING` sightings (`ledger.py:299-303`) |
| `date` | string `YYYY-MM-DD` | yes | Defaults to today; validated with `date.fromisoformat` (`ledger.py:282-286`, `ledger.py:515`, `ledger.py:553-554`) |
| `evidence` | string \| null | yes (key) | Evidence artifact path; required non-blank for `PROMOTED` (`ledger.py:454-455`); always null on `RETIRED-NOISE` (`retire` passes no evidence, `_transition` default `None`: `ledger.py:426`, `ledger.py:459-460`) |
| `status` | enum string | yes | `NEW` \| `RECURRING` \| `INVESTIGATING` \| `PROMOTED` \| `RETIRED-NOISE` (`ledger.py:90`). On `add`: `RECURRING` only if a prior sighting of the fingerprint came from a **different** `run_id`, else `NEW` (`ledger.py:362-364`) |

`REQUIRED_KEYS` = `fingerprint, file, claim, tier, source, run_id, date, evidence, status` (`ledger.py:92-93`); `fingerprint_scheme`, `category`, `symbol` are written on every new event but not required on read.

### FindingsLedgerExportRow

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `persistence` |
| **Ingestion route** | Written by `ledger.py export --out <path>` (`cmd_export`, `ledger.py:467-496`) to a user-supplied path, intended as a committable team-shared artifact outside `.claude/ledger/` (e.g. `.claude/ledger-shared/<name>.jsonl`, `ledger-format.md:187-188`). Refuses any `--out` that resolves inside the ledger's directory (the ledger, its `.lock` sidecar, or any sibling) with exit 2 (`ledger.py:470-472`). Reads events under the same `locked()` sidecar lock as writers (`ledger.py:473-474`). JSONL, one row per fingerprint, sorted by fingerprint, `sort_keys=True, ensure_ascii=False` (`ledger.py:476`, `ledger.py:480-481`). Rewritten in full atomically: `tempfile.mkstemp(dir=out.parent, prefix=".<name>.", suffix=".tmp")`, `chmod` to `0o666 & ~umask`, write, then `os.replace` onto `--out`; the temp file is unlinked on any failure (`ledger.py:482-495`). No claim text, but `file` and `symbol` are reviewer-supplied free text copied verbatim from the first event, so in team-shared mode they land in git history (`ledger-format.md:196-199`). Not read back by `ledger.py` (`ledger-format.md:199`). |
| **Source** | `.claude/skills/findings-ledger/scripts/ledger.py` (lines 297-319, 463-496); prose `.claude/skills/findings-ledger/references/ledger-format.md` (lines 170-201) |

Keys are exactly `EXPORT_KEYS` (`ledger.py:463-464`), derived from `summarize` (`ledger.py:297-319`):

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fingerprint` | string (16 hex) | yes | Group key — `ledger.py:308` |
| `fingerprint_scheme` | integer `1` \| `2` | yes | From first event; `1` when absent (`ledger.py:309`) |
| `category` | string \| null | yes (key) | From first event (`ledger.py:310`); same format as the event `category` (may be `namespace/slug`) |
| `symbol` | string \| null | yes (key) | From first event (`ledger.py:311`), raw as passed to `add` — reviewer-supplied free text, committed in team-shared mode |
| `file` | string | yes | From first event (`ledger.py:315`), raw as passed to `add` — reviewer-supplied free text, committed in team-shared mode |
| `count` | integer ≥ 0 | yes | Distinct `run_id`s among `NEW`/`RECURRING` events (`ledger.py:299`, `ledger.py:303`) |
| `status` | enum string | yes | Status of the most recent event (`ledger.py:304`) |
| `first_seen` | string `YYYY-MM-DD` | yes | Earliest sighting date; first event's `date` if no sightings (`ledger.py:306`, `ledger.py:317`, `ledger.py:479`) |
| `last_seen` | string `YYYY-MM-DD` | yes | Latest sighting date; first event's `date` if no sightings (`ledger.py:306`, `ledger.py:318`, `ledger.py:479`) |

### InstallPackList

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `persistence` (shipped config) |
| **Ingestion route** | Committed files `assets/packs/<pack>.txt`, one per opt-in install pack: `assets/packs/games.txt`, `assets/packs/marketing.txt`. Hand-maintained (no generator). Ships in the release tarball (`ARCHIVE_PATHS` includes `assets/packs`, `release.sh:72`; comment `release.sh:69-70`). **Read** on install, update and dry run (never on uninstall) before anything is staged: `install.sh` `load_pack` (`install.sh:1318-1336`, path `$REPO_ROOT/assets/packs/<pack>.txt` at `install.sh:1321`, called from `pack_plan` for each name in `PACKS`, `install.sh:1495-1496`, into `$WORK/pack.<pack>`) and `install.ps1` `Read-PackList` (`install.ps1:851-866`, path `install.ps1:854`, called from `Set-PackPlan`, `install.ps1:965-966`). The parsed entries are also the paths `pack_tracked` / `pack_shipped_files` / `Get-PackEvidence` look for, and the paths `pack_adopt` hands to `sync_place` for a `--without-<pack>` on a root with no manifest (see `AgenticOsInstallManifest` "Pack evidence" and "Pre-manifest `--without`"). Also read by `eval/metrics/lib/arm-isolation.sh` `iso_on_manifest` (`arm-isolation.sh:158-163`, see `ArmIsolationRecord`). **Checked** by `scripts/validate.sh` invariant `packs` (`check_packs`, `validate.sh:1157-1271`, called `validate.sh:1291`) and by the pack-list parity part of invariant `ship-manifest` (`validate.sh:573-597`). The parsed entries decide which skills and agents are left out of a run's stage and recorded in the manifest's `packs` array (see `AgenticOsInstallManifest`). Exercised by `scripts/install-packs-test.sh` (read, not run, for this catalog). |
| **Source** | `assets/packs/games.txt` (lines 1-12); `assets/packs/marketing.txt` (lines 1-14); `install.sh` (lines 77-81, 1299-1548); `install.ps1` (lines 68-70, 836-866, 879-1001, 1035-1038); `scripts/validate.sh` (lines 573-597, 1157-1271, 1291); `scripts/release.sh` (lines 69-72) |

**Shape** (`assets/packs/games.txt:1-12`):

```
# Games pack: opt in with `install.sh --with-games` (install.ps1 -WithGames).
# Not part of a default install.
# One entry per line: skills/<name> or agents/<name>.md. validate.sh (packs)
# checks every entry exists and is in exactly one pack.
skills/game-balancer
skills/game-systems-designer
skills/godot-engineer
skills/iap-manager
skills/phaser-engineer
agents/game-design-shaper.md
agents/godot-engineer.md
agents/phaser-engineer.md
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| file name | `<pack>.txt` | yes | The pack name is the basename. `validate.sh` requires `^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*\.txt$` and a regular file (`validate.sh:1179-1182`). The set of names must equal `PACKS` in `install.sh` (`PACKS="games marketing"`, `install.sh:79`) and `$Packs` in `install.ps1` (`install.ps1:70`), else `ship-manifest` fails with `PACKS (<sh>) != pack files in assets/packs/ (<files>)` / `$Packs (…) != …`, or `no PACKS="..." list, so the packs in assets/packs/ (<files>) would ship as core` when the list is missing (`validate.sh:578-597`). The installer flags `--with-<pack>` / `--without-<pack>` (ps1 `-With<Pack>` / `-Without<Pack>`) take these names |
| comment / blank line | line | no | Skipped when it matches `^[[:space:]]*(#\|$)`, i.e. blank, whitespace-only or a whole-line `#` comment (`install.sh:1329`; `install.ps1:861` uses `^\s*(#\|$)`; `validate.sh:1188`; `arm-isolation.sh:169`). A trailing CR is stripped first (`install.sh:1328`, `install.ps1:860`, `validate.sh:1187`). An inline comment after an entry is not allowed (the entry would fail the regex) |
| entry | line | no (a pack may be empty for the installer; `install-packs-test.sh` requires both shipped lists non-empty) | `skills/<name>` (a skill directory under `.claude/skills/`) or `agents/<name>.md` (an agent file under `.claude/agents/`). Installer regex `PACK_ENTRY_RE='^(skills/[a-z0-9-]+\|agents/[a-z0-9-]+[.]md)$'` (`install.sh:1315`; ps1 `$PackEntryRe`, `install.ps1:846`, case-sensitive `-cnotmatch`, `install.ps1:862`); any other line aborts the run with `Error: <f> lists '<line>', which is not skills/<name> or agents/<name>.md. Nothing was changed.` (`install.sh:1330-1333`, `install.ps1:862`). `validate.sh` is stricter: `^(skills/[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*\|agents/[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*\.md)$` (kebab-case, no leading, trailing or doubled `-`, `validate.sh:1170`), the item must exist (`.claude/skills/<name>/` dir or `.claude/agents/<name>.md` file, `validate.sh:1193-1195`), and an item may appear only once across all packs (`… is already in the <pack> pack (an item belongs to one pack)`, `validate.sh:1196-1201`). Order is not significant |

**Rules:**

- **Missing list** — `install.sh` `load_pack` refuses a missing or symlinked list: `Error: <f> is missing (or a symlink): this payload predates opt-in packs or is incomplete. Use the install.sh from the same release as the payload (the pinned one-liner URL), or install from a local clone. Nothing was changed.`, exit 1 (`install.sh:1322-1325`); ps1 throws the same text naming `install.ps1` for a reparse point or non-file (`install.ps1:855-857`). `validate.sh` `check_packs` returns 0 when `assets/packs/` does not exist (`validate.sh:1175`).
- **Effect on staging** — per root kind an entry names: Claude `skills/<n>/` and `agents/<n>.md`; Codex skills root `<n>/`; Codex config root `agents/<n>.toml` (`pack_path`, `install.sh:1338-1348`). The entries of every pack that is not installed are removed from the stage (`unstage_packs`, `install.sh:1538-1548`: `rm -rf <skills stage>/<n>`, `rm -f <agents stage>/<n>.md`; Codex agents skipped at conversion, `install.sh:1582`; ps1 `Remove-Item -Recurse` on `Join-Rel $Stage <entry>`, `install.ps1:1035-1038`). The installer re-checks every entry against `PACK_ENTRY_RE` because it deletes staged paths by them (comment `install.sh:1318-1319`, `install.ps1:851-852`). For a `--without-<pack>` on a root with no manifest, `pack_adopt` also uses the entries to find the pack's AgenticOS copies on disk (Claude, Codex skills root) or to name leftover `agents/<n>.toml` files (Codex config root, `install.sh:1456-1464`); see `AgenticOsInstallManifest` "Pre-manifest `--without`".
- **No cross-pack links** (`validate.sh` `packs` (d), `validate.sh:1165-1168`, `validate.sh:1206-1270`): a relative markdown link from a core file (any `.claude/skills/**/*.md`, `.claude/agents/*.md`, or shipped command) into a pack item, or from one pack into another, fails with `relative link '<target>' reaches the <pack> pack from <core\|<owner> pack>; an install without that pack would have a dangling link — write the name as plain text` (`validate.sh:1269`). Two link forms are scanned (`validate.sh:1218-1221`): inline links, every `](target)` match (`grep -oHE '\]\([^)]+\)'`), and reference-style definitions, whole lines matching `^[[:space:]]{0,3}\[[^]]+\]:[[:space:]]*[^[:space:]]` (at most three spaces of indent, `grep -HE`). For an inline link the trailing `)` is cut, and when the target starts with `<` (the `](<target>)` form) that `<` and everything from the first `>` are cut (`validate.sh:1249-1250`). For a definition the `[ref]:` prefix and following blanks are cut, then one leading `<` and everything from the first `>` (`validate.sh:1252-1253`). For both forms the target is then cut at the first blank or tab (dropping a `"title"`) and at `#` (`validate.sh:1255`); empty targets, URLs (`://`), `mailto:` and absolute `/` paths are skipped (`validate.sh:1256`). The rest resolves lexically against the linking file's dir, and a hit is reported only when both the file and the target lie under `.claude/` and the target's pack differs from the file's (`validate.sh:1257-1262`). A failure of the scan itself fails the check (`validate.sh:1263-1265`). Links inside one pack are allowed. This is why the core skills and agents in this diff name pack items as plain text.
- **Arm isolation** — `iso_on_manifest <src> [enabled packs]` cats every `assets/packs/<pack>.txt` whose `<pack>` is not in the space-separated enabled list (from the arm's `--with-<pack>` flags; empty for `run-arms.sh`), when the directory exists (failing the call if `cat` fails), and drops a listed item's files from the expected ON-arm file set; a `skills/<n>/…` path is matched on `skills/<n>`; an export with no `assets/packs` (v3.0.1 and earlier) ships everything (`arm-isolation.sh:150-172`).

### MemoryEntryFile

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `persistence` |
| **Ingestion route** | One Markdown file per durable fact written to `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-.}/.claude/memory/<snake_case>.md` by the `memory-extraction` skill (procedure step 5, `SKILL.md:85`). Append-or-update, never clobber — only the entry file(s) for facts written this run are touched (`SKILL.md:137-157`). |
| **Source** | `.claude/skills/memory-extraction/SKILL.md` (Memory-file format, lines 93–135) |

Filename is `snake_case.md` (underscores), a short subject-descriptive slug (`SKILL.md:97`).

**Shape** (YAML frontmatter + Markdown body; example from `SKILL.md:103-108`):

```
---
name: no-python-use-rust
description: One line — the fact and, for a user/feedback fact, whose it is.
metadata:
  type: feedback
---

<body — Markdown prose; conventions vary by type>
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `name` | string (kebab-case) | yes | Same words as the filename with `_`→`-`, e.g. `no-python-use-rust` — `SKILL.md:100`, `SKILL.md:111-112` |
| `description` | string (one line) | yes | The fact; for a user/feedback fact, whose it is — `SKILL.md:105` |
| `metadata` | object | yes | Container nesting `type` (a top-level `type:` is wrong) — `SKILL.md:99-100`, `SKILL.md:106` |
| `metadata.type` | enum string | yes | `user` \| `feedback` \| `project` \| `reference` — `SKILL.md:113` |
| body | Markdown prose | yes | Per-`type` conventions below — `SKILL.md:114-123` |

Body conventions:

- **feedback** — leads with the rule/directive (quote the correction verbatim when available), then a `**Why:**` line and a `**How to apply:**` line — `SKILL.md:115-117`.
- **project** / **user** — states the fact and why it matters; add `**How to apply:**` when there is a concrete action — `SKILL.md:118-119`.
- Relative dates converted to absolute (`"Thursday"` → `2026-07-13`) — `SKILL.md:120`.
- Sibling cross-references via wikilink `[[other_slug]]` (snake_case filename, no extension) that must resolve to a real sibling — `SKILL.md:121-123`.

Created via `mkdir -p` (`memory-extract.sh:54`), overwritten each Stop with `printf '%s %s\n'` (`memory-extract.sh:59`, `memory-extract.sh:67`), read back at `memory-extract.sh:49`. `<sid>` = the Stop event `session_id` sanitized to `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]` with `..` neutralized, defaulting to `last` when empty/`.`/`..` (`memory-extract.sh:38-42`). <turns> <nudged_at>

### MemoryExtractStopHookIO

| Field | Value |

| **Kind** | `message` |
| **Ingestion route** | The Claude Code **Stop** hook. INPUT is the Stop event JSON read from **stdin** (`event="$(cat)"`, `memory-extract.sh:29`); OUTPUT is one JSON object printed to **stdout** and consumed by the Claude Code hook runtime. Fail-open: any error (no `jq`, empty/malformed stdin, unwritable fs) emits allow `{}` and exits 0 (`memory-extract.sh:26`, `memory-extract.sh:31-34`, `memory-extract.sh:54`). |
| **Source** | `.claude/hooks/memory-extract.sh` (lines 26–73) |

**INPUT** (Stop event, stdin) — only `session_id` is consumed:

```json
{ "session_id": "sess_xyz" }
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `session_id` | string | no | Only field read (`.session_id // empty`); empty → `"last"`, then sanitized — `memory-extract.sh:38-39` |

`transcript_path` and `stop_hook_active`, though standard Stop-event fields, are NOT referenced by this hook (verified: no occurrence in the script; the header comment explicitly disclaims any dependence on `stop_hook_active` — `memory-extract.sh:15`).

**OUTPUT** (stdout) — exactly one of:

Allow (`memory-extract.sh:26`):

```json
{}
```

Block / steer (`memory-extract.sh:72`):

```json
{ "decision": "block", "reason": "<nudge text>" }
```

| `decision` | string | only when blocking | Literal `"block"` — `memory-extract.sh:72` |
| `reason` | string | only when blocking | Nudge instructing the still-live agent to run the `memory-extraction` skill now; interpolates the sanitized `session_id` — `memory-extract.sh:70`, `memory-extract.sh:72` |

### PostEditScanHookIO

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `message` |
| **Ingestion route** | The Claude Code **PostToolUse** hook, registered with matcher `Edit\|Write\|MultiEdit` and `"timeout": 30` in `.claude/settings.json:28-34` (consumer copy: `assets/consumer/claude-settings.json:18-24`). INPUT is the PostToolUse event JSON read from **stdin** (`input=$(< /dev/stdin)`, `post-edit-scan.sh:50`); OUTPUT is printed to **stdout** for the hook runtime. Always exits 0. **Fails open** (`post-edit-scan.sh:37-40`): missing `jq`, an unparseable payload, a missing/non-string/empty `file_path`, or `mktemp` failure prints one `post-edit-scan: ...` line to stderr and exits 0 with no stdout (`note()`, `post-edit-scan.sh:48`, `post-edit-scan.sh:51`, `post-edit-scan.sh:59-63`, `post-edit-scan.sh:98`). |
| **Source** | `.claude/hooks/post-edit-scan.sh` (lines 42–261) |

**INPUT** (PostToolUse event, stdin) — only this field is consumed:

```json
{ "tool_input": { "file_path": "/abs/path/to/edited/file" } }
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tool_input.file_path` | string (non-empty) | yes | Not a string → jq `error("no file_path")` → fail-open note; `""` → fail-open note — `post-edit-scan.sh:59-63`. **Unsafe characters:** `UNSAFE_RE='[\p{Cc}\p{Cf}\p{Zl}\p{Zp}]'` (C0/C1 controls, format chars such as zero-width and bidi overrides, U+2028/U+2029) — `post-edit-scan.sh:53-56`. A payload path matching it (jq `test($re)`) becomes `"skip"` and exits 0 silently; the path is never echoed — `post-edit-scan.sh:58-64`. Symlinks resolved (`resolve()`, `post-edit-scan.sh:66-76`); silently skipped (exit 0, no stderr) if resolution fails, the resolved path is outside the project root, the resolved path contains a control character (bash `*[[:cntrl:]]*`, `post-edit-scan.sh:91`) or matches `UNSAFE_RE` (`jq -Rse ... 'test($re) \| not'`; a jq error also skips, `post-edit-scan.sh:89-92`), it is not a regular file, or it is over `MAX_BYTES` = 1048576 — `post-edit-scan.sh:44`, `post-edit-scan.sh:87-95` |

**Environment** consumed:

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` | string (dir path) | no | Containment root. Falls back to `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`, then `pwd -P` — `post-edit-scan.sh:78-85`. Finding paths are relative to this root (`rel`, `post-edit-scan.sh:96`) |
| `GITLEAKS_CONFIG`, `GITLEAKS_CONFIG_TOML` | string | no | Deliberately **unset** for the gitleaks run (edited tree is untrusted) — `post-edit-scan.sh:199` |

**Consumed external payload** — gitleaks JSON report (`--report-format json`, written to a temp file, `post-edit-scan.sh:199-202`). gitleaks runs whenever it is installed (`post-edit-scan.sh:197`), including after the regex pass hit the repeat cap. The report is read only when gitleaks exits 0 within `GITLEAKS_TIMEOUT` = 5 s and is a JSON array (`post-edit-scan.sh:46`, `post-edit-scan.sh:204-209`); otherwise a stderr line is printed and the regex findings still stand (`post-edit-scan.sh:214`).

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `[].StartLine` | number | yes | Line number in the finding line — `post-edit-scan.sh:212` |
| `[].RuleID` | string | yes | Emitted as rule `gitleaks:<RuleID>`; also the `hit()` rule key — `post-edit-scan.sh:210-212` |

**OUTPUT** (stdout) — exactly one of:

Clean or skipped: no output, exit 0 (`[ -s "$first" ] || exit 0`, `post-edit-scan.sh:247`, plus the skip/fail-open exits above).

Findings (`post-edit-scan.sh:249-260`). The reason text is assembled in a temp file and passed to jq on **stdin** (`jq -Rsc '{decision:"block", reason:rtrimstr("\n")}' < "$tmp/reason"`, `post-edit-scan.sh:260`), not via argv:

```json
{"decision":"block","reason":"<text>"}
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `decision` | string | yes | Literal `"block"`. For PostToolUse this attaches `reason` to the tool result; the edit is not undone — `post-edit-scan.sh:28-30`, `post-edit-scan.sh:260` |
| `reason` | string | yes | Header `post-edit-scan found problems in the file you just edited (<relpath>). The edit was applied; fix these now:` (`post-edit-scan.sh:250`), then `- <finding>` lines: all first hits (`sort -u "$first"`, `post-edit-scan.sh:251`), then the repeat hits (`sort -u "$extra" | grep -vxF -f "$first"`, at most `MAX_FINDINGS` = 20, `post-edit-scan.sh:45`, `post-edit-scan.sh:252`). When the cap was hit, the line `- ...stopped after 20 repeat findings (the first hit of every rule is always listed); more may exist. Fix these, and the next edit rescans the file.` follows — `post-edit-scan.sh:253-255`. If any first-hit line contains ` secret `, a rotation/remediation sentence is appended — `post-edit-scan.sh:256-258`. Trailing newline trimmed (`rtrimstr`) — `post-edit-scan.sh:260` |

Finding line formats inside `reason`:

| Format | Rule / checker values | Source |
|---|---|---|
| `<relpath>:<line> secret <rule>` | Case-sensitive regexes: `aws-access-key-id`, `private-key`, `github-token`, `slack-token`, `stripe-live-key`, `openai-api-key` (`sk-proj-`), `anthropic-api-key` (`sk-ant-`), `google-api-key`, `jwt`. Case-insensitive `generic-secret-assignment` (see Notes). `gitleaks:<RuleID>` | `post-edit-scan.sh:121-131`, `post-edit-scan.sh:136-137`, `post-edit-scan.sh:169-193`, `post-edit-scan.sh:210-212` |
| `<relpath> syntax bash -n: line <N>: syntax error` | `*.sh` files | `post-edit-scan.sh:229-233` |
| `<relpath> syntax jq: line <N>, column <M>: invalid JSON` | `*.json` files, except JSONC files (`*.jsonc`, `*/.vscode/*.json`, basename `tsconfig*.json`, `jsconfig*.json`, `devcontainer.json`, `.devcontainer.json`, `.eslintrc*.json`, `biome.json`), which skip the jq check | `post-edit-scan.sh:221-227`, `post-edit-scan.sh:234-238` |
| `<relpath> syntax python: line <N>: <ErrorClass>` | `*.py` files, when `python3` is on PATH. `<ErrorClass>` is the last `^[A-Za-z]+(Error\|Exception)` match (fallback `compile failed`); bytecode goes to a temp `pycache_prefix` | `post-edit-scan.sh:239-244` |

In all three syntax formats the `line …: ` prefix is omitted when no `line N(, column M)?` is found in the checker output (`line_of()`, `${pl:+$pl: }`, `post-edit-scan.sh:220`, `post-edit-scan.sh:231-232`, `post-edit-scan.sh:236-237`, `post-edit-scan.sh:241-243`). Checker messages themselves are never echoed (`post-edit-scan.sh:218-219`). Syntax findings are written straight to `$first` (not through `hit()`), so they are never capped (`post-edit-scan.sh:232`, `post-edit-scan.sh:237`, `post-edit-scan.sh:243`).

Notes:
- Secret **values are never emitted** — only `file:line rule`; gitleaks runs with `--redact` (`post-edit-scan.sh:12-13`, `post-edit-scan.sh:199`). Syntax findings keep only the line/column and error class because checker messages can quote file content (`post-edit-scan.sh:17-18`, `post-edit-scan.sh:218-219`).
- **Cap** (`hit <rule> <finding>`, `post-edit-scan.sh:100-118`): the first hit of each rule key (built-in rule names, `generic-secret-assignment`, `gitleaks:<RuleID>`) is always written to `$first`, tracked in the space-delimited `seen` list. Later hits of an already-seen rule go to `$extra` while `extras` < `MAX_FINDINGS` = 20; past that, `hit` sets `capped=1` and returns 1. The built-in rule loop and the generic-assignment loop `break` on that return (`post-edit-scan.sh:174`, `post-edit-scan.sh:192`); the gitleaks loop ignores it (`|| true`, `post-edit-scan.sh:211`). So after the cap, each later rule (and each new gitleaks RuleID) still records its first hit; only repeats are dropped. `capped` is set only when a repeat is actually refused, so exactly 20 repeats with none further does not print the stop line. Each file is `sort -u`-ed, and repeat lines whose text equals a first-hit line (for example two matches of one rule on the same line) are dropped with `grep -vxF -f "$first"`, so no finding is listed twice (`post-edit-scan.sh:251-252`).
- **`generic-secret-assignment`** (`GENERIC_RE`, `grep -i`, `post-edit-scan.sh:132-137`, `post-edit-scan.sh:177-193`): the key name must END in `password`, `passwd`, `passphrase`, `secret`, `token`, `api[_-]?key`, `access[_-]?key`, or `private[_-]?key`, optionally followed by `[_-]?(key|secret)` and an optional closing quote, then `:=`, `=>`, `:` or `=`. The value is either quoted (16+ chars, no quote or whitespace) or unquoted for `.env`/YAML (16+ chars excluding quotes, whitespace, `#,;(){}<>`). The extracted value must also pass `looks_secret()` (`post-edit-scan.sh:148-162`, call `post-edit-scan.sh:182`): contains a digit AND a letter; no `/` or whitespace; does not start with `$` (rejects `$VAR` and bcrypt/argon hashes); not a dotted identifier (`^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*(\.[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)+$`). **AWS exemption** (`post-edit-scan.sh:182-191`): a value that fails `looks_secret()` is still reported if it matches `^[A-Za-z0-9/+]{40}$`, contains a digit, and the full source line (`sed -n "${ln}p"`) contains `aws_secret` or `secret_access_key` (case-insensitive, under `nocasematch`, `post-edit-scan.sh:139`, `post-edit-scan.sh:195`).
- Matches judged placeholders are suppressed, case-insensitively (`is_placeholder()`, `post-edit-scan.sh:140-146`; called at `post-edit-scan.sh:173`, `post-edit-scan.sh:181`): substrings `example`, `xxxx`, `changeme`, `change_me`, `replace`, `placeholder`, `your_`, `your-`, `redacted`, `dummy`, or `<...>`, `${`, `{{`, `://`. Lockfiles skip the built-in regex set but not gitleaks (`post-edit-scan.sh:164-169`).
- `.gitleaksignore` and inline `gitleaks:allow` are ignored (`--gitleaks-ignore-path` to an empty temp dir, `--ignore-gitleaks-allow`, `post-edit-scan.sh:198-201`). A missing scanner is never reported (`post-edit-scan.sh:35`).

---

### ReleaseArmProxyLogLine

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `persistence` |
| **Ingestion route** | `--sandbox docker` only. The HEAD copy of `lib/api-proxy.mjs` is staged into the harness dir (`run-release-arms.sh:275-277`) and baked into the **base** image as `/opt/harness/lib/api-proxy.mjs`, the only harness file in that image (`docker/Dockerfile:40-45`); the full harness goes only into the scorer image (`docker/Dockerfile.scorer:1-13`; both built at `run-release-arms.sh:373-390`). One proxy container per sample, `aos-proxy-<run_id[0:8]>-<k>`: uid 1000, `--cap-drop ALL`, `no-new-privileges`, read-only root fs (`DOCKER_HARDEN`, `run-release-arms.sh:462`), pids limit 64, on the run's `--internal` network with alias `aos-api-proxy`, then also connected to an egress network (`run-release-arms.sh:483-491`). **Env from the harness** (`run-release-arms.sh:485-489`): `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `PROXY_UPSTREAM`, `PROXY_MODELS="<--model> <--small-model>"`, `PROXY_BETAS`, `PROXY_MAX_TOKENS`, `PROXY_MAX_UNITS`, `PROXY_RESERVE_OVERHEAD`, `PROXY_CACHE_READ_WEIGHT` and, **new in round 7**, `PROXY_MAX_INFLIGHT` (`run-release-arms.sh:489`). Harness defaults `32000` / `2000000` / `4096` / `0.1` / `8`, each overridable from the operator's environment (`run-release-arms.sh:104-108`); `PROXY_BETAS` default `claude-code-20250219 fine-grained-tool-streaming-2025-05-14 prompt-caching-2024-07-31`, UNVERIFIED, without the 1M-context beta (`run-release-arms.sh:109-112`). The harness refuses to start, under either `--sandbox`, unless `PROXY_CACHE_READ_WEIGHT` matches `^[0-9]*\.?[0-9]+$` and is `> 0` and `<= 1` (`run-release-arms.sh:148-150`; tested with `0`, `run-release-arms-test.sh:432-435`). That is stricter than the proxy, which accepts any `Number()` form such as `1e-1`. `PROXY_MAX_INFLIGHT` is **not** validated by the harness; the proxy turns an unset, `0` or non-numeric value into `8` (`api-proxy.mjs:80`). The same values are recorded in every sample row's `settings` (see `ReleaseArmSampleRow.settings`). Not passed, so the proxy defaults apply: `PROXY_MAX_REQUESTS` (400), `PROXY_DRAIN_MS` (25000), `PROXY_IMAGE_RESERVE` (1600), `PROXY_UPSTREAM_TIMEOUT_MS` (600000), `PROXY_PORT` (8080), `PROXY_HOST` (`0.0.0.0`) (`api-proxy.mjs:72`, `api-proxy.mjs:82-83`, `api-proxy.mjs:285`, `api-proxy.mjs:321`). The round-6 `PROXY_MAX_QUEUE` and `PROXY_MAX_QUEUE_WAIT_MS` are **removed** (no queue). The proxy writes one JSON line per event to stdout (`log`, `api-proxy.mjs:104`). The harness waits up to 50 x 0.2 s for the `"listening"` line; if it does not appear, or the container stops, the sample start fails (`run-release-arms.sh:492-497`). **Capture:** `docker_capture_proxy` first removes the **sample** container (no new request can start), then runs `docker stop -t 30` on the proxy (SIGTERM, then up to 30 s for the drain and the `summary` line, longer than the 25 s default drain), then copies `docker logs <proxy> 2>&1` (stdout and stderr merged) through `lib/redact.mjs` into `<out>/proxy-<tag>.log` (`run-release-arms.sh:574-582`). Removing the sample container closes its connections, so any request still in flight is expected to end as a client abort (see Spend accounting rules; UNVERIFIED under docker, tested only without it). Called after the sample's prompts (`run-release-arms.sh:670`) and after a failed start (`run-release-arms.sh:634`). `<tag>` = `<arm>-<scenario>-r<round>` (`run-release-arms.sh:623`). The capture runs before the transcript copy and scoring (`run-release-arms.sh:669-673`). No file under `--sandbox none`. **Read by `sample-row.mjs`** as `PROXY_LOG` (`run-release-arms.sh:696`; `sample-row.mjs:64-124`), the authoritative source of a row's `tokens`, `proxy` and the proxy stop signals (see `ReleaseArmSampleRow`). It reads `cause` (`drain`, `inflight` and `budget` lines are counted apart, `sample-row.mjs:83-86`), the `end` lines' `units` and `note` (`sample-row.mjs:91-92`) and the summary fields (`sample-row.mjs:96-101`). Also read by the `--smoke` checklist, `lib/smoke-check.mjs` (run from the working tree, `run-release-arms.sh:610-615`): item 1 lists up to 5 `denied` lines that have no `cause` or have `cause:"budget"`, and fails on any. `cause:"inflight"` and `cause:"drain"` lines are ignored (**changed in round 8**, `smoke-check.mjs:26-29`). This now matches the row builder, where a budget 429 is a stop signal and an inflight 429 is not (`sample-row.mjs:118-123`), and the README (`eval/metrics/README.md:173-186`, `eval/metrics/README.md:289-290`). The round-7 code disagreement (a `cap` 429 failed item 1) is gone. **Changed in round 9 (comment only):** the item-1 comment now reads `no proxy denial (403 path, 400 beta/model/tool/field) and no spent unit budget (429 budget)` (`smoke-check.mjs:25`). The PASS text still reads `no proxy denials (no 403, no rejected beta/model/tool)` (`smoke-check.mjs:29`). Item 4 prints each `phase:"end"` line's `model` and `usage` (`smoke-check.mjs:52-57`). Read by `run-release-arms-docker-test.sh` (`run-release-arms-docker-test.sh:115`: `"denied":"not on the allowlist"` and `tool type not allowed: web_search`; `run-release-arms-docker-test.sh:116-117`: a `"phase":"start"` and a `"phase":"end"` line, and a `"summary":true` line in both sample logs). Exercised directly, without docker, by `eval/metrics/api-proxy-test.sh` (sections 1-12 rewritten in round 7; **sections 13-16 new in round 8**, `api-proxy-test.sh:218-250`; **section 17 new in round 9**, `api-proxy-test.sh:252-278`) against a plain-HTTP fake upstream (`fixtures/proxy/upstream.mjs`, modes `json` \| `sse` \| `gzip` \| `garbage` \| `error` \| `stall` \| **`midfail`** \| **`nodelta`**, `upstream.mjs:6-9`; **new:** `midfail` sends 200 headers and one `message_start` event, then destroys the socket after 200 ms, and `nodelta` sends a complete SSE stream with only `message_start`, `upstream.mjs:33-44`), a parallel client (`fixtures/proxy/client.mjs`), `fixtures/proxy/abort-client.mjs` (one `POST /v1/messages` per argument, in parallel, each destroyed after its argument's ms; **new:** if the responses have not all ended after 8 s it prints `["HUNG"]` and exits 3, so a hung response fails the check instead of hanging the test, `abort-client.mjs:19-22`) and the **new** `fixtures/proxy/deep-client.mjs` (posts one `/v1/messages` body whose message content is `tool_result` blocks nested `<depth>` deep, built as a string, and prints the HTTP status or `ERR <code>`; `deep-client.mjs:1-17`). The test reads the proxy's stdout and the `summary` line (`api-proxy-test.sh:29-53`); run in CI (`.github/workflows/validate.yml:40`). Not read by `summarize-samples.mjs` directly (it reads the row's `proxy`). The `--smoke` probe proxy (`aos-probe-proxy-<run_id[0:8]>`) is a separate echo-upstream container whose log is never captured, started without `PROXY_MAX_INFLIGHT` (`run-release-arms.sh:589-607`). |
| **Source** | `eval/metrics/lib/api-proxy.mjs` (lines 1-348); `eval/metrics/run-release-arms.sh` (lines 104-114, 148-151, 270-279, 327-355, 373-394, 461-504, 574-615, 623, 634, 669-673, 696); `eval/metrics/lib/smoke-check.mjs` (lines 1-74; no data shape of its own); `eval/metrics/api-proxy-test.sh` (lines 1-280); `eval/metrics/run-release-arms-test.sh` (lines 432-435); `eval/metrics/fixtures/proxy/upstream.mjs` (lines 1-60); `eval/metrics/fixtures/proxy/client.mjs`; `eval/metrics/fixtures/proxy/abort-client.mjs` (lines 1-22); `eval/metrics/fixtures/proxy/deep-client.mjs` (lines 1-17); `eval/metrics/docker/Dockerfile` (lines 40-45); `eval/metrics/docker/Dockerfile.scorer`; `eval/metrics/lib/redact.mjs`; `.github/workflows/validate.yml` (line 40); prose `eval/metrics/README.md` ("Sandbox", proxy container, lines 131-199; "Trust model", lines 211-222) |

**Spend accounting rules** (from the code; per proxy = per sample). Round 7 replaced the single-slot queue with concurrent admission (`api-proxy.mjs:25-47`).

- **Concurrent admission, no queue.** A request that passes the pre-body checks and validation goes straight to `forward` (`api-proxy.mjs:318`). It is admitted only if all of these pass. **Changed in round 9:** they run in this order (`api-proxy.mjs:222-231`). (a) **Budget:** `committed + its reservation <= PROXY_MAX_UNITS`. Other in-flight reservations are **not** counted here. A failure gives `cause:"budget"` (`api-proxy.mjs:225-227`). (b) Fewer than `PROXY_MAX_INFLIGHT` requests are in flight, else `cause:"inflight"` (`api-proxy.mjs:228`). (c) `committed + (sum of in-flight reservations) + its reservation <= PROXY_MAX_UNITS` (`reservedInFlight`, `api-proxy.mjs:107`). A failure here means the request fits on what is spent but not together with the other in-flight reservations. The code calls that a transient spike, and it gives `cause:"inflight"`, **no longer `budget`** (`api-proxy.mjs:222-224`, `api-proxy.mjs:229-231`). Otherwise it gets an **immediate 429**: no queue, no waiting. Since round 8, the 429s are split by cause (`api-proxy.mjs:111-115`). `cause:"inflight"` is counted in `inflight_rejected`: transient, neither a denial nor a stop. `cause:"budget"` is counted in `budget_rejected`: not a denial either, but downstream it is a stop (`ReleaseArmSampleRow.stop_signals`). The budget check now runs first, so a request that fails (a) and also (b) is a `budget` 429 (round 8: `inflight`). So a fan-out arm's own parallelism can no longer produce a `budget` 429 while what it has spent still leaves room for the request (`eval/metrics/README.md:174-184`). Since round 9 the file-header comment (`api-proxy.mjs:29-34`) matches this order and names the spike case as `inflight`. **Changed in round 8:** only an admitted request bumps the request-cap counter `count`; past `PROXY_MAX_REQUESTS` it is denied `request cap <N> reached` (429, no `cause`, a denial) (`api-proxy.mjs:232-234`). So 429 retries, and every request denied earlier, no longer use up the request cap. A request that passes all three takes the next `id`, counts in `requests`, is added to the in-flight map with its reservation, and raises `max_inflight_seen` if needed (`api-proxy.mjs:235-238`). The in-flight limit is 8 by default; the code comment gives the reason (the main loop plus a few parallel subagents / background calls) (`api-proxy.mjs:76-80`). Because every in-flight reservation counts, parallel requests cannot race the cap. Tested: 100 parallel requests against a 400000-unit cap end at `committed_units <= 400000`, `max_inflight_seen` between 2 and 8, `denials 0`, `inflight_rejected + budget_rejected > 0` and an `inflight` or `budget` cause line, and the log has no `queue_wait` or `"dropped"` text (`api-proxy-test.sh:55-66`). With `PROXY_MAX_INFLIGHT=2`, 5 requests against an 800 ms upstream give 2 forwarded, 3 x 429, `inflight_rejected 3`, `budget_rejected 0`, `denials 0` (`api-proxy-test.sh:179-184`). **New:** with `PROXY_MAX_INFLIGHT=1` and `PROXY_MAX_REQUESTS=3`, 6 parallel requests (1 admitted, 5 inflight 429s) and then 2 more, one at a time, give 3 forwarded, a final `200` and no `request cap` line (`api-proxy-test.sh:218-226`). With `PROXY_MAX_UNITS=300`, one request over the cap gives `budget_rejected 1`, `inflight_rejected 0`, `denials 0` and a `unit budget exhausted` line (`api-proxy-test.sh:228-233`). **New in round 9, section 17** (`api-proxy-test.sh:252-278`). (1) With `PROXY_MAX_UNITS=6000`, 2 parallel requests and then 1 more give `budget_rejected 0`, `inflight_rejected >= 1`, and a `200` for the later request (`api-proxy-test.sh:255-261`). (2) A 30-request fan-out against a 60000-unit cap gives `budget_rejected 0` and `committed_units < 6000` (`api-proxy-test.sh:264-268`). (3) With `PROXY_MAX_INFLIGHT=1` and one request in flight, a request whose reservation alone exceeds a 5000-unit cap gives `budget_rejected 1` and `inflight_rejected 0` (`api-proxy-test.sh:270-278`).
- **Reservation.** `reserved` = re-serialized body bytes + `PROXY_RESERVE_OVERHEAD` + `max_tokens` + images x `PROXY_IMAGE_RESERVE` for `POST /v1/messages`, and `0` for `count_tokens` and `GET /v1/models` (`api-proxy.mjs:221`). It is computed before the admission checks. `images` = the number of `image` blocks found by the content-block walk, nested `tool_result.content` included (`api-proxy.mjs:139-140`, `api-proxy.mjs:164-171`). The per-image extra exists because image tokens do not scale with base64 bytes; the 1600 bound is UNVERIFIED (`api-proxy.mjs:83`). The bound assumes a response's units do not exceed its reservation ("a token covers at least a byte of the request", `api-proxy.mjs:27-29`); that assumption is UNVERIFIED. `document` blocks are rejected outright because a compressed PDF can tokenize past its bytes (`api-proxy.mjs:18-19`). A response whose parsed usage exceeds its reservation is still charged in full, so `committed` can then pass the cap by that excess. Tested: an honest upstream never pushes `committed_units` past the cap (`api-proxy-test.sh:68-73`); two base64 images give bytes + 100 + 10 + 3200 (`api-proxy-test.sh:213-216`).
- **Cap units.** `units(u) = input + cache_creation + output + ceil(cache_read x PROXY_CACHE_READ_WEIGHT)` (`api-proxy.mjs:106`). The weight is `0.1` when the variable is unset. When it is set, it must be a finite number with `0 < w <= 1`; any other value (`0`, an empty string, a negative, `abc`) becomes `1`, so cache reads count in full (fail closed; `api-proxy.mjs:81`). UNVERIFIED that `0.1` matches billing. Tested: `12 + 1000 + 300 + 50000 x 0.1 = 6312` (`api-proxy-test.sh:141-147`); `abc` and `0` both give 51312 (`api-proxy-test.sh:150-158`).
- **Charging** (`finish`, `api-proxy.mjs:242-255`). One outcome per request: the first `finish` call removes it from the in-flight map and later calls do nothing (`api-proxy.mjs:245-246`). Non-`messages` routes are charged `0` (`api-proxy.mjs:248`). A `messages` response whose usage parsed is charged `units(usage)` and added to `by_model` (`charge`, `api-proxy.mjs:212-218`, `api-proxy.mjs:249`). Without usage the request is charged its **full reservation**, booked to `by_model` as `input = reserved - max_tokens`, `output = max_tokens`, and counted in `unparsed`, in two cases (`api-proxy.mjs:250-253`): (1) a reply that ended normally with a 2xx status and no parsable usage; (2) **new:** a failure (`failed = true`) after the request body had been fully written upstream (`bodySent`, set by the `up.end` callback, `api-proxy.mjs:240`, `api-proxy.mjs:292`). Failures are a client abort, an upstream request error or timeout, and an upstream response stream error (`api-proxy.mjs:256-261`, `api-proxy.mjs:280`, `api-proxy.mjs:285-288`). **New in round 8:** on an upstream response stream error (mid-pipe, after the headers were passed on) the proxy also destroys the client response, so the sample is not left waiting for the rest (`api-proxy.mjs:278-280`). Its `finish` runs first, so the client-side `close` that follows finds the request already ended and does not count it in `client_aborted` (`api-proxy.mjs:256-257`). A failure **before** the body was written is charged `0`, as is a non-2xx reply without usage (UNVERIFIED that upstream does not bill it, `api-proxy.mjs:45-47`). Tested: unparseable 2xx = reservation and `unparsed 1`; upstream error body = `0`, `unparsed 0` (`api-proxy-test.sh:83-92`); an upstream killed before the request (body never sent) = `0`, `unparsed 0` (`api-proxy-test.sh:159-163`); **new:** a `midfail` upstream returns `aborted` to the client within 2 s and is charged the reservation with `unparsed 1` (`api-proxy-test.sh:235-243`).
- **Client abort (changed).** When the sample's connection closes before the response was fully written, the proxy counts `client_aborted`, runs `finish(0, null, 'client aborted request', true)`, and destroys the upstream request (`api-proxy.mjs:256-261`). So the `end` line has status `0`, `usage_parsed: false`, note `client aborted request`, and the full reservation if the body had been written upstream, else `0`. The round-6 split on whether upstream had responded, and status `-1`, are gone. Destroying the upstream request is expected to log a `phase:"error"` line with `error` `client aborted` after the `end` line (`api-proxy.mjs:260`, `api-proxy.mjs:286-288`); this is not tested. A charged abort also counts in `unparsed`. Tested: 3 requests aborted after 300 ms against a 1500 ms upstream all reach upstream and give `client_aborted 3` and 3 x the reservation, with 3 `client aborted request` lines (`api-proxy-test.sh:165-173`). An abort after 400 ms against a `stall` upstream gives `client_aborted 1` and the reservation (`api-proxy-test.sh:174-177`).
- **Identity encoding and defensive decompression.** Every upstream request carries `accept-encoding: identity` (`api-proxy.mjs:315`; the client's own `accept-encoding` is in `DROP`, `api-proxy.mjs:97-99`). The reply is still decoded for parsing when its `content-encoding` names `gzip`, `br` or `deflate`; a decode error means "no usage" (`parseUsage`, `api-proxy.mjs:175-182`). The reply bytes reach the sample unchanged (`api-proxy.mjs:281-282`). Usage is read from the SSE `message_start.message.usage` and `message_delta.usage` events, or from the JSON body's `usage`. For each of the four fields the largest finite value `>= 0` seen is kept (`api-proxy.mjs:184-209`). **New in round 8:** an SSE reply (`content-type` matching `text/event-stream`) with no `message_delta` event parses to no usage (`null`), even when `message_start` carried usage (`api-proxy.mjs:195-204`). So such a 2xx reply is charged its reservation and counted in `unparsed`; a non-2xx one is charged `0` (see Charging). Tested: a `nodelta` upstream gives `committed_units` = the reservation and `unparsed 1` (`api-proxy-test.sh:245-250`). Only the first 32 MiB of a reply are kept for parsing (`api-proxy.mjs:84`, `api-proxy.mjs:276`). Tested: identity asked on 3 of 3 requests, a gzip reply still counted (`api-proxy-test.sh:75-81`).
- **Re-serialized forward body.** For the two POST routes, the body forwarded upstream is `JSON.stringify` of the parsed, validated object, so a duplicated JSON key is collapsed to the value that was validated (`api-proxy.mjs:23`, `api-proxy.mjs:171`, `api-proxy.mjs:292`). `GET /v1/models` forwards the raw body (`api-proxy.mjs:146`). Tested (`api-proxy-test.sh:112-117`).
- **Validator errors are 400s (new).** `validate` runs inside `try`; any exception becomes the denial `invalid request (validator error)` (400) instead of a crash (`api-proxy.mjs:312`). Tested: a body nested 20000 `tool_result` levels deep gets 400 and the proxy still answers the next request (`api-proxy-test.sh:207-209`).
- **Top-level field allowlist (new, replaces the round-6 `mcp_servers` / `container` / `service_tier` checks).** Every top-level key of a POST body must be in `BODY_KEYS`: `model`, `messages`, `system`, `max_tokens`, `tools`, `tool_choice`, `stream`, `temperature`, `thinking`, `metadata`, `stop_sequences`, `top_p`, `top_k` (`api-proxy.mjs:93-94`, `api-proxy.mjs:150`). Any other key is denied `request field not allowed: <key, cut to 40>`. So `service_tier` is now rejected with any value, including `auto`. UNVERIFIED that Claude Code needs no other field (`api-proxy.mjs:12-13`). The field values (other than `model`, `max_tokens`, `tools`, `messages`, `system`) are not checked. Tested: `inference_geo`, `speed`, `mcp_servers`, `container`, `service_tier`, `context_management` are rejected; all thirteen allowed fields pass together; the `inference_geo` rejection is logged (`api-proxy-test.sh:131-139`).
- **Tool allowlist.** `tools`, when present, must be an array of objects. Each may have only `name`, `description`, `input_schema`, `cache_control`, `type`. `type` must be absent or `"custom"`. `name` must be a string, and `input_schema` an object that is not an array (`api-proxy.mjs:95`, `api-proxy.mjs:155-163`). UNVERIFIED that Claude Code 2.1.311 sends only such tools (`eval/metrics/README.md:205-208`). Tested: a plain tool passes; `web_search_20250305`, an unknown `type`, an unknown key and an array `input_schema` are rejected (`api-proxy-test.sh:122-127`).
- **Content-block allowlist (changed).** These blocks are checked: every content block in each message's `content`, in `system`, and recursively in a `tool_result` block's `content`. Each must be an object whose `type` is one of `text`, `image`, `tool_use`, `tool_result`, `thinking`, `redacted_thinking`; `document` and any other type are rejected (`api-proxy.mjs:96`, `api-proxy.mjs:129-143`). A string `content` / `system` is allowed as is. **Changed:** a block with a `source` must have `source.type` exactly `base64`; `text`, `url` and `file` sources are rejected (`api-proxy.mjs:136-138`). **New:** the walk stops at `MAX_DEPTH` = 32; messages and `system` start at depth 1 and each nested `tool_result` adds 1, and a walk past 32 is denied `content nested deeper than 32` (`api-proxy.mjs:85`, `api-proxy.mjs:130`, `api-proxy.mjs:140`, `api-proxy.mjs:168`, `api-proxy.mjs:170`). `tool_use.input` is not walked. UNVERIFIED that Claude Code sends no other block type; the README states it only for `document` (`eval/metrics/README.md:161-165`). Tested: a base64 image and text / `tool_use` / `tool_result` blocks pass; a url image, a file source, a text source, a url image nested in `tool_result.content`, a base64 `document` and `server_tool_use` are rejected; nesting 30 passes and 40 is rejected; at least 7 block rejections are logged (`api-proxy-test.sh:186-212`).
- **Drain on SIGTERM / SIGINT (changed)** (`summary`, `api-proxy.mjs:326-346`). The first signal sets `draining` and closes the listener; a second signal does nothing (`api-proxy.mjs:327-329`). A new request is denied 503 `proxy is shutting down` with `cause:"drain"`, checked on arrival and again after its body is read (`api-proxy.mjs:296`, `api-proxy.mjs:314`). Such denials count in `drain_rejected`, not `denials` (`api-proxy.mjs:114`). The proxy polls every 50 ms, for up to `PROXY_DRAIN_MS`, until **every** in-flight request has ended (`api-proxy.mjs:331-332`). Each request still in flight after that is charged its reservation, booked as `input`, counted in `unfinished`, and logged as a `phase:"unfinished"` line (`api-proxy.mjs:333-338`); with concurrency `unfinished` can now exceed 1. Then the `summary` line is written and the process exits 0 (`api-proxy.mjs:340-343`). Tested: 5 parallel requests in flight at SIGTERM are all counted (5 received, 5 `start` lines, `committed_units 100`) (`api-proxy-test.sh:94-103`); a request still in flight after `PROXY_DRAIN_MS=200` is charged its reservation (`api-proxy-test.sh:104-110`).

**Env knobs** (all read once at start): `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (`api-proxy.mjs:70`). `PROXY_UPSTREAM`, default `https://api.anthropic.com` (`api-proxy.mjs:71`). It has two test hooks: `echo` answers locally (`api-proxy.mjs:262-271`), and an `http://` URL selects the `http` module, used only by `api-proxy-test.sh` (`api-proxy.mjs:61-63`, `api-proxy.mjs:273`; `api-proxy-test.sh:42`). The harness itself accepts only `echo` or `https://api.anthropic.com` (`run-release-arms.sh:151`). `PROXY_MAX_REQUESTS` (400), `PROXY_MAX_TOKENS` (32000), `PROXY_MAX_UNITS` (2000000), `PROXY_RESERVE_OVERHEAD` (4096), **`PROXY_MAX_INFLIGHT` (8, new)**, `PROXY_DRAIN_MS` (25000) and `PROXY_IMAGE_RESERVE` (1600) each fall back to the default when unset, `0` or not a number (`Number(x) || default`, `api-proxy.mjs:72-75`, `api-proxy.mjs:80`, `api-proxy.mjs:82-83`). So neither the in-flight limit nor the image reservation can be set to `0`. `PROXY_CACHE_READ_WEIGHT`: see Cap units (`api-proxy.mjs:81`). `PROXY_UPSTREAM_TIMEOUT_MS` (600000) is a socket idle timeout via `setTimeout`, then `destroy(new Error('upstream timeout'))` (`api-proxy.mjs:285`). `PROXY_PORT` (8080) and `PROXY_HOST` (`0.0.0.0`) (`api-proxy.mjs:321`). `PROXY_MODELS` and `PROXY_BETAS` are space-separated sets (`api-proxy.mjs:86-87`). An empty `PROXY_MODELS` denies every POST (`api-proxy.mjs:151`). Fixed constants: 32 MiB body limit, `MAX_DEPTH` 32 (`api-proxy.mjs:84-85`). `PROXY_MAX_QUEUE` and `PROXY_MAX_QUEUE_WAIT_MS` are no longer read.

**Shape** (one JSON object per line; `t` first, then the event keys, `api-proxy.mjs:104`; values illustrative; `echo` upstream lines look the same as forwarded ones):

```json
{"t":"2026-10-28T12:00:00.000Z","listening":8080,"upstream":"api.anthropic.com","max_units":2000000,"max_tokens":32000,"max_inflight":8,"cache_read_weight":0.1,"models":["claude-sonnet-5-5","claude-haiku-5-5"]}
{"t":"2026-10-28T12:00:01.000Z","phase":"start","id":1,"path":"/v1/messages","model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","reserved":42810,"committed":0,"inflight":1}
{"t":"2026-10-28T12:00:01.100Z","phase":"start","id":2,"path":"/v1/messages","model":"claude-haiku-5-5","reserved":5200,"committed":0,"inflight":2}
{"t":"2026-10-28T12:00:04.000Z","phase":"end","id":1,"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","status":200,"usage":{"input":12,"cache_read":50000,"cache_creation":1000,"output":300},"units":6312,"usage_parsed":true,"committed":6312}
{"t":"2026-10-28T12:00:05.000Z","method":"POST","path":"/v1/messages","status":400,"denied":"request field not allowed: service_tier"}
{"t":"2026-10-28T12:00:06.000Z","method":"POST","path":"/v1/messages","status":429,"denied":"proxy concurrency: 8 requests already in flight","cause":"inflight"}
{"t":"2026-10-28T12:00:07.000Z","phase":"end","id":2,"model":"claude-haiku-5-5","status":0,"usage":null,"units":5200,"usage_parsed":false,"committed":11512,"note":"client aborted request"}
{"t":"2026-10-28T12:00:07.000Z","phase":"error","id":2,"error":"client aborted"}
{"t":"2026-10-28T12:00:30.000Z","phase":"unfinished","id":5,"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","units":42810}
{"t":"2026-10-28T12:00:30.000Z","method":"POST","path":"/v1/messages","status":503,"denied":"proxy is shutting down","cause":"drain"}
{"t":"2026-10-28T12:00:30.000Z","summary":true,"signal":"SIGTERM","requests":5,"denials":1,"inflight_rejected":1,"budget_rejected":0,"drain_rejected":1,"client_aborted":1,"max_inflight_seen":2,"committed_units":54322,"unparsed":1,"unfinished":1,"by_model":{"claude-sonnet-5-5":{"input":42822,"cache_read":50000,"cache_creation":1000,"output":300},"claude-haiku-5-5":{"input":5190,"cache_read":0,"cache_creation":0,"output":10}},"cache_read_weight":0.1}
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `t` | string (ISO 8601 UTC) | yes | `new Date().toISOString()` when the line is written (`api-proxy.mjs:104`) |
| `listening` | integer | startup line only | `Number(PROXY_PORT) \|\| 8080` (`api-proxy.mjs:321-322`); `8080` under the harness. The startup line has exactly `t`, `listening`, `upstream`, `max_units`, `max_tokens`, **`max_inflight` (new)**, `cache_read_weight`, `models` (`api-proxy.mjs:321-324`); the image reservation is not logged |
| `upstream` | string | startup line only | `"echo"` under the echo hook, else `new URL(PROXY_UPSTREAM).host` (`api-proxy.mjs:322`): `api.anthropic.com` in a live run, `127.0.0.1:<port>` in `api-proxy-test.sh` |
| `max_units` | integer | startup line only | The per-sample cap in cap units, `PROXY_MAX_UNITS` (`api-proxy.mjs:74`, `api-proxy.mjs:323`) |
| `max_tokens` | integer | startup line only | The `max_tokens` ceiling per `/v1/messages` request, `PROXY_MAX_TOKENS` (`api-proxy.mjs:73`, `api-proxy.mjs:323`) |
| `max_inflight` | integer | startup line only | **New.** The in-flight limit, `PROXY_MAX_INFLIGHT` after the `\|\| 8` fallback (`api-proxy.mjs:80`, `api-proxy.mjs:323`) |
| `cache_read_weight` | number | startup and summary lines | The weight used for `cache_read` in cap units (`api-proxy.mjs:81`, `api-proxy.mjs:323`, `api-proxy.mjs:342`). Always a finite number with `0 < w <= 1` |
| `models` | array of string | startup line only | The `PROXY_MODELS` set, in first-seen order without duplicates (`api-proxy.mjs:86`, `api-proxy.mjs:323`). Under the harness: `[<--model>, <--small-model>]`, default `["claude-sonnet-5-5","claude-haiku-5-5"]` (`run-release-arms.sh:102-103`, `run-release-arms.sh:486`) |
| `phase` | enum string | request lines | `start` \| `end` \| `error` \| `unfinished` (`api-proxy.mjs:239`, `api-proxy.mjs:254`, `api-proxy.mjs:287`, `api-proxy.mjs:337`). Absent on startup, denial and summary lines |
| `id` | integer | `phase` lines | Per-proxy counter, 1-based. **Changed:** assigned only after a request passes the three admission checks (round 9) and, since round 8, the request cap (`api-proxy.mjs:222-235`), so an `inflight` / `budget` 429 or a request-cap denial takes no id and ids do not skip. Denied and drain-rejected requests take no id |
| `path` | string | `start` and denial lines | `safePath`: the URL pathname (query string dropped) cut to 200 characters, or `/?` when the URL does not parse (`api-proxy.mjs:105`, `api-proxy.mjs:116`, `api-proxy.mjs:239`) |
| `model` | string \| null | `start`, `end`, `unfinished` lines | `String(body.model)` for the POST routes; `null` for `GET /v1/models` (`api-proxy.mjs:146`, `api-proxy.mjs:171`) |
| `reserved` | integer | `start` lines | The reservation (see Spend accounting rules, `api-proxy.mjs:221`, `api-proxy.mjs:239`) |
| `committed` | number | `start` and `end` lines | `S.committed`: the sample's committed cap units, before this request on `start` and after its charge on `end` (`api-proxy.mjs:239`, `api-proxy.mjs:254`). It does not include other requests' in-flight reservations |
| `inflight` | integer | `start` lines | **New.** Number of requests in flight including this one, `1..PROXY_MAX_INFLIGHT` (`api-proxy.mjs:237-239`). Not read by `sample-row.mjs` |
| `status` | integer | `end` and denial lines | `end`: the upstream `statusCode` (`api-proxy.mjs:277`), or `200` for echo (`api-proxy.mjs:270`). It is `0` after an upstream request error or timeout (`api-proxy.mjs:288`), on a response stream error (`api-proxy.mjs:280`), and on a client abort (`api-proxy.mjs:259`). The round-6 status `-1` no longer occurs. Denial: the status sent to the sample (see `denied`). An upstream error status such as `429` appears on an `end` line, never as a denial |
| `usage` | object \| null | `end` lines | `{input, cache_read, cache_creation, output}` = the API's `input_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, each the largest finite value `>= 0` seen (`api-proxy.mjs:184-194`). `null` when no usage field was found, decoding failed, or the request ended in a failure (`api-proxy.mjs:209`, `api-proxy.mjs:254`). Echo: `{input: 7, cache_read: 0, cache_creation: 0, output: 11}` (`api-proxy.mjs:263`) |
| `units` | number | `end` and `unfinished` lines | Cap units charged for this request (see Charging, `api-proxy.mjs:247-253`); `unfinished`: its reservation (`api-proxy.mjs:337`). Read by the no-summary rebuild: `usage_parsed: false` with `units > 0` counts as unparsed (`sample-row.mjs:91`) |
| `usage_parsed` | boolean | `end` lines | Whether usage was parsed (`api-proxy.mjs:254`). `false` with `units > 0` on a `messages` request means the worst-case charge |
| `note` | string | some `end` lines | **Changed.** Present only on a failure: `client aborted request` (`api-proxy.mjs:259`), `upstream response error` (`api-proxy.mjs:280`) or `upstream request error`, which also covers the upstream timeout (`api-proxy.mjs:285-288`). The round-6 `client closed before/after upstream responded` values are gone. The no-summary rebuild counts `client aborted request` notes (`sample-row.mjs:92`) |
| `error` | string | `error` lines | `e.code`, else `e.message`, else `upstream error` (`api-proxy.mjs:287`). It is `upstream timeout` after `PROXY_UPSTREAM_TIMEOUT_MS` (`api-proxy.mjs:285`), and is expected to be `client aborted` after a client abort (`api-proxy.mjs:260`). After an upstream error, the `error` line comes first and the `end` line follows with status `0`; after a client abort, the `end` line comes first (`api-proxy.mjs:259-260`, `api-proxy.mjs:287-288`). The client gets `502` if no headers were sent yet (`api-proxy.mjs:289`) |
| `method` | string | denial lines | `req.method` (`api-proxy.mjs:116`) |
| `denied` | string | denial lines | Every rejection goes through `deny`. It logs `{method, path, status, denied}`, plus `cause` when given, and answers `{"type":"error","error":{"type":"proxy_denied","message":<denied>}}` (`api-proxy.mjs:111-119`). In check order: (1) `proxy is shutting down` (503, `cause:"drain"`) once draining (`api-proxy.mjs:296`). (2) `not on the allowlist` (403). Allowed are exactly `POST /v1/messages`, `POST /v1/messages/count_tokens`, `GET /v1/models` on the parsed pathname. An absolute-form or `//` target, a target not starting with `/`, an unparsable URL, or a pathname containing `..` or `%2` is refused (`routeFor`, `api-proxy.mjs:88-92`, `api-proxy.mjs:120-126`, `api-proxy.mjs:298`). (3) `proxy has no ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (503, `api-proxy.mjs:299`). (4) **Moved in round 8:** the request-cap check is no longer here; see (12).
