Drain-time 503s and inflight / budget 429s are not in a row's `denials`, so they are not counted here; inflight and budget 429s are summed in `proxy_inflight_429s` and `proxy_budget_429s`, and `proxy.drain_rejected` is not summed. Tested: `>= 12` over the docker test's two rows (`run-release-arms-docker-test.sh:94-95`) |
| `proxy_inflight_429s` | integer | yes | **New (round 8)**, replaces `proxy_cap_rejected`. Sum over **every** row of the group of `Number(row.proxy.inflight_rejected ?? row.proxy.cap_rejected)`, so an older row's `cap_rejected` is used when it has no `inflight_rejected`; a missing or non-numeric value adds 0 (`summarize-samples.mjs:103-106`, `summarize-samples.mjs:125`). The harness's own transient in-flight-limit 429s, which do not make a row incomplete. Printed as `inflight_429s=` |
| `proxy_inflight_429s_per_sample` | number | yes | **New (round 8).** `proxy_inflight_429s / samples`, rounded to 2 decimals; `0` for an empty group (`summarize-samples.mjs:126`). Read by `comparable` for its `warnings`. Not printed in the group header |
| `proxy_budget_429s` | integer | yes | **New (round 8).** Sum of `Number(row.proxy.budget_rejected)` over **every** row of the group; a missing or non-numeric value adds 0 (`summarize-samples.mjs:107`, `summarize-samples.mjs:127`; round 9 inlined the former `sumOf` helper with no change in value). A row with any is incomplete (`unit budget exhausted`), so it is not in `complete`. Printed as `budget_429s=` |
| `completion_rate` | number | yes | **New (round 7).** `complete / samples`, rounded to 3 decimals; `0` for an empty group (which cannot occur) (`summarize-samples.mjs:128`). **Changed (round 8):** no longer read by `comparable`. Printed in the group header as `completion=<P>%` (`summarize-samples.mjs:234`) |
| `run_ids` | array of string | yes | **New (round 7).** Distinct cleaned row `run_id` values over every row, a `null` or missing one as `unknown`, sorted (`summarize-samples.mjs:129`). Read by `comparable` |
| `sandboxes` | array of string | yes | **New (round 7).** Distinct cleaned row `sandbox` values over every row, a `null` or missing one as `unknown`, sorted (`summarize-samples.mjs:130`). Read by `comparable` |
| `settings` | array of string | yes | **New (round 7).** Distinct `JSON.stringify(row.settings ?? null)` strings over every row, sorted; a row without `settings` gives the string `"null"` (`summarize-samples.mjs:131`). Not cleaned, unlike the other string fields. Two rows match only when their `settings` objects serialize identically, key order included (`sample-row.mjs:217-221` always writes the same order). Read by `comparable` |
| `cost_sources` | object | yes | **New (round 6).** `{<cleaned cost_source>: <row count>}` over **every** row of the group, complete or not; a `null` or missing `cost_source` counts as `none` (`summarize-samples.mjs:100-101`, `summarize-samples.mjs:132`). Keys are sorted after cleaning. Values are the `ReleaseArmSampleRow.cost_source` strings, so an `unknown: N cap units …` row gets its own key per distinct `N` |
| `price_missing` | array of string | yes | **New (round 6).** Distinct cleaned entries of every row's `price_missing` (non-array values skipped), over every row, sorted (`summarize-samples.mjs:102`, `summarize-samples.mjs:133`). `[]` when no row lacks a price |
| `models` | array of string | yes | **New (round 6).** Distinct cleaned row `model` values over every row, a `null` or missing one as `unknown`, sorted (`summarize-samples.mjs:134`). Read by `comparable` |
| `prompts` | array | yes | **New (round 6).** Distinct raw row `prompts` values over every row, sorted numerically, not cleaned or type-checked (`summarize-samples.mjs:135`). Read by `comparable` |
| `harness` | array of string | yes | **New (round 6).** Distinct cleaned row `harness_sha` values over every row, a `null` or missing one as `unknown`, sorted (`summarize-samples.mjs:136`). Read by `comparable` |
| `primary` | object | yes | **Changed.** The proxy-authoritative block, over **complete rows only** (`summarize-samples.mjs:87-96`). Keys, in order, each `{n, mean, min, max}` (`stats`): `proxy_input_tokens` (row `tokens.input`), **`proxy_cache_read_tokens`** (`tokens.cache_read`, new), **`proxy_cache_creation_tokens`** (`tokens.cache_creation`, new), `proxy_output_tokens` (`tokens.output`), **`proxy_cap_units`** (`tokens.cap_units`, new), **`cost_usd_estimate`** (row `cost_usd`; renamed from `cost_usd`), `cli_cost_usd`. **Removed in round 7:** `proxy_queue_wait_ms_total` (rows no longer carry `proxy.queue_wait_ms`). Under `--sandbox none` (`proxy: null`) it has `n: 0`. Non-number values are skipped, so `n` counts the rows that have the value (`summarize-samples.mjs:29-34`). For example `--sandbox none` rows have `tokens: null` and give `n: 0`, and `price unknown` rows have `cost_usd: null` and do not count in `cost_usd_estimate`. `cost_usd_estimate` averages whatever `cost_source` each row has, and `cost_source` is not read here (it is counted in `cost_sources`): proxy estimates, `0` for `proxy: no billable requests`, and `cli-only (model-writable)` under none. `mean` is rounded to 2 decimals, so a small dollar cost can show as `0` or `0.01` while `min` / `max` are exact (`summarize-samples.mjs:27`, `summarize-samples.mjs:32`) |
| `metrics` | object | yes | Advisory (model-writable). Over **complete rows only** (`summarize-samples.mjs:108-111`). Fixed keys, each `{n, mean, min, max}`: `turns`, `output_tokens`, `input_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens`, `cache_creation_tokens` (from `metrics.turns` / `metrics.tokens.{output,input,cache_read,cache_creation}`), `repeat_read_files`, `repeated_tool_calls` (from `metrics.awareness_signals`), then `compact_boundaries` (from the row field) (`summarize-samples.mjs:16-24`, `summarize-samples.mjs:110-111`). `n` = number of numeric values; `mean` is rounded to 2 decimals; with `n: 0`, `mean` / `min` / `max` are `null` (`summarize-samples.mjs:27`, `summarize-samples.mjs:29-34`). Tested: a stopped sample gives `complete 0`, `turns.n 0` (`run-release-arms-test.sh:339-340`) |
| `metrics_trust` | string | yes | Always the constant `"awareness metrics are model-writable (advisory); primary tokens/cost are proxy-authoritative"` (`summarize-samples.mjs:139`) |
| `exploit` | object | yes | `{pass, fail, error, not_applicable}` counts over **complete rows only**, so the four sum to `complete`. A complete row's `exploit` other than `pass` / `fail` / `error` (i.e. `null`) counts as `not_applicable` (`summarize-samples.mjs:112-116`) |

### ReleaseHashesFile

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `persistence` (shipped config) |
| **Ingestion route** | Committed file `assets/release-hashes.txt`, regenerated by `scripts/gen-release-hashes.sh` (`OUT`, `gen-release-hashes.sh:25`; RELEASING.md step 0, `RELEASING.md:43-55`). Deterministic: same tags in, same bytes out (`LC_ALL=C`, `gen-release-hashes.sh:22`). Tags are `git tag -l` filtered to `^v[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$`, minus `--exclude <tag>`, sorted numerically (`gen-release-hashes.sh:31`, `gen-release-hashes.sh:39`); no tags → exit 2 (`gen-release-hashes.sh:40-43`). For each tag, each of `.claude/{skills,agents,commands,hooks}` that exists in the tag (`git cat-file -e`, `gen-release-hashes.sh:51`) is extracted with `git archive` (`gen-release-hashes.sh:48-53`). A tag with none of them is skipped (`gen-release-hashes.sh:54`); every other tag is appended to `$tmp/hashed` (`gen-release-hashes.sh:55`) and its files are hashed (`gen-release-hashes.sh:56-57`). Default mode rewrites the file (`mv`, `gen-release-hashes.sh:74`); `--check` compares the would-be output with `cmp -s` and exits 1 when the committed file is stale, 0 when current (`gen-release-hashes.sh:66-72`). `scripts/release.sh` runs `gen-release-hashes.sh --check --exclude "$VERSION"` (the version being built) before packing and ships the file in `ARCHIVE_PATHS` (`release.sh:44`, `release.sh:71-72`; the same array now also ships `assets/packs`, see `InstallPackList`). **Read** from `$REPO_ROOT/assets/release-hashes.txt` (local clone or extracted release) by `install.sh` (`RELEASE_HASHES`, `install.sh:377-380`) in `load_known` (`install.sh:532-544`, per root, called `install.sh:1060`, `install.sh:1259`, and by `pack_shipped_files` `install.sh:1376-1377`) and in the `SKILL_NAMES` computation (`install.sh:1622-1627`); and by `install.ps1` into `$script:KnownHashes` / `$script:SkillNames` (`install.ps1:354-369`). A missing file is tolerated: no known copies and no extra skill names (`install.sh:538`, `install.sh:1625`; `install.ps1:360`). Consumed for `AgenticOsInstallManifest` adoption ("Unmodified"), for classifying retired-skill files as prunable or foreign and so deciding which ones `--prune-legacy` / `-PruneLegacy` may delete (`install.sh:1170`, `install.ps1:1139`), for the skill-name path allowlist, as `ondisk` pack evidence (a pack file on a root with no manifest, or in `--codex --project`, counts only when it matches an earlier-release copy of that path here or this release's source copy; `AgenticOsInstallManifest` "Pack evidence"), and, by the same match, to pick the pre-manifest pack files that `install.sh --without-<pack>` may remove (`pack_adopt`; "Pre-manifest `--without`"). Also copied (and appended to) by `scripts/install-lifecycle-test.sh:73`, `install-lifecycle-test.sh:104`, `install-lifecycle-test.sh:530`. |
| **Source** | `scripts/gen-release-hashes.sh` (lines 1-75); `scripts/release.sh` (lines 67-72); `install.sh` (lines 377-380, 532-552, 1165-1189, 1365-1429, 1450-1473, 1622-1627); `install.ps1` (lines 354-377, 879-959, 1135-1150); `RELEASING.md` (lines 43-55) |

**Shape** (header from `gen-release-hashes.sh:60-64`; example lines from `assets/release-hashes.txt:1-3`):

```
# Generated by scripts/gen-release-hashes.sh — do not edit by hand.
# Tags: v1.0.0 v1.1.0 … v3.0.1
001d1aa9f60d961e87ddfd44b6404c324f87dd165cc932748cd1e16078ca555b  skills/phaser-engineer/SKILL.md
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| line 1 | comment | yes | Literal `# Generated by scripts/gen-release-hashes.sh — do not edit by hand.` (`gen-release-hashes.sh:61`) |
| line 2 | comment | yes | `# Tags: <space-separated vX.Y.Z>` in numeric order, listing **only the tags actually hashed**: the contents of `$tmp/hashed` (written per tag at `gen-release-hashes.sh:55`, after the skip at `gen-release-hashes.sh:54`), newlines turned to spaces and the trailing space removed (`gen-release-hashes.sh:62`). A tag excluded by `--exclude`, or one with none of the four `.claude/` dirs, is not listed. With no hashed tag the line is `# Tags: ` (the `cat` failure is tolerated, `gen-release-hashes.sh:62`) |
| body line `sha256` | string (64 lowercase hex) | yes | First 64 chars of `shasum -a 256` / `sha256sum` output (`gen-release-hashes.sh:37`, `gen-release-hashes.sh:57`) |
| separator | two spaces | yes | Exactly `"  "` (`gen-release-hashes.sh:57`) |
| body line `path` | string | yes | Relative to `.claude/` with the leading `./` removed, e.g. `skills/<name>/…`, `agents/…`, `commands/…`, `hooks/…`; lines whose path is outside `^[A-Za-z0-9._@+/-]+$` are dropped (`gen-release-hashes.sh:56-57`) |

Body lines are one per (sha, path) pair over every file in every hashed tag, `sort -u` deduplicated and byte-sorted (`gen-release-hashes.sh:58`), so a path appears once per distinct content it has had.

**Reader rules:**

- `install.sh` `load_known <skillp> <out>` (`install.sh:535-544`): skips `#` lines, lines without exactly 2 fields, and lines whose sha is not 64 chars (`install.sh:540`); emits `<path>\t<sha>`, `sort -u`. Path mapping by the root's skills prefix: `"skills/"` (Claude) and `"-"` (Codex config) keep the path as-is; `""` (Codex skills root) keeps only `skills/…` lines and strips `skills/` (`install.sh:542`). Looked up by exact `<rel>\t<sha>` match in `unmodified` (`grep -qxF`, `install.sh:551`).
- `install.sh` retired-skill classification (`install.sh:1165-1189`): each untracked file in a retired skill dir is checked with `unmodified "$w/known.tsv" <rel> <on-disk sha> "-"` (`install.sh:1170`), with or without `--prune-legacy`. The manifest sha is the placeholder `"-"`, so only an exact `<rel>\t<sha>` line in the known list makes the file prunable. Prunable files are queued for removal only with `--prune-legacy` (`install.sh:1171-1174`). Any other file is foreign and is never removed (`install.sh:1175-1181`). The counts drive the no-flag reporting (`install.sh:1183-1188`; see `AgenticOsInstallManifest`).
- `install.sh` `SKILL_NAMES` (`install.sh:1624-1627`): adds every `<name>` from lines matching `^[0-9a-f]{64}  skills/<name>/…` (`sed`, `install.sh:1626`) to the tombstones and this release's `$SRC/skills` dirs (`install.sh:1624`).
- `install.sh` `pack_shipped_files` (`install.sh:1373-1421`; also behind the `pack_shipped_file` wrapper, `install.sh:1423-1429`, and `pack_adopt` in `all` mode, `install.sh:1466`): `load_known "skills/"` (Claude) or `load_known ""` (Codex skills root) into `$WORK/pack.known` (`install.sh:1375-1379`; the Codex config root returns 1 and is never checked), then an exact `grep -qxF -- "<rel>\t<sha>"` per candidate file whose `<rel>` passed `REL_RE` (`install.sh:1411`).
- `install.ps1` (`install.ps1:357-369`): each line matching `^([0-9a-f]{64})  ([A-Za-z0-9._@+/-]+)$` (case-sensitive, `install.ps1:362`) adds the string `<path>` + TAB + `<sha>` to the ordinal `HashSet` `$script:KnownHashes` (`install.ps1:357`, `install.ps1:363`) and, for `skills/<name>/…`, `<name>` to `$script:SkillNames` (`install.ps1:364`); tombstones and `$Src\skills` dirs are added after (`install.ps1:368-369`). Paths are used as-is (Claude root only). Looked up in `Test-Unmodified` (`install.ps1:376`), including the retired-skill classification with a `$null` manifest sha (`install.ps1:1139`), and directly by `Get-PackShippedFiles` (no-manifest pack evidence via `Get-PackEvidence`, and the `-Without<Pack>` adoption) (`install.ps1:950`).

### ReviewGateStopHookIO

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `message` |
| **Ingestion route** | The Claude Code **Stop** hook, registered in `.claude/settings.json:41-43` (alongside `memory-extract.sh`; consumer copy `assets/consumer/claude-settings.json:28-29`). INPUT is the Stop event JSON on **stdin**, truncated to the first 4 MiB (`head -c 4194304`, `review-gate-stop.sh:53`). Reads `ReviewVerdictArtifact` files and git state only — never LLM output (`review-gate-stop.sh:2-6`). **Fails open** (`review-gate-stop.sh:42-45`, `fail_open` `review-gate-stop.sh:61-75`): missing `jq`/`git` (`review-gate-stop.sh:77-78`), unparseable payload (`review-gate-stop.sh:80`), no HEAD (`review-gate-stop.sh:98`), no default branch (`review-gate-stop.sh:104`), no merge-base (`review-gate-stop.sh:105`), `git diff`/`git ls-files` failure (`review-gate-stop.sh:119-121`), planner failure (`review-gate-stop.sh:173`, `review-gate-stop.sh:186`) → allow with a note, at most once per session when a `session_id` was read (see OUTPUT). |
| **Source** | `.claude/hooks/review-gate-stop.sh` (lines 47–216) |

**INPUT** (Stop event, stdin) — only these fields are consumed:

```json
{ "session_id": "abc-123", "stop_hook_active": false, "cwd": "/repo" }
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `session_id` | string | no | **Top-level only**, read with `jq -r '.session_id // empty \| strings'` after the jq-present and `stop_hook_active` checks (`review-gate-stop.sh:83-84`); kept only if the whole value matches `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,128}$`, otherwise treated as empty (`review-gate-stop.sh:85`). Initialised empty (`review-gate-stop.sh:55-57`), so on the jq-missing (`review-gate-stop.sh:77`) and unparseable-payload (`review-gate-stop.sh:80`) paths there is no session id and those fail-open notes repeat every turn. Keys the once-per-session fail-open marker; empty → no de-duplication (note printed every time) — `review-gate-stop.sh:65` |
| `stop_hook_active` | boolean | no | `.stop_hook_active // false`; `true` → allow (exit 0, no output) — one block per stop attempt — `review-gate-stop.sh:14`, `review-gate-stop.sh:80-81` |
| `cwd` | string | no | `.cwd // empty \| strings`. First candidate for the git root: candidates are payload `cwd`, then `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, then `$PWD`; empty candidates are skipped and the first one where `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` succeeds wins (worktree-aware). A `cwd` outside any repo falls through to the next candidate. Only when none of the three is inside a git repo → allow silently (exit 0, no output) — `review-gate-stop.sh:15-16`, `review-gate-stop.sh:87-95` |

**Environment:**

| Var | Use |
|---|---|
| `AGENTIC_OS_REVIEW_GATE` | Exactly `off` → allow immediately (opt-out) — `review-gate-stop.sh:13`, `review-gate-stop.sh:51` |
| `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` | Second git-root candidate, tried when payload `cwd` is empty/absent or not inside a git repo; then `$PWD` — `review-gate-stop.sh:90` |
| `TMPDIR` | Parent of the fail-open marker dir `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/agentic-os-review-gate-<uid>` (`id -u`) — `review-gate-stop.sh:64` |
| `SHIP_GATES_CHANGED_FILES` | Not read from the caller; set by the hook when invoking `scripts/gate-plan.sh --checkboxes` — `review-gate-stop.sh:156` |

**Decision inputs:**

- Default branch ref: first that resolves of `origin/HEAD`, `origin/main`, `origin/master`, `main`, `master`; none → fail-open — `review-gate-stop.sh:20-21`, `review-gate-stop.sh:99-104`. Base = `merge-base(ref, HEAD)` — `review-gate-stop.sh:105`.
- Moved-base warning: when the merge-base equals HEAD (no commits beyond `ref`) but `rev-list --count <r>..HEAD` ≥ 1 for another resolving candidate `r`, sets `review-gate: HEAD has no commits beyond <ref> but has commits beyond <r> — if <ref> was moved, the gate is checking the wrong base.` — `review-gate-stop.sh:107-116`.
- Changed paths = `git diff --name-only <base> HEAD` + `git diff --name-only HEAD` + `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard`, minus paths under `.claude/reviews/` and `.claude/worktrees/` (`strip_noise`), and minus untracked `dir/` entries where `<root>/<dir>.git` exists (nested repos / worktrees) — `review-gate-stop.sh:17-19`, `review-gate-stop.sh:118-128`. "Dirty" = tracked-dirty + untracked after the same filtering — `review-gate-stop.sh:127`.
- Planner: `<x>/scripts/gate-plan.sh` is used only when the hook's own physical dir ends in `/.claude/hooks` (`<x>` = the prefix), `<x>` and the reviewed root share the same physical `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` (covers worktrees), and the script exists — `review-gate-stop.sh:34-40`, `review-gate-stop.sh:136-150`. Required reviewers = planner output ∩ the four reviewer names (`review-gate-stop.sh:155-160`). Otherwise (consumer fallback) `code-reviewer` + `security-reviewer` whenever any changed path is not `''`, `*.md` (except `DATA_MODEL.md`), `LICENSE`, `NOTICE`, or `docs/*` — `review-gate-stop.sh:38-40`, `review-gate-stop.sh:162-170`.
- No changed paths or no required reviewers → allow (`review-gate-stop.sh:134`, `review-gate-stop.sh:174`).

**OUTPUT** (stdout) — exactly one of:

Allow, silent: no output, exit 0 (`review-gate-stop.sh:51`, `review-gate-stop.sh:81`, `review-gate-stop.sh:95`; and `review-gate-stop.sh:130-133` when no warning is set).

Allow with message (`{"systemMessage": string}`):

```json
{"systemMessage":"review-gate: HEAD has no commits beyond origin/main but has commits beyond main — ..."}
```

- Moved-base warning on an allow path — built with `jq -cn` (`allow`, `review-gate-stop.sh:130-133`; reached from `review-gate-stop.sh:134`, `review-gate-stop.sh:174`, `review-gate-stop.sh:207`).
- Fail-open note `review-gate-stop: <reason> — gate skipped (fail-open)` — also echoed to stderr; built with `printf` (no `jq`), JSON-safe only because notes contain literals and fixed ref names (`review-gate-stop.sh:59-62`, `review-gate-stop.sh:72-73`). Printed **once per session**: when a valid `session_id` was read and the marker dir `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/agentic-os-review-gate-<uid>` exists (created `mkdir -m 700` if absent) as a real directory, not a symlink, owned by the user, an existing marker file `<dir>/<session_id>` → exit 0 with no output at all; else the marker is created and the note printed (`review-gate-stop.sh:63-71`). If `session_id` is empty (always so for the jq-missing and unparseable-payload notes) or the dir check fails, the note is printed without de-duplication.

Block (`block`, `review-gate-stop.sh:176-181`, built with `jq -cn`):

```json
{"decision":"block","reason":"review-gate: <text>"}
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `systemMessage` | string | only on allow-with-message | See above. Never emitted together with `decision` |
| `decision` | string | only when blocking | Literal `"block"` — `review-gate-stop.sh:179` |
| `reason` | string | only when blocking | Prefixed `review-gate: `; when a moved-base warning is set, ` Warning: <warning without its review-gate: prefix>` is appended (`review-gate-stop.sh:177-178`). Either (a) dirty paths that themselves require reviewers (`review-gate-stop.sh:185-188`): `uncommitted or untracked changes need review (<up to 5 dirty paths>). Verdicts bind to a HEAD sha, so commit only the changes you intend to ship, then dispatch <all required agents> <how>. Never commit files you did not create for this task (user work in progress, build output); if such files are what remains, tell the user instead of committing them.` (`review-gate-stop.sh:189`); or (b) `HEAD <12-char sha> has changes vs <ref> that require review.` (`review-gate-stop.sh:209`) plus `No captured verdict for this HEAD from: <agents>. Dispatch exactly these agents (subagent_type as named) <how>.` (`review-gate-stop.sh:211`), `Evidenced hold from: <agents> — fix the tier 0/1 findings, commit, and re-dispatch those agents.` (`review-gate-stop.sh:213`), and `Info (advisory, not blocking): fix-before-merge from: <agents>.` (`review-gate-stop.sh:215`). `<how>` = `with only the base ref (<ref>) and the changed paths; each must end with its agentic-os/review-verdict@1 block, which the SubagentStop hook records in .claude/reviews/` (`review-gate-stop.sh:183`); agent lists are comma-joined (`list`, `review-gate-stop.sh:182`) |

Always exits `0` (`review-gate-stop.sh:74`, `review-gate-stop.sh:132`, `review-gate-stop.sh:180`; blocks via the JSON `decision`, not the exit code). `fix-before-merge` never blocks on its own (`review-gate-stop.sh:31-32`, `review-gate-stop.sh:201`, `review-gate-stop.sh:207`).

### ReviewScanReport

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `message` |
| **Ingestion route** | CLI stdout + process exit code. `.claude/skills/security/scripts/security-scan.sh [--base <ref>] [--paths <path>...] [--json]` (`security-scan.sh:6`, `security-scan.sh:133`) and `.claude/skills/code-review-and-quality/scripts/check-imports.sh` (same flags, `check-imports.sh:6`, `check-imports.sh:126`), run from the project root. Both ship to consumers with their skills; `scripts/security-scan.sh` and `scripts/check-imports.sh` are now `exec` wrappers onto them (`scripts/security-scan.sh:2-4`, `scripts/check-imports.sh:2-5`). With `--json`, exactly one JSON object is printed to **stdout** (`security-scan.sh:309-310`, `check-imports.sh:706-707`; check-imports' nothing-in-scope early exit prints at `check-imports.sh:323-324`); without it, a human-readable text rendering of the same data (not part of this contract, but note: every repo-controlled string has C0/C1 control chars and DEL escaped as `\xNN`, and every line is prefixed `security-scan: ` / `check-imports: ` so none can start with a `::` workflow command — `security-scan.sh:300-303`, `security-scan.sh:311-322`, `check-imports.sh:88-89`, `check-imports.sh:308-320`, `check-imports.sh:327`). Consumed by reviewer agents as Tier 0/1 evidence. Usage errors go to stderr only, with no JSON. In this section, `security-scan.sh:N` and `check-imports.sh:N` cite the canonical skill copies. |
| **Source** | `.claude/skills/security/scripts/security-scan.sh` (header lines 1–89; `safe_path`/`not_found` lines 93–120; env lines 122–130; ROOT + second PATH filter lines 173–179; `in_root` lines 229–236; `skip` lines 238–241; `SCRUB_SUBS`/`emit`/`notes_json` lines 264–288; `finish` result/exit lines 305–332; semgrep lines 377–409; offline dependency-audit lines 572–579), `.claude/skills/security/scripts/security-scan-semgrep.yml` (offline default ruleset, rule ids `aos.*`), `.claude/skills/code-review-and-quality/scripts/check-imports.sh` (header lines 1–101; `safe_path` lines 104–123; ROOT + second PATH filter lines 203–209; `PY_REASON`/`py_ok` lines 211–223; `skip`/`skip_once` lines 238–244; nothing-in-scope lines 322–330; `SCRUB_SUBS`/`emit`/`finding`/`status_of` lines 334–356; result/exit lines 704–716); wrappers `scripts/security-scan.sh`, `scripts/check-imports.sh` |

**Inputs** (environment; affect which findings appear, not the payload shape):

| Variable | Script | Default | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `SECURITY_SCAN_OFFLINE` | security-scan | unset (online) | Only the exact value `1` enables offline mode (`[[ "${SECURITY_SCAN_OFFLINE:-}" == 1 ]]`); any other value is online — `security-scan.sh:55-64`, `security-scan.sh:122-123`. Offline: the `SEMGREP_CONFIG` default changes (next row); a `SEMGREP_CONFIG` that is not an existing local file or directory (`! -e`) makes semgrep `unavailable` and is never fetched (`security-scan.sh:381-383`); dependency-audit is `unavailable` whenever a lockfile was collected (`security-scan.sh:577-579`; the `"no supported lockfile found"` check runs first, `security-scan.sh:574-576`). gitleaks unchanged (`security-scan.sh:64`) |
| `SEMGREP_CONFIG` | security-scan | online: `p/security-audit`; offline: absolute physical path of `security-scan-semgrep.yml` beside the script (`$(cd -P -- "$(dirname -- "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd -P)/security-scan-semgrep.yml`) | Registry name fetches rules over the network; may be a local file/dir — `security-scan.sh:34-37`, `security-scan.sh:56-60`, `security-scan.sh:124-128` |
| `SECURITY_SCAN_NPM_REGISTRY` | security-scan | `https://registry.npmjs.org/` | Passed as explicit `--registry` to `npm audit`; a repo `.npmrc` is never read — `security-scan.sh:45-47`, `security-scan.sh:129`, `security-scan.sh:503`. Not reached in offline mode |
| `SECURITY_SCAN_GITLEAKS_CONFIG` | security-scan | unset (gitleaks default rules) | When set, passed as `--config` to `gitleaks dir` — `security-scan.sh:27-28`, `security-scan.sh:130`, `security-scan.sh:343` |
| `PATH` | both | caller's `PATH` | **Sanitized before any tool lookup.** `safe_path <dir>...` keeps only absolute entries whose physical location (`cd -P`) is outside every given dir; relative/empty entries and entries that cannot be `cd`'d into are dropped. Applied right after `set -euo pipefail` against the physical cwd (`security-scan.sh:93-112`, `check-imports.sh:104-122`), then again against the repo root and physical cwd once `ROOT` is known (`security-scan.sh:179`, `check-imports.sh:209`). So `jq`, `git`, `gitleaks`, `semgrep`, `osv-scanner`, `npm`, `pip-audit`, `cargo`/`cargo-audit` and `$PYTHON` are never taken from the scanned tree (header `security-scan.sh:66-70`). security-scan keeps `ORIG_PATH` only to word the `unavailable` reason (`not_found`, `security-scan.sh:113-120`; see `reason`); a dependency auditor found only in-tree falls through to the existing `"no audit tool for <lock> (...)"` reasons (`security-scan.sh:580-593`). The `jq` presence check (exit 2) runs on the cwd-filtered PATH (`security-scan.sh:137`, `check-imports.sh:130`) |
| `PYTHON` | check-imports | `python3` | Interpreter for Python import resolution / `pyproject.toml` parsing — `check-imports.sh:37-40`, `check-imports.sh:123`. Resolved with `command -v` on the sanitized PATH; **an in-tree interpreter is refused, never run**: if the physical directory of the resolved path (`cd -P` on its `dirname`; the final symlink target is deliberately not followed, since a venv python still loads the venv's site-packages) is inside `ROOT` or the physical cwd, or cannot be resolved, `PY_REASON="PYTHON (<value>) is inside the scanned repo; not run"`; if not found, `PY_REASON="<value> not found"` (`check-imports.sh:211-223`). With `PY_REASON` set: py-imports emits `error` (`check-imports.sh:550-553`), unused-deps emits `error` for a `pyproject.toml` manifest (`check-imports.sh:631-633`), and the `dists` name-mapping step for requirements/pyproject is skipped (`check-imports.sh:676`) |
| `PYTHONPATH` | check-imports | unset | Helper runs under `python -I` (which ignores it), then re-appends each non-empty entry to `sys.path`; empty entries (= cwd) dropped — `check-imports.sh:41-43`, `check-imports.sh:508-510` |

**Shape** (both scripts — `security-scan.sh:74-79`, `check-imports.sh:77-79`; built at `security-scan.sh:307-308`, `check-imports.sh:704`):

```json
{
  "scanners": [
    {
      "name": "npm-audit",
      "status": "findings",
      "reason": "optional string",
      "notes": ["ignored repo-local .npmrc"],
      "findings": [
        { "file": "package-lock.json", "line": null, "rule": "https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-...",
          "severity": "HIGH", "message": "..." }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "skipped": [
    { "path": "link-to-etc-passwd", "reason": "resolves outside the repo root" }
  ]
}
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `scanners` | array of Scanner | yes | One element per `emit` call, in run order (slurped from `results.jsonl`, `security-scan.sh:307`, `check-imports.sh:704`). A name may repeat (e.g. `npm-audit` / `dependency-audit` emitted once per lockfile, `security-scan.sh:582-595`). `[]` when nothing is in scope: security-scan calls `finish` before any scanner (`security-scan.sh:335`); check-imports prints `{scanners: [], skipped: $k}` and exits 3 (`check-imports.sh:322-329`) |
| `skipped` | array of Skipped | yes | Always present (may be `[]`); paths (in-scope files, lockfiles, manifests) dropped without being read (`security-scan.sh:238-241`, `check-imports.sh:238-244`) |

Scanner object (`emit`, `security-scan.sh:276-284`, `check-imports.sh:345-350`):

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `name` | string | yes | security-scan: `gitleaks`, `semgrep`, `osv-scanner`, `npm-audit`, `pip-audit`, `cargo-audit`, `dependency-audit` (`security-scan.sh:340-593`). check-imports: `js-imports`, `py-imports`, `unused-deps` (`check-imports.sh:496`, `check-imports.sh:590`, `check-imports.sh:697`), each emitted only when files of that kind are in scope (`check-imports.sh:700-702`) |
| `status` | enum string | yes | `ran` (no findings) \| `findings` \| `unavailable` \| `error` (`security-scan.sh:72`). check-imports never emits `unavailable` — only `ran`/`findings` (`status_of`, `check-imports.sh:356`) and `error` (`check-imports.sh:551`, `check-imports.sh:581`, `check-imports.sh:632`, `check-imports.sh:635`, `check-imports.sh:678`); header `check-imports.sh:79`. An in-tree or missing `$PYTHON` is therefore `error`, not `unavailable` |
| `reason` | string | no | Key omitted when empty (`security-scan.sh:281`, `check-imports.sh:348`). Set for `unavailable`/`error`; also on one `ran` entry: `"no packages found in lockfiles"` (osv-scanner exit 128, `security-scan.sh:463`). Tool-missing `unavailable` reasons for gitleaks / semgrep come from `not_found <tool>` (`security-scan.sh:113-120`, used at `security-scan.sh:340`, `security-scan.sh:379`): `"<tool> not installed"` when the tool is absent from the original PATH too, else `"<tool> found only inside the scanned repo or on a relative PATH entry; not run"`. check-imports `PY_REASON`-based `error` reasons: py-imports `"<PY_REASON>; cannot resolve Python imports"` (`check-imports.sh:551`), unused-deps `"<PY_REASON>; cannot parse <manifest>"` (`check-imports.sh:632`), where `PY_REASON` is `"PYTHON (<value>) is inside the scanned repo; not run"` or `"<value> not found"` (`check-imports.sh:219`, `check-imports.sh:221`); helper failures `"<PYTHON> helper failed: …"` (`check-imports.sh:581`, `check-imports.sh:678`); `"cannot parse <manifest>: …"` (`check-imports.sh:635`). **Scrubbed** in both scripts: `emit` passes `reason` through jq `scrub` (`reduce $subs[] as $s (.; split($s[0]) \| join($s[1]))`), literal substitutions from `SCRUB_SUBS` applied in this order: temp dir (logical `$TMP`, then physical) → `<tmp>`; repo root + `/` → `""`; bare repo root → `.`; physical cwd + `/` → `""`; logical `$PWD` + `/` → `""`; `$HOME` → `~`. Values of length ≤ 1 (empty or `/`) are skipped (`def ok: length > 1`) — `security-scan.sh:265-274`, `security-scan.sh:279-281`, `check-imports.sh:334-343`, `check-imports.sh:347-348`. Only `reason` is scrubbed; `notes`, finding `file` and `message` are not. Offline-mode `unavailable` reasons: semgrep `"offline mode: SEMGREP_CONFIG is not a local file or directory (<value>)"` (`security-scan.sh:382`); dependency-audit `"offline mode: dependency audits query online vulnerability databases"` (`security-scan.sh:578`). security-scan copy/containment `error` reasons: npm-audit `"<dir/>package.json resolves outside the repo root; not copied"` (`security-scan.sh:490`), `"cannot copy <dir/>package.json: …"` (`security-scan.sh:493`), `"cannot copy <lock>: …"` (`security-scan.sh:497`); pip-audit `"cannot read <lock>: …"` (`security-scan.sh:526`); cargo-audit `"cannot copy <lock>: …"` (`security-scan.sh:557`) |
| `notes` | array of string | no | **security-scan only.** Key omitted when empty (`security-scan.sh:282`); de-duplicated and sorted via `jq 'unique'` (`notes_json`, `security-scan.sh:286-288`). Lists repo-local config deliberately ignored (`security-scan.sh:79`): `"ignored repo-local <path>"` for `.gitleaks.toml` / `.gitleaksignore` at repo root or cwd (gitleaks, `security-scan.sh:344-349`), `osv-scanner.toml` beside a lockfile (`security-scan.sh:454-455`), `.npmrc` beside a lockfile (`security-scan.sh:484`); `"stripped <N> option line(s) (e.g. --index-url, -r) from <lockfile>"` (pip-audit, `security-scan.sh:529-530`). Carried on gitleaks `error`/result (`security-scan.sh:362`, `security-scan.sh:365`, `security-scan.sh:373`), osv-scanner all outcomes (`security-scan.sh:463`, `security-scan.sh:465`, `security-scan.sh:474`), npm-audit all outcomes (`security-scan.sh:490`, `security-scan.sh:493`, `security-scan.sh:497`, `security-scan.sh:505`, `security-scan.sh:515`), pip-audit (`security-scan.sh:538`, `security-scan.sh:545`; not on the `cannot read` error, `security-scan.sh:526`); never on `unavailable` (including both offline-mode entries and the `not_found` entries), semgrep, or cargo-audit. check-imports' `emit` has no `notes` (`check-imports.sh:345-350`) |
| `findings` | array of Finding | yes | Always present; `[]` for `ran`/`unavailable`/`error` |

Finding object (security-scan per-scanner `jqr` mappings `security-scan.sh:370-568`; check-imports `finding`, `check-imports.sh:352-355`):

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | In-scope paths are symlink-resolved and rewritten `./<relative>` when under the physical cwd (`security-scan.sh:256`, `check-imports.sh:297`); a file inside the repo root but outside cwd keeps its physical absolute path (`check-imports.sh:297`). security-scan `REL_FILTER` then strips a leading `$PWD/`, physical-cwd `/`, or `./` (`security-scan.sh:295`); check-imports strips a leading `./` (`${2#./}`, `check-imports.sh:353`) |
| `line` | integer \| null | yes | `null` for all dependency-audit findings (`security-scan.sh:78`, `security-scan.sh:471`, `security-scan.sh:511`, `security-scan.sh:543`, `security-scan.sh:566`). check-imports: `($line \| tonumber? // null)` (`check-imports.sh:354`) — `null` for an `unused-dependency` whose name was not found by grep in the manifest (`check-imports.sh:693`) |
| `rule` | string | yes | security-scan: gitleaks `.RuleID` (`security-scan.sh:371`); semgrep: when `SEMGREP_CONFIG` names an existing local path (`[[ -e "$SEMGREP_CONFIG" ]]`, file or directory, online or offline), the rule is the trailing `aos.<seg>.<seg>` of `.check_id`, captured by `capture("(?:^\|\\.)(?<r>aos\\.[a-z0-9-]+\\.[a-z0-9-]+)$").r`, so the shipped ruleset yields ids like `aos.python.subprocess-shell-true` regardless of install location; with no match the full `.check_id` is kept (`// $id`); a registry config keeps the raw `.check_id` (`security-scan.sh:394-406`); osv/pip `.id` (`security-scan.sh:471`, `security-scan.sh:543`), npm advisory `url` (or `source`, or literal `npm-audit`) (`security-scan.sh:512`), cargo `.advisory.id` (or `cargo-audit`) (`security-scan.sh:566`). check-imports: `js-import-outside-root` (`check-imports.sh:481`), `js-unresolved-relative` (`check-imports.sh:483`), `js-undeclared-package` (`check-imports.sh:493`), `py-import-outside-root` (`check-imports.sh:564`), `py-unresolved-relative` (`check-imports.sh:569`), `py-unresolved-module` (`check-imports.sh:586`), `unused-dependency` (`check-imports.sh:694`); header `check-imports.sh:80-82`. `*-import-outside-root` fires when a relative or absolute import's lexically normalized target is outside the repo root; nothing outside is stat'ed (`check-imports.sh:83-87`, `check-imports.sh:478-481`, `check-imports.sh:562-565`) |
| `severity` | string | yes | Not a closed enum. security-scan: gitleaks always `HIGH` (`security-scan.sh:372`); semgrep `.extra.severity` else `UNKNOWN` (`security-scan.sh:407`); osv `.database_specific.severity` else `UNKNOWN` (`security-scan.sh:472`); npm upper-cased severity else `UNKNOWN` (`security-scan.sh:513`); pip/cargo `UNKNOWN` (`security-scan.sh:543`, `security-scan.sh:567`). check-imports: `ERROR` for import rules (`check-imports.sh:481`, `check-imports.sh:483`, `check-imports.sh:493`, `check-imports.sh:564`, `check-imports.sh:569`, `check-imports.sh:586`), `WARNING` for `unused-dependency` (`check-imports.sh:694`) |
| `message` | string | yes | Human-readable description. gitleaks runs with `--redact`, so secrets are not included (`security-scan.sh:24`, `security-scan.sh:358`) |

Skipped object (`skip`, `security-scan.sh:239-241`, `check-imports.sh:239-241`):

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | yes | The path as collected, leading `./` stripped (`${1#./}`) |
| `reason` | string | yes | Free text, currently one of: `"broken symlink or unresolvable path"` (`security-scan.sh:249`, `check-imports.sh:290`), `"resolves outside the repo root"` (`security-scan.sh:253`, `check-imports.sh:294`), `"lockfile resolves outside the repo root"` (security-scan cwd lockfile sweep, `security-scan.sh:433`), `"manifest resolves outside the repo root"` (security-scan: `package.json` beside an npm lockfile, `security-scan.sh:487-490`; check-imports: an ancestor `package.json` during JS package lookup, added once per path via `skip_once`, `check-imports.sh:242-244`, `check-imports.sh:431-434`). Repo root = git toplevel, else cwd (`security-scan.sh:174-178`, `check-imports.sh:204-208`) |

**Exit codes** (precedence in code: findings, then error, then nothing-ran — `security-scan.sh:328-330`, `check-imports.sh:714-715`; check-imports' exit 3 is only the early exit at `check-imports.sh:329`):

| Code | security-scan.sh (`security-scan.sh:81-87`) | check-imports.sh (`check-imports.sh:91-98`) |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | no findings, at least one scanner `ran`/`findings` (`security-scan.sh:331`) | checked, clean (`check-imports.sh:716`) |
| 1 | at least one finding (`security-scan.sh:328`) | findings (`check-imports.sh:714`) |
| 2 | usage error: bad flag (`security-scan.sh:160`), no `jq` on the sanitized PATH (`security-scan.sh:137`), `--base` with `--paths` (`security-scan.sh:164-165`), not a git work tree without `--paths` (`security-scan.sh:184`), invalid / unresolvable `--base` (`security-scan.sh:186-188`), missing `--paths` entry (`security-scan.sh:203-204`) | usage error: bad flag (`check-imports.sh:153`), no `jq` on the sanitized PATH (`check-imports.sh:130`), `--base` with `--paths` (`check-imports.sh:157-158`), `--base` outside git (`check-imports.sh:169`), invalid / unresolvable `--base` (`check-imports.sh:170-172`), not a git work tree without `--paths` (`check-imports.sh:179`), missing `--paths` entry (`check-imports.sh:190-191`) |
| 3 | nothing checked: no file in scope (`security-scan.sh:335`), or no scanner ran (all `unavailable` — offline mode, or tools found only in-tree, make this more likely) (`security-scan.sh:330`) | nothing to check — no JS/TS/Python sources or manifests in scope (`check-imports.sh:322-329`) |
| 4 | a scanner errored and none produced findings (`security-scan.sh:329`) | a check could not run and nothing was found: `$PYTHON` missing or in-tree (`check-imports.sh:551`, `check-imports.sh:632`), Python helper failed (`check-imports.sh:581`, `check-imports.sh:678`), manifest unparseable (`check-imports.sh:635`) (`check-imports.sh:715`) |

### ReviewVerdict

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `message` |
| **Ingestion route** | Emitted by gate reviewer agents as the **only** fenced `json` block of their final output, placed at the very end (`review-verdict.md:33-37`); consumed by the parser `.claude/hooks/lib/parse-verdict.sh [--expect-head <sha>] [FILE]` from the file argument or stdin (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:10-12`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:35-54`). `scripts/parse-verdict.sh` is now a one-line wrapper that `exec`s it (`scripts/parse-verdict.sh:4`; `review-verdict.md:99-100`). In-session consumer: the SubagentStop hook `review-verdict-capture.sh` (see `ReviewVerdictCaptureHookIO`). Emitters: `code-reviewer`, `security-reviewer`, `library-reviewer` (verdict `pass`\|`fix-before-merge`\|`hold`) and `data-model-verifier` (`pass`\|`hold` only, parser-enforced) — `review-verdict.md:12-17`; each agent's `## Verdict block` section. `library-investigator` and `adversarial-claims-reviewer` are exempt (`review-verdict.md:19-21`). |
| **Source** | `.claude/references/review-verdict.md` (Shape lines 31–75, rejections 88–95); `.claude/hooks/lib/parse-verdict.sh` (framing lines 56–80, duplicate-key check 84–104, validation 106–150, post-demotion checks 159–166); wrapper `scripts/parse-verdict.sh`; emitters `.claude/agents/code-reviewer.md:106-112`, `.claude/agents/security-reviewer.md:98-104`, `.claude/agents/library-reviewer.md:70-76`, `.claude/agents/data-model-verifier.md:75-81`; fixtures `scripts/fixtures/review-verdict/*.txt`, tests `scripts/parse-verdict-test.sh` |
| **Notes** | The parser now ships to consumers with the hooks (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:6-8`; `review-verdict.md:7-8`, `review-verdict.md:99-100`; `install.sh:1705` `install_dir "hooks"`). `.claude/references/review-verdict.md` and `scripts/` remain repo-side and do NOT ship (`review-verdict.md:7-10`), so each emitting agent restates the enum, required fields, and demotion rule inline in its `## Verdict block`, and states that the parser ships with the hooks at `.claude/hooks/lib/parse-verdict.sh` and that a SubagentStop hook runs it on the final message to record the verdict for the current HEAD (`code-reviewer.md:108`, `security-reviewer.md:100`, `library-reviewer.md:72`, `data-model-verifier.md:77`). |

**Framing** (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:56-80`; `review-verdict.md:33-37`): an opening fence line matching `^[[:space:]]*```json[[:space:]]*$` and a closing line matching `^[[:space:]]*```[[:space:]]*$` (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:60-61`). The output must contain **exactly one** such block and only whitespace may follow its closing fence. Rejected with exit `1`: zero blocks (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:65`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:75`), an unterminated block (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:66`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:76`), more than one block — including a `json` block quoted inside a longer fence (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:67`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:77`; fixtures `multiple-blocks.txt`, `nested-quote-override.txt`), or any non-whitespace after the closing fence (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:63`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:68`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:78`; fixture `trailing-text.txt`). Text *before* the block is allowed. The block body must parse as JSON (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:82`) and contain exactly one value (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:129`) that is an object (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:131`).

**Duplicate keys** (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:84-104`; `review-verdict.md:93-95`): checked with `jq --stream` over raw tokens (before jq's last-key-wins collapse) — a value path that is "closed" and then seen again means its key was repeated, at any depth. Rejection: stderr `parse-verdict: duplicate JSON key: <dot.path>` (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:98`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:103`), exit `1` (fixtures `duplicate-key.txt`, `duplicate-key-nested.txt`); a scan failure is also exit `1` (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:102`). Runs only when the block holds exactly one JSON value (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:89`); the multi-value case is rejected by validation. The former `python3` check is gone — no python dependency, because the parser now lives under `.claude/hooks/`, where Invariant 8 bans inline interpreters (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:84-85`).

**Shape** (`review-verdict.md:39-57`):

```json
{
  "schema": "agentic-os/review-verdict@1",
  "agent": "code-reviewer",
  "base": "origin/main",
  "head": "<40-hex output of git rev-parse HEAD>",
  "verdict": "pass | fix-before-merge | hold",
  "findings": [
    {
      "tier": 1,
      "severity": "high",
      "file": "src/auth.ts",
      "line": 42,
      "summary": "Session token compared with == (timing leak)",
      "evidence": "bash scripts/repro-timing.sh exits 1"
    }
  ]
}
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `schema` | string literal | yes | Exactly `agentic-os/review-verdict@1` — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:133-134` |
| `agent` | enum string | yes | One of `code-reviewer` \| `security-reviewer` \| `library-reviewer` \| `data-model-verifier`; anything else rejected (fixture `unknown-agent.txt`) — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:109`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:135-136`; `review-verdict.md:62` |
| `base` | string (non-blank) | yes | Base ref diffed against, e.g. `origin/main` — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:137` |
| `head` | string | yes | Full commit SHA, regex `^([0-9a-f]{40}\|[0-9a-f]{64})$` (lowercase hex) — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:138-139`. With `--expect-head <sha>`, must equal `<sha>` exactly or exit `1` — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:39-41`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:140-141`; `review-verdict.md:108` |
| `verdict` | enum string | yes | `pass` \| `fix-before-merge` \| `hold` — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:142-143`. `data-model-verifier` may emit only `pass` \| `hold`; `fix-before-merge` from it is rejected (fixture `verifier-fix-before-merge.txt`) — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:144-145`; `review-verdict.md:65` |
| `findings` | array of Finding | yes | May be empty; non-array rejected — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:146-148` |

Finding (each element must be an object — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:112`):

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tier` | number | yes | `0` \| `1` \| `2` — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:114-115` |
| `severity` | enum string | yes | `critical` \| `high` \| `medium` \| `low` — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:116-117`. Prose blocking / should-fix / nit map to `high`-or-`critical` / `medium` / `low` — `review-verdict.md:74-75` |
| `file` | string (non-blank) | yes | Repo-relative path by convention (`review-verdict.md:69`); parser checks only non-blank string — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:118-119` |
| `line` | integer ≥ 1 \| null | no | Omitted or `null` = file-level finding — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:120-121` |
| `summary` | string (non-blank) | yes | One sentence — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:122-123` |
| `evidence` | string \| null | no (schema) | Must be a string when present — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:124-125`. Contract-required for tier 0/1 (`review-verdict.md:72`); absence is not a validation error but triggers demotion (see `ReviewVerdictNormalized`) |

**Cross-field rejections** (evaluated after demotion, only when the schema checks above pass — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:159-173`; `review-verdict.md:88-92`), each exit `1`:

- `verdict: pass` with one or more tier 0/1 findings that still carry evidence after demotion (fixture `pass-contradiction.txt`) — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:162-163`.
- `agent: data-model-verifier` with `verdict: hold` and zero evidenced tier 0/1 findings — rejected, NOT downgraded (fixture `verifier-unevidenced-hold.txt`) — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:164-165`.

Unknown extra keys at either level are not rejected; they are dropped from the normalized output (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:153`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:171`).

---

### ReviewVerdictArtifact

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `persistence` |
| **Ingestion route** | One JSON file per (HEAD, reviewer) at `<git-root>/.claude/reviews/<HEAD-sha>-<agent>.json`. **Writer:** only the SubagentStop hook `review-verdict-capture.sh` (reviewers are read-only and never write it — `review-verdict-capture.sh:3-4`; `review-verdict.md:121-127`). **Reader:** only the Stop hook `review-gate-stop.sh` (`review-gate-stop.sh:193-205`; `review-verdict.md:127-129`). `<git-root>` is resolved from the SubagentStop payload `cwd` first (so a review in a git worktree binds to that worktree's HEAD), then `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, then `$PWD` (`review-verdict-capture.sh:22-23`, `review-verdict-capture.sh:56-60`). |
| **Source** | `.claude/hooks/review-verdict-capture.sh` (lines 56–62, 87–117); `.claude/hooks/review-gate-stop.sh` (lines 107, 182–194); `.claude/references/review-verdict.md` (lines 119–129); `.gitignore:29-31` |

**Content:** exactly the `ReviewVerdictNormalized` JSON (see that section — fields are not duplicated here): the parser's stdout written verbatim plus a trailing newline (`write_atomic` writes `printf '%s\n' "$2"` with `$2` = `$out`, `review-verdict-capture.sh:111`, `review-verdict-capture.sh:117`).

**Write conditions** (all must hold, else nothing is written — `review-verdict-capture.sh`):

| Rule | Detail |
|---|---|
| Agent allowlist | `agent_type` exactly one of `code-reviewer` \| `security-reviewer` \| `library-reviewer` \| `data-model-verifier`; anything else exits silently — `review-verdict-capture.sh:51-54` |
| HEAD-bound parse | Parser run as `lib/parse-verdict.sh --expect-head "$head"`, where `head` = `git rev-parse --verify -q HEAD` of the git root — `review-verdict-capture.sh:60-61`, `review-verdict-capture.sh:87` |
| Exit filter | Only parse exits `0` / `2` / `3` (pass / hold / fix-before-merge); exit `1` (invalid, stale head) writes nothing — `review-verdict-capture.sh:88-92` |
| Agent match | Normalized `.agent` must equal the subagent's `agent_type` — `review-verdict-capture.sh:94-95` |
| Symlink refusal | Refuses when any of `<root>/.claude`, `<root>/.claude/reviews`, `<root>/.claude/reviews/.gitignore` is a symlink (dangling included), checked against the physical root (`pwd -P`) — `review-verdict-capture.sh:19-20`, `review-verdict-capture.sh:97-103` |
| Containment | After `mkdir -p`, the physical path of `.claude/reviews` must equal `<physical root>/.claude/reviews`; otherwise refuses ("resolves outside the repo") — `review-verdict-capture.sh:104-106` |

**Filename:** `<head>-<agent>.json`; `head` must match `^([0-9a-f]{40}|[0-9a-f]{64})$` (`review-verdict-capture.sh:62`) and `agent` is the allowlisted `agent_type`, so the name cannot leave `.claude/reviews/` (`review-verdict-capture.sh:17-18`).

**Atomic write / overwrite:** both the verdict file and `.gitignore` go through `write_atomic`: temp file `mktemp "$reviews/.tmp.XXXXXX"` in the same dir, write, then `mv -f` onto the final name; on failure the temp file is removed (`review-verdict-capture.sh:108-114`). Per the hook comment, `mv` replaces a symlink at the destination instead of following it (`review-verdict-capture.sh:97-99`). A re-review of the same HEAD by the same agent overwrites the prior file (`review-verdict-capture.sh:117`); a failed verdict write → fail-open note (`review-verdict-capture.sh:117`).

**Self-gitignore:** whenever `.claude/reviews/.gitignore` does not exist at capture time, it is written atomically with the single line `*`; a failure there is ignored (`review-verdict-capture.sh:25-26`, `review-verdict-capture.sh:116`). The repo `.gitignore` also lists `.claude/reviews/` (`.gitignore:31`), and `review-gate-stop.sh` excludes `^\.claude/reviews/` from its changed-path set (`review-gate-stop.sh:118`, `review-gate-stop.sh:127-128`).

**Read rules** (`review-gate-stop.sh:193-205`): for each required reviewer `a`, reads `$root/.claude/reviews/$head-$a.json` and accepts it only when `.agent == a` AND `.head == <current HEAD>` and `.verdict` is in `pass` \| `fix-before-merge` \| `hold` (`review-gate-stop.sh:195-198`). `pass` → satisfied; `fix-before-merge` → satisfied (listed as advisory info); `hold` → block; missing / malformed / mismatched → treated as missing → block (`review-gate-stop.sh:199-204`).

---

### ReviewVerdictCaptureHookIO

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `message` |
| **Ingestion route** | The Claude Code **SubagentStop** hook, registered with matcher `code-reviewer\|security-reviewer\|library-reviewer\|data-model-verifier` in `.claude/settings.json:45-47` (consumer copy: `assets/consumer/claude-settings.json:31-33`, path `$HOME/.claude/hooks/review-verdict-capture.sh`). INPUT is the SubagentStop event JSON on **stdin**, truncated to the first 4 MiB (`head -c $((MAX_BYTES * 4))`, `MAX_BYTES=1048576` — `review-verdict-capture.sh:37`, `review-verdict-capture.sh:48`). Side effect: writes `ReviewVerdictArtifact`. **Fails open**: every error prints one `review-verdict-capture: <note>` line to stderr and exits 0 (`review-verdict-capture.sh:32-33`, `review-verdict-capture.sh:39`). |
| **Source** | `.claude/hooks/review-verdict-capture.sh` (lines 35–117) |

**INPUT** (SubagentStop event, stdin) — only these fields are consumed:

```json
{ "agent_type": "code-reviewer", "last_assistant_message": "<final text>", "agent_transcript_path": "~/.claude/projects/.../x.jsonl", "cwd": "/repo" }
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `agent_type` | string | yes (effectively) | Read as `.agent_type // empty \| strings`; not in the 4-name allowlist → exit 0, no output — `review-verdict-capture.sh:49`, `review-verdict-capture.sh:51-54`. Unparseable payload → fail-open note — `review-verdict-capture.sh:49` |
| `last_assistant_message` | string | no | Primary source of the reviewer's final message — `review-verdict-capture.sh:64`. Empty → not captured (`review-verdict-capture.sh:84`); over `MAX_BYTES` (1 MiB) **measured in bytes** (`printf '%s' "$msg" \| wc -c`) → not captured (`review-verdict-capture.sh:85`) |
| `agent_transcript_path` | string | no | Fallback only when `last_assistant_message` is empty — `review-verdict-capture.sh:66-68`. Leading `~` expanded (`review-verdict-capture.sh:69`); must be a regular non-symlink `*.jsonl` file (`review-verdict-capture.sh:70`) whose dir resolves under `${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects` (`review-verdict-capture.sh:71-73`). Only the last `MAX_BYTES` read; the last `type == "assistant"` entry's `.message.content` (string, or newline-joined `text` parts of an array, non-empty) is used — `review-verdict-capture.sh:74-81` |
| `cwd` | string | no | **Preferred** directory for `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`, so a reviewer in a git worktree binds to that worktree's HEAD; when empty/absent, falls back to `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, then `$PWD`. Not a git repo → fail-open note — `review-verdict-capture.sh:22-23`, `review-verdict-capture.sh:56-60` |

**Environment:**

| Var | Use |
|---|---|
| `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` | Fallback directory for the git root when payload `cwd` is empty/absent — `review-verdict-capture.sh:59` |
| `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` | Root of the transcript containment check (`<dir>/projects`), default `$HOME/.claude` — `review-verdict-capture.sh:71` |

Requires `jq` and `git` on PATH and `lib/parse-verdict.sh` next to the hook; absence of any → fail-open note (`review-verdict-capture.sh:41-46`).

**OUTPUT:** nothing on stdout in any path; always exit `0` (`review-verdict-capture.sh:39`, `review-verdict-capture.sh:53`, normal end after `review-verdict-capture.sh:117`). Diagnostics go to stderr only.

---

### ReviewVerdictNormalized

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Kind** | `message` |
| **Ingestion route** | Output of `bash .claude/hooks/lib/parse-verdict.sh [--expect-head <sha>] [FILE]` (or the `scripts/parse-verdict.sh` wrapper, `scripts/parse-verdict.sh:4`); stdin when FILE omitted (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:10-12`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:35-54`). On exit `0`, `2` or `3` a single line of compact JSON is printed to **stdout** (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:188-195`); on exit `1` nothing is printed to stdout and one `parse-verdict: <reason>` line per error goes to **stderr** (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:30`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:183-185`). Consumer: `review-verdict-capture.sh` (SubagentStop), which persists the stdout JSON as `ReviewVerdictArtifact` (`review-verdict-capture.sh:87-117`); also CI / manual use. |
| **Source** | `.claude/hooks/lib/parse-verdict.sh` (exit codes lines 21–26, normalization 152–180, emit 188–195); `.claude/references/review-verdict.md` (Normalization + rejections lines 77–95, exit codes 108–117) |
| **Notes** | The parser ships with the hooks (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:6-8`; `review-verdict.md:99-100`); the reference doc is repo-side only (`review-verdict.md:7-10`). |

**Exit codes** (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:21-26`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:191-195`; `review-verdict.md:110-117`):

| Exit | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `0` | Valid; normalized `verdict` is `pass` |
| `1` | Invalid: block count/position (zero, >1, unterminated, trailing text), invalid JSON, not exactly one value, duplicate keys, schema/enum/required-field/agent violation, a cross-field rejection (pass contradiction, `data-model-verifier` `fix-before-merge` or unevidenced `hold`), or `--expect-head` mismatch; also usage error, unreadable file, missing `jq`, or duplicate-key scan failure (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:30-33`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:40-44`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:50`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:73-80`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:82`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:102-103`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:181`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:183-185`) |
| `2` | Valid; normalized `verdict` is `hold` (at least one tier 0/1 finding survived demotion) |
| `3` | Valid; normalized `verdict` is `fix-before-merge`, including a downgraded unevidenced `hold` |

**Shape** (stdout, compact JSON; example per `scripts/fixtures/review-verdict/hold-unevidenced.txt` input, which exits `3`):

```json
{"schema":"agentic-os/review-verdict@1","agent":"code-reviewer","base":"origin/main","head":"0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef01234567","verdict":"fix-before-merge","findings":[{"demoted_from":1,"tier":2,"severity":"high","file":"src/a.ts","line":3,"summary":"Looks racy","evidence":"  "}],"verdict_demoted_from":"hold"}
```

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `schema` | string literal | yes | Passed through — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:171` |
| `agent` | enum string | yes | Passed through (already validated against the four-agent enum) — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:171` |
| `base` | string | yes | Passed through — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:171` |
| `head` | string | yes | Passed through; equals `--expect-head` when that flag was given — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:171` |
| `verdict` | enum string | yes | `pass` \| `fix-before-merge` \| `hold`. A `hold` with zero findings at tier ≤ 1 after demotion is rewritten to `fix-before-merge` — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:175-177`. Never fires for `data-model-verifier` (that case is rejected first — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:164-165`) |
| `findings` | array of NormalizedFinding | yes | Each input finding mapped through `normalize_finding` — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:171` |
| `verdict_demoted_from` | string literal `"hold"` | no | Present only when the `hold` → `fix-before-merge` downgrade fired — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:176` |

NormalizedFinding (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:152-156`):

| Name | Type | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `demoted_from` | number (`0` \| `1`) | no | Present only when the input tier was 0/1 and `evidence` was missing, `null`, or whitespace-only; holds the original tier — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:154-155` |
| `tier` | number | yes | `0` \| `1` \| `2`; set to `2` on demotion — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:155` |
| `severity` | enum string | yes | Passed through — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:153` |
| `file` | string | yes | Passed through — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:153` |
| `line` | integer ≥ 1 \| null | yes | Always present; omitted input → `null` — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:153` |
| `summary` | string | yes | Passed through — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:153` |
| `evidence` | string \| null | yes | Always present; omitted input → `null`; a blank string is kept as-is (even when demoted) — `lib/parse-verdict.sh:153` |

Key order: `demoted_from` is emitted first in a demoted finding; `verdict_demoted_from` last at top level (`lib/parse-verdict.sh:155`, `lib/parse-verdict.sh:176`). Consumers should not rely on key order.
